  - **Shared kit**: `icons.tsx` (`TrashIcon`, `ArchiveIcon`, `RestoreIcon` — outline glyphs on a 24px grid, 2px round strokes, `currentColor` so they follow text/dark mode, `aria-hidden` because a text label always accompanies them); `ItemActionFooter` (Archive/Restore + Delete, same two-button layout in every pane — Restore replaces Archive when the item is archived); `ItemActionDialog` (`mode` archive|delete, `noun` task|event|reminder, optional `archiveNote`/`alsoRemoves`/`deleteNote` for item-specific wording such as sub-tasks or "all of its occurrences", `canArchive`); `ArchivedBanner` (archive date + reason, info only — Restore lives in the footer); `useItemActions({ itemKey, archived, canArchive?, onClose, onRestore })` which owns the dialog state, the hotkeys (`Ctrl+Shift+A`, `Delete` when not typing, `Ctrl+Shift+D`) and Escape (closes the dialog before the pane). To bring another pane onto the pattern: call the hook above the pane's `if (!item) return null`, render the footer + dialog, and give the entity `archivedAt`/`archiveReason`.
  - **Data**: `CalendarEvent`/`CalendarReminder` gained `archivedAt: string | null` + `archiveReason: string | null`; calendarStore **v9** (backfills `null`); new actions `archiveEvent/restoreEvent/archiveReminder/restoreReminder`; sync mappers carry both columns; Supabase `029_calendar_archive.sql` (applied — see "Live migration status").
  - **Hidden when archived** (kept, not deleted): `CalendarView` (month/week/day items, untimed rows, multi-day span pills, reminders) and `useNotificationChecker` (no new notifications, and a pending one is dropped). Archiving applies to the **whole repeating series**, not one occurrence — the dialog says so; the `RecurrenceScopeBar`'s per-occurrence "Delete only this one / this and following" actions are unchanged and still act immediately.
  - **Finding archived items again**: `CalendarSidePane` has a collapsible **Archived (N)** section (events + reminders, newest first); clicking one opens its pane with the reason and a Restore button. Task-linked shadow events/reminders are excluded — they follow the task — and a task's scheduled shadow event has no Archive button in its pane (delete stays).
  - **Behaviour change**: deleting an event or reminder now asks for confirmation (it used to delete on one click).
  - **Other**: `AddTaskModal`-style modal tiers unchanged; the dialog is 500px wide so its three buttons fit one row. Copy moved from `LABELS.taskArchive` to `LABELS.itemActions`. Hotkey group renamed "Tasks" → "Item panes".
  - **Verified** headlessly (22 checks on events/reminders + the 35 task checks re-run against the refactor): icons on both footer buttons, confirmation copy/focus/Escape, Delete key, archive with reason via Ctrl+Enter, disappearing from the grid, the Archived list → reason → restore via `Ctrl+Shift+A`, repeating-event wording, permanent delete, reminder parity. **Not verified**: Android, and the Supabase round trip.
  - **Not done / next**: Notes, List items, Portfolio and Records entries don't have panes on this pattern yet (Notes already has an unused `archivedAt` field — see BACKLOG.md); **No Ctrl+Z / undo for deletions, by decision (2026-09-20).** Archiving *is* the reversible path; delete is deliberately hard to reach (confirmation, "cannot be undone", Cancel focused by default), so a delete is assumed intentional. Don't add an undo stack or toast for deletes, and don't list one as backlog.
- [x] **Task links from notes, sub-task priority inheritance, and a modal z-index fix** (2026-09-20).
  - **Links typed into a task's notes are copied into its links list**, never overwriting existing ones. `extractUrls(text)` and `mergeNewLinks(existing, notes, previousNotes?)` live in `src/utils/links.ts` (`textToTask.ts` now uses the shared `extractUrls` instead of its own copy). Applied centrally — `taskService.createTask` (all URLs in the new task's notes) and `taskStore.updateTask` (whenever `notes` is in the changes) — so the Task pane, Add Task modal, quick-add and Notes "Create ▸ Task" all behave the same. Comparison is protocol/`www.`/case/trailing-slash insensitive (`https://EXISTING.example.com/` matches `existing.example.com`). **Only links *new* to the notes are added** (in the new notes, not the previous ones): a link the user deliberately deletes from the links list is not resurrected by a later unrelated notes edit. Trade-off: it only triggers when the notes are saved, not as you type; and removing a link from the *notes* never removes it from the links list.
  - **Sub-tasks inherit the parent's priority**: the Task pane's quick "Add sub-task…" passes `priority: task.priority`; `AddTaskModal` starts a sub-task on its parent's priority and follows the "Parent task" dropdown until the user picks a priority themselves (`priorityTouched` ref), after which their choice always wins. Priority is the only field inherited besides `collectionId` — extend the same `addTask({...})` list in `TaskPane.handleAddSubtask` for more.
    - **Endeavour inheritance only actually works from `TaskPane`'s quick "Add sub-task…"**, not from `AddTaskModal` — corrected 2026-09-24, this line previously overclaimed. `addTaskWithCalendar` (`services/taskCalendarLinks.ts`) treats `collectionId: undefined` as "inherit from parent," which the quick-add path relies on by omitting the field entirely. `AddTaskModal` seeds its `collectionId` state from `activeCollectionId`/prefill, never from the parent task even when opened as a subtask, and always sends an explicit value (never `undefined`) on submit — so a subtask created via the full modal does not inherit the parent's Endeavour. Tags/purposes are not inherited from either path. See BACKLOG.md "Sub-task inheritance — Endeavour gap in AddTaskModal, tags/purposes not inherited anywhere" for the fix.
  - **`AddTaskModal` overlay z-index 30 → 102.** It's opened from inside `TaskPane` (the ⊞ "Open full task form" button, still open behind it) and at 30 it rendered *under* the pane's 100 overlay — visible but unclickable. Found while testing the above; same tier as `AddScheduleModal`/`TaskActionDialog`.
- [x] **Suite-wide Quick Access pane (`Ctrl+G`) — v1: search + recent/frequent history, extensible provider registry.** Addresses BACKLOG.md's "Suite-wide Quick Access pane" entry (see there for what's explicitly deferred to a later pass — pinning, granular in-entity targets, Portfolio/Fitness coverage, Android entry point, fuzzy matching). Opens a centred search-and-jump overlay from anywhere in the suite: typing filters by title across every registered destination type; an empty query instead browses a Recent/Frequent history of what's actually been opened app-wide, capped to a user-adjustable count.
  - **Provider registry, the extensibility mechanism** (`src/utils/quickAccess.ts`): a `QuickAccessProvider` per destination type — `list()` (all items, for search), `resolve(entityId)` (re-derive a live item from a bare id, for rendering a history entry whose title may have changed or whose entity may be gone), `navigate(entityId)` (how to actually jump there). Seven providers ship in v1: `note`, `notebook` (`NoteTag` where `kind='area'`), `task`, `list`, `endeavour` (`Collection` where `kind` is `'project'|'list'` — trackers/routines are separate types below since they navigate to Records, not a Tasks filter), `tracker`, `routine` (the latter two both `Collection`, filtered by `kind`). Adding a new destination type later (a Calendar event, a Portfolio ticker, a Fitness activity, a specific List item or Tracker entry) is one new provider + one new `QuickAccessTargetType` union member (`src/store/recentItemsStore.ts`) — this was a deliberate v1 design goal, not deferred complexity. `navigate()` for each type reuses the exact same uiStore actions any other in-app navigation already uses (`openTaskPane`, `openNote`, `setSelectedNoteTag`, `setActiveTracker`, `setActiveRoutine`, `setActiveCollection`) — Quick Access has no navigation logic of its own, it's a second caller of paths that already existed.
  - **Recent/frequent visit tracking** (`src/store/recentItemsStore.ts`, persisted to `todo-recent-items`, added to `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS`): `RecentItemEntry { type, entityId, lastVisitedAt, visitCount }`, keyed `${type}:${entityId}`. Capped at 500 entries (trimmed to the 400 most-recently-visited on overflow) since nothing else bounds it and it persists indefinitely. **Visits are recorded at each destination's own natural "open" point, not inside Quick Access itself** — `noteStore.touchNote` (already existed for `Note.lastViewedAt`; now also calls `recordVisit`), `uiStore.openTaskPane`/`setSelectedNoteTag`/`setActiveTracker`/`setActiveRoutine`/`setActiveCollection` (each guarded to skip recording on a `null`/clearing call), and `ListsSection.tsx`'s `handleSelectList` (Lists has no uiStore-level "selected list" — see below). This was a deliberate choice over recording only inside `navigateToQuickAccessItem`: it means "recent"/"frequent" reflects visits from *anywhere* (sidebar clicks, cross-app links, hotkeys), not just prior uses of Quick Access itself, which is what actually makes a recency/frequency feature useful rather than self-referential. Stale entries (an id whose entity was since deleted or archived) are dropped from what's displayed and pruned out of the store lazily, the first time `QuickAccessPane` tries to resolve one — `resolveRecentItems()` is a pure function (safe to call during render, returns `{ items, stale }`), and pruning itself (`pruneStaleRecentEntries()`, a real store write) happens in a `useEffect`, not during the render that discovers the staleness.
  - **Lists' selection-external-navigation gap**: every other provider's destination state already lived in `uiStore` (reachable from anywhere), but `ListsSection.tsx`'s `selectedListId` is local `useState`, seeded from `uiStore.listsLastActiveListId` only once on mount — so an external request to select a *different* list while already inside the Lists section had nothing to act on. Fixed with the same "pending request, consumed by the one component that can act on it" shape `pendingArtifactLink` already established: `uiStore.pendingListSelectionId` + `requestListSelection(id)`, consumed by a `ListsSection` effect that calls the existing `handleSelectList()` (so an external jump also records a visit and resets filters/tabs exactly like a sidebar click) and then clears the field itself.
  - **UI** (`src/components/QuickAccessPane/`): portaled to `document.body` (can be summoned from inside any other pane, so it can't risk an ancestor `transform` breaking `position: fixed` — see the Modals section's "Escaping an ancestor's transform" note), `z-index: 400` — above every existing pane/modal tier (100–102) and below `LinkHoverPreview`'s small corner tooltip (10000, inconsequential to overlap). Search input autofocuses on open; `↑`/`↓`/`Enter` navigate the result list from the input itself (`onKeyDown`, not a document listener, so it only acts while the input has focus); Escape closes (the standard per-component `keydown` `useEffect` pattern). Empty query shows a Recent/Frequent segmented toggle (mirrors the Overview/Focused toggle's styling in `App.module.css`) plus a "Show N" numeric stepper bound to `settingsStore.quickAccessRecentCount` (3–20, default 8) — hidden while actively searching, since a live count doesn't apply to ranked search results. Each row shows an icon (provider-supplied — reuses `getNotebookIcon()` for notebooks so Quick Access's icon matches what Chronicle already shows for that same notebook), title, and a subtitle naming the type (and, for Tasks, the Endeavour it belongs to).
  - **Hotkey**: `Ctrl+G`, registered as `action-quick-access` in `src/config/hotkeys.ts` (`customizable: true`, dispatched centrally in `App.tsx`'s keydown handler like every other global Action hotkey — blocked while typing in an input, same as `S`/`M`/`E`/`P`/Backspace, for consistency rather than as a special case). The Android hardware back button closes it through its `useEscapeClose` registration (since 2026-10-04; it used to be on `closeTopmostMobileOverlay()`'s flag list, deleted in W2), even though nothing yet *opens* it from touch UI (see BACKLOG.md).
  - **Verified live via Playwright round-trips** (not just build-checked): created a task via `QuickAddInput`, opened it (recording a visit), searched for it in Quick Access and confirmed one result, selected it via Enter and confirmed it opened the right `TaskPane` and closed the overlay, reopened Quick Access with an empty query and confirmed it appeared under Recent, and confirmed Escape closes the pane. Separately verified the Lists and Notebook navigation paths end-to-end (create → select → switch away → Quick Access search → click result → confirm the correct section/item is showing), specifically because those two go through `requestListSelection`/`setSelectedNoteTag` rather than the simpler `openTaskPane`/`openNote` paths already proven by cross-app linking elsewhere in this file.
- [x] **Fixed: editing an Endeavour/Purpose/Tag from inside the Manage view opened the edit modal *behind* the Manage pane.** `ManagePane` (`z-index: 100`) opens `AddCollectionModal`/`AddPurposeModal`/`AddTagModal` in edit mode via the same `openEditCollection`/`openEditPurpose`/`openEditTag` actions used everywhere else — but all three modals were still on the everyday-modal tier (`z-index: 30`), so they rendered underneath the pane instead of on top of it, same root cause already documented for `EditActivityTypeModal`/`AddScheduleModal` opening from inside an already-open 100-tier pane. An audit confirmed all three (not just Endeavours) had the bug. Fixed by bumping all three modals' `.overlay` to `z-index: 102`, the same tier `AddScheduleModal` already established for this exact "opens from inside an already-open pane" situation — copy the tier from context, not from the component's own category, per the standing note in the Modals section above.
- [x] **Back/forward app-section navigation — `Alt+Left`/`Alt+Right`, Backspace kept as a secondary alternate for back.** Extends the previous Backspace-only "go back" with a real forward stack, standard browser semantics: `uiStore.setActiveView(view, opts?: { mode?: 'push' | 'back' | 'forward' })` — `'push'` (any normal navigation: a nav hotkey, clicking a section icon) pushes the section being left onto `sectionHistory` and **clears** `sectionForwardHistory`; `'back'` pushes the left section onto `sectionForwardHistory` instead (leaving `sectionHistory` alone — it was already popped by `navigateBack()` before calling `setActiveView`); `'forward'` is the mirror image. `navigateForward()` pops `sectionForwardHistory` and calls `setActiveView(next.view, { mode: 'forward' })`, mirroring the pre-existing `navigateBack()`. `Alt+Left`/`Alt+Right` registered as `action-back` (primary, Backspace now secondary)/`action-forward` (new) in `hotkeys.ts`, dispatched in `App.tsx`'s central keydown handler like every other global Action hotkey. **Superseded 2026-10-07 by "One history, and the history browser (Alt+N)"**: `sectionHistory` and `notesHistory` are now one history of places (`navHistory`/`navForward`).
- [x] **uiStore session persistence — navigation/session memory survives closing the app; modal/pane state deliberately does not.** `uiStore` was memory-only before this; it's now wrapped in zustand's `persist` middleware (`todo-ui-session`, localStorage, **v1**, added to `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS`) with a `partialize` that persists only: `activeView`, `sectionHistory`/`sectionForwardHistory` (the back/forward stacks above), `activeCollectionIdByView`, `activePurposeIds`, `notesLastEditingNoteId`/`notesLastActiveTabId`, `selectedNoteTagId`, `listsLastActiveListId`/`listsLastActiveTabId`, `activeTrackerId`/`activeRoutineId`, `calendarViewMode`. Every modal/pane/dropdown open-state (`openModal`, `endeavourPickerOpen`, `manageOpen`, `settingsOpen`, `editTrackerOpen`, …) and every one-off "currently editing X" pointer stay memory-only by omission from `partialize` — deliberately, so a reload can never reopen a modal pointing at an item that no longer exists. Per-device only (no Supabase sync) — see BACKLOG.md "uiStore persistence" for why cross-device sync is a separate, still-undecided feature.
- [x] **Fixed: Notes tab restoration silently raced against its own "remember the last tab" mirror, and was rebuilt to match Lists' already-working pattern.** `NoteEditor.tsx`'s `activeTabId` used to start at a plain `useState(null)` and get corrected to the remembered tab *after* mount by a separate load-effect (`initialTabRestore` + `hasRestoredTabRef`) — but the effect that mirrors the current tab back into `uiStore.notesLastActiveTabId` fired on every render *including that first one*, so it could write the not-yet-restored `null` into the store before the restore effect ever ran, racing whichever ran first. Rebuilt to match `ListsSection.tsx`'s `selectedListId`/`selectedTabId` pattern exactly, which never had this problem: `activeTabId` is now seeded directly via `useState(() => ...)`'s lazy initializer, reading `uiStore.notesLastEditingNoteId`/`notesLastActiveTabId` once at construction time (validated against the note's actual `tabs`, falling back to Main/`null` if the remembered tab no longer exists) — so the *first* render already has the correct tab, no post-mount correction needed. The mirror effect was changed from firing its `setNotesLastActiveTab` call in the effect body (on every render) to firing it only in the **cleanup** function (`useEffect(() => { return () => setNotesLastActiveTab(activeTabId) }, [activeTabId, ...])`) — cleanup only runs right before the value is about to change again, or on unmount, so it can never fire with a stale/default value the way an effect-body call can. `hasRestoredTabRef` still exists, simplified to just "is this the very first run" (leave the seeded tab alone) vs. "a real note switch happened" (reset to Main, the pre-existing behavior). Note-level restoration (`notesLastEditingNoteId`, handled entirely inside `uiStore.setActiveView`) never had this specific race — only the tab-level mirror did.
  - **Does not cover, and was never meant to cover, revisiting a different note within the same Notes session** (confirmed 2026-09-24, still open — see BACKLOG.md "Notes: per-note tab memory"). There is exactly one "last active tab" slot (`notesLastActiveTabId`), not one per note, and `NoteEditor.tsx`'s `hasRestoredTabRef` deliberately forces `activeTabId` back to Main on every note switch after the component's first mount — clicking note A (its tab 2), then note B, then back to note A shows note A's Main tab, not tab 2. Only leaving Notes entirely and coming back (which remounts `NoteEditor`) restores the last-used tab.
- [x] **Structured tag entries — a generic framework for built-in tags with their own separate data, Acronym is the first one.** Selecting text and applying the **Acronym** tag (🔤, 8th built-in — `Ctrl+8`, the `#Tag`/Ctrl+Space picker, or the ✎ that appears on hover over an existing one) doesn't just mark the passage the way Important/Concept/etc. do — it also creates a separate `StructuredTagEntry` record (`src/types/notes.ts`) with its own fields, browsable later without having to remember which note it was in.
  - **Generic by construction, not Acronym-specific**: `src/config/structuredTagTypes.ts`'s `STRUCTURED_TAG_TYPES: StructuredTagTypeDef[]` is the registry — each entry pairs a `key` (matching a `BuiltinTag.typeKey`) with its own field schema and an optional non-AI `infer()` function. Adding a second type (Definition, Question, …) later is one new `StructuredTagTypeDef` + one new `BuiltinTag` — the create popover, the hover-edit affordance, the mark attribute, the store actions, and the `TagView` browsable list are all written against the registry, never against `'acronym'` by name. See BACKLOG.md "Structured tag entries" for the deferred scope (cross-note dedup, editing from `TagView` directly, tab-level context).
  - **Data model**: `StructuredTagEntry { id, typeKey, tagId, term, fields: Record<string, unknown>, noteId, collectionId, createdAt, updatedAt }` (`src/types/notes.ts`, re-exported via `index.ts`) — `term` is the highlighted text itself (e.g. "ASX"), `fields` is keyed by that type's own field schema ids (Acronym: `expansion` "Stands for", `explanation`). Stored in `noteStore.structuredTagEntries: Record<StructuredTagEntryId, StructuredTagEntry>` (bumped noteStore to **v10**, migration backfills `{}`); actions `addStructuredTagEntry`/`updateStructuredTagEntry`/`deleteStructuredTagEntry`.
  - **The mark carries the link, both directions**: `NoteTagMark` (`extensions/NoteTagMark.ts`) gained a `structuredEntryId` attribute (default `null` — every plain annotation tag keeps working unchanged), rendered as `data-structured-entry-id` on the DOM `<mark>`. This is what lets the tagged passage and its entry find each other: hovering the mark shows the ✎ edit button (reads the attribute off the hovered DOM element); deleting the entry walks the currently-loaded note's doc for any text node carrying that `structuredEntryId` and unsets the mark there in one combined transaction.
  - **Create flow — compact preview by default, Enter accepts as-is, "More options" expands in place**: `src/components/NoteEditor/StructuredTagPopover.tsx`, a portaled popover shared by both create and edit (`mode: 'create' | 'edit'`). Create mode opens compact — term + the type's first field only (Acronym: "Stands for"), pre-filled by `typeDef.infer()` where confident, Enter in either input accepts immediately. An inline "More options…" link expands the *same* popover (not a separate surface) to show every field, an Endeavour picker (`CollectionPicker`, reused directly), and — edit mode only — the entry's read-only location breadcrumb (`getNoteBreadcrumb()`, `src/utils/notes.ts`, extracted as a shared helper) and created/updated timestamps. Edit mode starts expanded (opened via the hover ✎, or clicking `# Tag` on already-tagged text); a Delete button (edit mode only) removes both the entry and its mark.
  - **Applying the tag two ways, one shared entry point**: both `NoteEditor.tsx`'s `Ctrl+1`–`Ctrl+8` handler and `FloatingToolbar.tsx`'s tag-search picker (`applyTagItem`) check `getStructuredTagType(tag.typeKey)` before applying a mark directly — if it's a structured type, they call `openStructuredTagCreate(tag, from, to)` (defined in `NoteEditor.tsx`, passed down to `FloatingToolbar` as a prop) instead, which reads the selection + surrounding paragraph text, runs the type's `infer()`, and opens the popover. Untagging (pressing the same digit again, or the toolbar's "Remove tag") deletes the associated `StructuredTagEntry` too, matching this app's "no dead links" convention elsewhere.
  - **Non-AI inference** (`src/utils/acronymInference.ts`): parenthetical patterns in either order ("Expansion (ACRONYM)" / "ACRONYM (Expansion)"), colon/dash patterns ("ACRONYM: Expansion", "ACRONYM - Expansion"), and — the case the user specifically called out as *certain*, not just a heuristic — an all-caps token at the very start or end of the highlighted phrase whose letters exactly match the initials of the remaining words (skipping stopwords like "of"/"the"/"and"). Consistent with the rest of the app's non-AI inference (`textToTask.ts`); an LLM-backed fallback for what these patterns miss is a documented stretch goal in BACKLOG.md, not built here.
  - **Browsable list — reuses Notes' existing `TagView`, doesn't duplicate it**: clicking the Acronym tag (same entry point as any other built-in tag) shows every `StructuredTagEntry` for it, grouped by notebook-path location exactly like `TagView` already grouped plain tagged-text segments — a structured-type tag group renders each entry's term + non-empty field values instead of a raw text quote, reusing the pre-existing `getNotebookPath` breadcrumb grouping. Clicking a row navigates to its source note, same as every other `TagView` row.
  - **Cleanup on note deletion**: `crossAppLinkCleanup.ts`'s `deleteNoteWithCleanup()` now also deletes every `StructuredTagEntry` whose `noteId` matches, alongside its existing Task-`crossAppRefs` cleanup — an entry's anchoring mark disappears along with the note's content, so it would otherwise become permanently unreachable, not just orphaned.
  - **Bug found and fixed 2026-09-24: opening the popover threw "Maximum update depth exceeded" and crashed the Notes section.** `StructuredTagPopover.tsx` selected `useTaskStore((s) => Object.values(s.collections))` — a Zustand selector that allocates a brand-new array on every call. Under React 18's `useSyncExternalStore` (what Zustand v5's `useStore` is built on), a selector that never returns a referentially-stable snapshot makes every render look "changed," which schedules another render indefinitely. Every other caller in the codebase (e.g. `EditTrackerPane.tsx`) instead selects the raw `s.collections` record — a stable reference that only changes when collections actually change — and calls `Object.values()` in the render body. Fixed the same way. Added a pattern test (`src/test/patterns.test.ts`, "zustand selectors return a stable reference") that greps for `useXStore((s) => Object.values/keys/entries(...))` or an inline array/object literal selector anywhere in `src/`, so this shape can't recur unnoticed.
- [x] **Fixed: `Alt+Left`/`Alt+Right` back/forward section navigation did nothing while focus was in a text field.** `App.tsx`'s central keydown handler dispatched `action-back`/`action-forward` after the `isTyping` guard, unlike the Notes-zoom and dictation hotkeys just above it (both explicitly documented as needing to fire *before* that guard). Moved the two dispatches above the guard, matching that existing precedent — Alt+Left/Right isn't a text-editing binding in any browser/OS field, so there's nothing for it to conflict with.
- [x] **Fixed: hovering a calendar event/reminder showed its notes but never its links.** `CalendarView.tsx`'s `CalDisplayItem`/`TooltipState` carried `notes` but never `links`, even though `CalendarEvent.links`/`CalendarReminder.links` have existed since migration 033. Added `links: string[]` to both, populated at the two `CalDisplayItem` build sites, and rendered as a `.tooltipLinks` list under the notes line (plain text, not clickable — the tooltip's `pointer-events: none` means nothing inside it can be interacted with anyway).
- [x] **Tentative events — a "this isn't confirmed yet, just a placeholder" state for self-created Calendar events.** Researched first, confirmed with the user before building: the iCalendar spec (RFC 5545) has a `STATUS` property (`TENTATIVE`/`CONFIRMED`/`CANCELLED`) and Outlook is the one mainstream app that surfaces "tentative" for a user's *own* time (diagonal-hatch rendering, distinct from a normal solid event) — Google/Apple Calendar only expose "tentative" as an *invitee's* RSVP response, not a concept the creator can set on their own event, so there was no existing "just copy this" UI to reuse for the specific self-created-placeholder case; Outlook's visual convention was adopted anyway since it's the closest real precedent.
  - **Data model**: `CalendarEvent.status: EventStatus` (`'confirmed' | 'tentative'`, default `'confirmed'`) — a dedicated type alias (`src/types/index.ts`), not inlined, specifically because the user asked to keep the concept easy to rename/extend later. `calendarStore` bumped to **v4**, migration backfills `status: 'confirmed'`. Supabase: `017_event_status.sql` adds `status text not null default 'confirmed'` to `calendar_events` (applied to live — see "Live migration status"); `mappers.ts`'s `eventToRow`/`rowToEvent` updated.
  - **Scope — originally Events only, extended to Reminders 2026-09-24 (superseded)**: this entry originally scoped the field to Events only, with Reminders and Schedule blocks excluded on the reasoning that a Reminder is a point-in-time nudge rather than time-blocking, so "tentative" had no obvious meaning there. **Reopened and extended to Reminders 2026-09-24** at explicit user request — `CalendarReminder.status: EventStatus`, same field, same values, same UI copy, no reminder-specific wording invented. `calendarStore` bumped to **v11**, migration backfills `status: 'confirmed'`. Supabase: `035_reminder_tentative.sql` adds `status text not null default 'confirmed'` to `calendar_reminders` (**Pending — not yet run**); `mappers.ts`'s `reminderToRow`/`rowToReminder` updated. Schedule blocks still don't get this field — they have their own separate `active`/commitment-mode mechanism.
  - **UI for reminders**: `AddCalendarItemModal`'s existing Tentative checkbox (previously gated `kind === 'event' && !isBirthday`) now shows whenever `!isBirthday` — reminders are never birthdays, so this is a pure broadening, no new branch. `CalendarReminderPane` got its own copy of the checkbox, placed the same way `CalendarEventPane`'s is (right after the Important checkbox).
  - **Rendering for reminders — no new render-site edits needed**: `CalendarView.tsx`'s single shared `isTentativeItem(item)` helper (used by all ~9 per-kind render-site checks below) was extended to also check `item.kind === 'reminder'` — since every render site already calls through that one helper rather than checking `item.status` directly, this one change was enough; no per-site edits required. The `layerVisibility.tentative` filter (previously applied only when building Event `CalDisplayItem`s) is now applied identically when building Reminder ones.
  - **UI**: a checkbox — "Tentative — not confirmed yet, just a placeholder" — in `AddCalendarItemModal` (create; shown for `kind==='event'` and not birthday, under "More options" — it started in the base options and was moved there 2026-09-20) and `CalendarEventPane` (edit; the pane has no More-options split, so it stays inline) — default unchecked. Unchecking it later is how a tentative event gets "confirmed."
  - **Rendering**: `.calItemTentative` (`CalendarView.module.css`) — a diagonal repeating-linear-gradient `background-image` blended via `background-blend-mode: overlay` over whatever background the item already has (its kind's base color, or an Endeavour color set inline via `getPillStyle`/`getSpanPillStyle`). The `background-image` is `!important` deliberately: an inline `style={{ background: color }}` (as `getPillStyle`/`getSpanPillStyle` set for an Endeavour-colored item) would otherwise silently clobber the class's `background-image`, since a non-important inline style normally beats any stylesheet rule regardless of specificity — `!important` is the documented, correct way to still override that. Applied everywhere a `CalDisplayItem`/`SpanSlot` of `kind:'event'` renders: month pills, week/day untimed rows, week/day hourly time-grid blocks, and multi-day span pills — one small `isTentativeItem()`/`slot.status` check added to each of the ~9 render sites rather than a shared component, matching this file's existing "small check duplicated per render site" convention (see the `getItemEndMinutes`/`getItemLocation` precedent for when that duplication *did* get extracted instead — only once it had actually caused a bug, which this hasn't).
  - **Layer filter**: `settingsStore.calendarLayerVisibility` gained a `tentative` key (default `true`) — a 5th toggle in `CalendarLayersPicker`, confirmed with the user as worth adding since the infrastructure already existed for exactly this. It's a filter *on top of* the existing `events` layer, not a separate category — hiding `events` still hides tentative ones too; this toggle only lets tentative ones specifically be hidden while confirmed events keep showing. `settingsStore` bumped to **v2**: existing users' already-persisted `calendarLayerVisibility` object (missing this new key) needed a backfill migration, not just an in-code default — zustand's `persist` merge is shallow, so a persisted object missing a key doesn't fall back to the in-code default for that one key, it silently ends up `undefined` (falsy), which would have hidden every tentative event by default for anyone who already had settings persisted before this shipped.
- [x] **Calendar: editing individual occurrences of a repeating event/reminder** (2026-09-20). Previously a repeat was all-or-nothing — opening any occurrence edited/deleted the whole series. Now follows the standard "this occurrence / this and following / all" pattern.
  - **Data**: `RepeatConfig.exceptions?: string[]` (YYYY-MM-DD dates removed from the series). It lives inside `repeat`, an existing jsonb column, so it needed **no** schema change or store version bump. `src/utils/recurrence.ts` owns the logic (moved out of `CalendarView.tsx`): `expandRepeat` skips exceptions; `withException` / `endedBefore` / `tailOf` build the resulting repeat configs (`tailOf` carries the remaining count for a count-limited series). `count` still counts skipped dates — deleting one occurrence of "5 times" leaves 4 shown, it doesn't extend the series.
  - **Store actions** (`calendarStore`): `skip/endBefore/detach/split` × `Event`/`Reminder`. *skip* = add an exception; *endBefore* = end the series the day before (deletes it outright if that's the first occurrence); *detach* = exception on the original + a standalone copy for that date; *split* = end the original the day before + a new series from that date (so later occurrences can be edited independently). Copies get `crossAppRefs: []` and lose external-sync provenance.
  - **UI**: `CalendarView` items now carry `occurrenceDate`; clicking one calls `openCalendarEventPane(id, occurrenceDate)` / `openCalendarReminderPane` (`uiStore.editingCalendar{Event,Reminder}Occurrence`). `RecurrenceScopeBar` (top of both panes, only when the item repeats) offers *Edit only this one*, *Edit this and following*, *Delete only this one*, *Delete this and following* — the two Edit actions split the item out and switch the pane to the copy, so the pane's live-edit fields below keep working unchanged; the fields otherwise still edit the whole series. The footer button reads "Delete all occurrences" for a repeating item. The panes' `saveRepeat` preserves `exceptions` when the repeat settings are edited.
  - **Notifications**: `useNotificationChecker` skips a base date that was individually deleted. (It has only ever notified for an item's base date, not each generated occurrence — pre-existing, unchanged.)
- [x] **Calendar: "important" flag** (2026-09-20). `CalendarEvent.important` / `CalendarReminder.important` (boolean, default false; store v6 backfills). Checkbox "❗ Important — highlight on the calendar" in `AddCalendarItemModal` (base options) and both panes. Rendered as `.calItemImportant` — a red **inset box-shadow** outline (not a border, so it can't shift time-grid block geometry or be clobbered by the inline `borderLeft` Endeavour/Schedule color) plus a ❗ prefixed onto the item's `typeIcon`; applied at every render site via `isImportantItem()` (month/week/day/untimed/day-pane, and multi-day span pills via `SpanSlot.important`).
- [x] **Calendar: whole-day reminder notifications** (2026-09-20). A reminder with a date but no time used to never notify (`reminderTrigger` returned null without a time). It now carries its own moment: `CalendarReminder.notifyDaysBefore` (0 = on the day, 1 = the day before…) and `notifyAtTime` (HH:MM), **defaulting to 1 day before at 17:00** (`src/config/notifyDefaults.ts`). Edited with `AllDayNotifyField` (a "N days before / On the day" select + `TimeInput`) under a "Notify me" label in `AddCalendarItemModal` and `CalendarReminderPane`, shown only while the reminder has no time — once a time is set, the reminder simply notifies at that time and the field disappears (its values are kept, not cleared). Notification body reads "Reminder tomorrow" / "today" / "in N days" (task-deadline shadow reminders: "Due …"), so an untimed task deadline now notifies too. `calendarStore` v7 backfills the defaults on existing reminders. **Two guards in `reminderTrigger`** so upgrading doesn't spam: a trigger that predates the reminder's `createdAt`, or is more than 24 h old, is ignored (only applied to this whole-day path; timed reminders behave as before). A consequence: creating a reminder for tomorrow *after* today's 17:00 gets no evening-before notification, since that moment had already passed. Supabase: migration 026. Not built: turning this notification off entirely for a reminder.
- [x] **Google Calendar import: notifications** (2026-09-20). Imported events used to arrive with no notification. `mapGoogleEvent` now sets `notifyBeforeValue/Unit` via `deriveNotifyBefore` (`src/utils/googleReminders.ts`): (1) copy Google's **popup** reminder — the event's own overrides, or, when it says `useDefault`, its **calendar's** `defaultReminders` (`api/google-calendar-sync.ts` now also fetches the calendar list and sends each result's `defaultReminders` + `accessRole`); email/SMS reminders are ignored; if several popups exist the one **closest to the start** wins (this app holds one lead time per event). (2) Reminders deliberately cleared in Google (`useDefault: false`, none listed) stay **off**. (3) Otherwise fall back to **30 min before** (timed) or **7 h before midnight = 17:00 the evening before** (all-day, matching the whole-day Reminder default) — but only on calendars where `accessRole` is owner/writer, so read-only ones (public holidays, subscribed calendars) don't notify for every entry.
  - **All-day events can now notify at all**: `eventTrigger` used to return null without a `startTime`. It now counts the lead time back from midnight at the start of the day — exactly how Google measures all-day reminders ("1 day before at 9 AM" = 900 min = 15 hours) — with body text "All day today/tomorrow/in N days". This applies to native all-day events too, when "Notify before" is ticked (a 1-hour lead on an all-day event fires at 23:00 the night before).
  - **Stale guards** in `eventTrigger`: a trigger more than 24 h old is ignored, and for imported events one that predates the import (`createdAt`) is ignored — otherwise importing the last month of events would fire a notification for every one of them.
  - `calendarStore` v8: already-imported, never-edited (`updatedAt === createdAt`) Google events with no notification get one derived from the reminders kept in `sourceRaw` (only the event's own — the calendar defaults weren't stored, so those fall to the fallback). Sync still only ever *creates* events, so **events already imported before this keep whatever they had** apart from that migration; re-deriving on every sync would overwrite a deliberate change.
  - Not built: a setting for the fallback lead time; keeping *multiple* reminders per event; syncing reminder changes made later in Google.
- [x] **Calendar: optional notify-before** (2026-09-20). `CalendarEvent.notifyBeforeValue` is now `number | null`; null = no notification (opt-in, like Repeat). Checkbox "Notify before" in `AddCalendarItemModal` (More options, default off) and `CalendarEventPane`; the number/unit inputs only show when checked. **Existing events keep their integer value, so everything that was notifying still does**; new events (including Google-synced and Android quick-add ones) default to off. `eventTrigger` returns null when it's null. `notify_before_value` is now nullable in Supabase (migration 025).
- [x] **Notes: Create ▸ Calendar item from a selection** (2026-09-20). The second target of the cross-app "Create ▸" menu (Ctrl+Q → `2`), completing the BACKLOG item. Same architecture as Task — see "Cross-app linking".
  - `inferCalendarItemFromSelection(text, extraLinks)` (`src/utils/textToTask.ts`) reuses the task date/time/link inference (the date step was factored into a shared `inferDate`). New: `findTimeRange` ("2pm-3pm", "2-3pm", "14:00 to 15:30" — each side needs a colon or am/pm, so a bare "10-15" isn't a time), and an event-vs-reminder guess (a time range, or a word like meeting/appointment/lunch/interview, → event; otherwise reminder). The first link becomes an event's `location`, the rest go in notes (calendar items have no links field). Always shown to the user pre-filled in the real `AddCalendarItemModal` (which still has the Event/Reminder toggle) before anything is created.
  - **Title cleanup**: whatever gets lifted into its own field is removed from the title — the date, the time or time range, and any plain URL (which goes to location/notes) — together with the connector words that introduced it (`on`/`by`/`before`/`until`/`till`/`at`/`from`/`around`/`@`, plus an article: "on the 5th"). "The report is due on 2026-10-02" → title "The report is due"; "Team meeting on Friday at 2pm-3pm" → "Team meeting". Words that say what the thing is ("due") stay. Tidies leftover punctuation/empty brackets; if nothing meaningful is left (the selection was only a date) it keeps the original text. `stripSpans` in `textToTask.ts`; the date/time/range matchers now return their position (`inferDateMatch`, `findTimeMatch`, `findTimeRange().span`). The same cleanup applies to Task creation (`inferTaskFromSelection`) — its time is only stripped when a date was also found, and priority words ("urgent") stay in the title.
  - `showAddCalendarItem(date, kind, time, title, extra?)` gained `extra: CalendarItemPrefillExtra` (`endTime`, `notes`, `location`, `collectionId`; `uiStore.calendarItemExtra`). Endeavour inherits from the note, falling back to the Notes section's focused one — same rule as Task.
  - **Link round-trip**: `CalendarEvent.crossAppRefs` / `CalendarReminder.crossAppRefs` (`CrossAppRef[]`, store v6 backfills `[]`, `cross_app_refs jsonb` in migration 025) hold the reverse link. `AddCalendarItemModal` records the note as a ref and calls `resolveArtifactLink(id, 'event' | 'reminder')` — the new optional `targetType` arg matters because the user can flip Event/Reminder in the modal after the toolbar guessed; NoteEditor then applies the `ArtifactLinkMark` (📅 for event, ⏰ for reminder). Closing the modal without saving clears the pending link.
  - **No dead links**: `crossAppLinkCleanup.ts` gained `deleteEventWithCleanup` / `deleteReminderWithCleanup` (strip the note's mark; used by both panes' Delete), `deleteNoteWithCleanup` now also strips the note from every event/reminder's `crossAppRefs`, and `removeCrossAppRefFromTarget` handles event/reminder targets. Clicking the mark switches to Calendar, jumps it to the item's date (`uiStore.requestCalendarDate` → `pendingCalendarDate`, consumed by `CalendarView`'s `jumpToDate` — same pending-request shape as `pendingListSelectionId`, since the visible month/week is local state), and opens the event/reminder pane; both panes have a "Linked items" `CrossAppRefPicker` like TaskPane. Verified end-to-end (create → mark appears → click opens pane → delete event → mark removed, text kept).
  - Not built: linking a note from *inside* `AddCalendarItemModal` (only the Notes selection flow creates a link at creation time; add more from the pane afterwards).
- [x] **Notes: switching notebooks closes the open note; the empty editor shows where you are** (2026-09-20). `uiStore.setSelectedNoteTag` now also clears `editingNoteId` when the notebook actually changes — previously the old note stayed in the editor under the new notebook's list. The editor column's empty state (`ChronicleView/NotebookLocationView`) is a small clickable tree: the path from the root notebook down, the current notebook highlighted "◀ here", then its sub-notebooks and notes (respecting the Endeavour filter) — click a notebook to jump there or a note to open it, so it doubles as navigation. With no notebook selected it still says "Select a notebook to get started".
  - **Related NoteEditor fix**: the debounced autosave wasn't flushed when the note closed or another note loaded. Closing on notebook change made this easy to hit, and the loading-another-note case was a latent bug — the pending timer read the editor at fire time, i.e. after `setContent`, and could save the *new* note's content under the *old* note's id. The note-load effect now flushes first (while the editor still holds the old content), and an unmount cleanup flushes any pending edit. Verified: text typed <1.5 s before switching notebooks was persisted.
- [x] **Notes Supabase sync** (2026-09-19) — Notes was localStorage-only since it shipped; `notes`/`note_tags` existed as tables since `008_notes_initial.sql` (reserved early, never wired into `syncService.ts`) but were missing every field added to the domain model since. `020_notes_sync.sql` catches both up (`abstract`, `parent_id`, `tabs`, `main_tab_name`, `tab_order`, `template_id`, `collection_id`, `tag_data`, `is_encrypted` on `notes`; `kind`, `field_schema`, `preset_key`, `collection_id` on `note_tags`; `deleted_at` tombstones on both) and creates a new `structured_tag_entries` table (didn't exist at all — that feature shipped after 008). Wired into the exact same `syncService.ts` pipeline as every other synced store — soft-delete tombstones, `updatedAt`-aware `mergeRecords()`, the `enqueue()` mutex (see "Cross-device sync") — via new mappers (`noteToRow`/`rowToNote`, `noteTagToRow`/`rowToNoteTag`, `structuredTagEntryToRow`/`rowToStructuredTagEntry` in `mappers.ts`). `noteStore` bumped to **v11** (backfills `isEncrypted: false`). `runInitSync`'s `isEmpty` push-vs-pull gate now also checks live `notes` rows — an account with only notes synced (nothing yet in tasks/collections/events/schedules/lists) is correctly treated as non-empty and pulled, not overwritten. Applied to the live Supabase project (see "Live migration status").
- [x] **Client-side encryption for Note content** (2026-09-19) — a per-note opt-in "encrypt this" toggle, not full-schema encryption; see BACKLOG.md "Client-side encryption for sensitive content" for the full design discussion this followed (why per-item over full-schema, why the wrapping secret can't be derived from the Supabase login password, the recovery-code and "trust this device" decisions). Confirmed motivation: as the Supabase project admin, the app owner can read every row via the dashboard/SQL editor regardless of RLS policies — RLS only gates the PostgREST API layer — so nothing previously protected data from the operator's own access; this is the first feature that does.
  - **`src/services/vault.ts`** (new) — the whole mechanism. Generates a symmetric AES-GCM "vault key" client-side (Web Crypto `SubtleCrypto`), wraps it twice with PBKDF2-derived keys (250,000 iterations) — once from a user-chosen passphrase, once from a randomly generated 128-bit recovery code (`XXXX-XXXX-…` formatted) — and stores only the two wrapped blobs in a new `user_vault` table (`019_user_vault.sql`, one row per user, RLS `auth.uid() = user_id`). The raw vault key and both unwrap secrets never cross the network; Supabase only ever sees ciphertext plus the (already-public) salts/IVs needed to re-derive the wrap keys locally. `getVaultStatus()`/`onVaultStatus()` expose `'unknown' | 'not-set-up' | 'locked' | 'unlocked'` as a plain module-level status (not a Zustand store, deliberately — a persisted store risks someone later adding the raw key to its serialized state by accident; module-scoped state makes that mistake structurally harder).
  - **Why the wrapping secret can't be the Supabase login password**: Supabase's own server verifies that password on every sign-in, so anything derivable from it is something Supabase's infrastructure necessarily touches — if it could also derive the vault key, a breach (or the admin) could reconstruct it too, defeating the entire point. The vault passphrase is a deliberately separate secret Supabase's server never sees or verifies at all.
  - **"Trust this device"**: `trustThisDevice(userId)`/`untrustThisDevice(userId)` cache the *unwrapped* key in IndexedDB (`organisaitor-vault` DB), keyed by user id — checked by `checkVaultStatus()` on sign-in before falling back to `'locked'`. Pure local convenience, not a weaker security model — Supabase never has access to the cached key either way. Default-on in the Settings UI so normal daily use never shows a lock screen; only a genuinely new/untrusted device prompts for the passphrase. **Not yet built**: an OS-backed secure store for this cache on Tauri (`tauri-plugin-stronghold` or the OS keychain) or Android (Keystore) — currently IndexedDB on every platform, flagged in BACKLOG.md as a real upgrade path, not required for this pass.
  - **Recovery code — mandatory at setup**, shown exactly once with an "I've saved this" checkbox gating the "Continue" button (`AccountPane.tsx`) — there is no way to retrieve it again afterward, and no support/account-recovery path exists if both the passphrase and recovery code are lost. This severity was a deliberate, discussed decision (see BACKLOG.md), not an oversight.
  - **Settings UI** — `AccountPane.tsx`, a new bordered `.vaultSection` between the sync hint and the Force-upload button (signed-in state only): first-time setup form (passphrase + confirm), the recovery-code-confirmation step, a locked state that shows only a clickable 🔒 "Encryption locked — click to unlock" button (the passphrase form stays hidden until it's clicked, then offers passphrase or, via a toggle link, the recovery code — either independently unwraps the same vault key — with a "Trust this device" checkbox and a Cancel), and an unlocked-state badge with a "Lock now" button.
  - **Superseded 2026-09-20 — encryption now covers the whole note, not just `content`** (see the next entry, "Client-side encryption for Notes — comprehensive"). The vault mechanism above is unchanged; what changed is what gets encrypted and how it's read. The `NoteEditor` wiring originally described here (inline decrypt-on-load / encrypt-on-save) was removed in favour of the store-level routing below.
  - **Not yet extended to other apps**: Lists/Tasks/Calendar's existing Add/Edit UI is untouched in this pass — the underlying vault mechanism is generic and app-agnostic by design (encrypting any other field later is a small, additive change), but there was no immediate trigger to wire the toggle into their forms yet. See BACKLOG.md for the confirmed scope decision and reasoning.
- [x] **Client-side encryption for Notes — comprehensive** (2026-09-20). Extends the vault to every field of a note that can carry sensitive text, plus the structured-tag entries derived from it, so the cloud copy of an encrypted note reveals nothing but its organisation. Confirmed with the user: *"the most important thing is that things are encrypted when they go into the cloud."*
  - **What's encrypted** (when a note's 🔒 toggle is on): `title`, `content`, `abstract`, every extra tab's **name and content**, `mainTabName`, `tagData` (per-note attribute values, e.g. a Source URL), **and** each `StructuredTagEntry` created from the note (`term` is a verbatim excerpt of the note's text, `fields` its expansion/explanation). **Deliberately still plaintext**: `tagIds` (which notebook/tags — needed for the tree and filtering), `color`, `pinned`, `parentId`, `tabOrder` (tab ids only), `templateId` (reveals the note *type*, e.g. "Meeting Minutes"), `collectionId`, and all timestamps. **Not covered at all**: notebook/tag *names*, and anything created *from* a note in another app — a Task made via "Create ▸ Task" keeps its plaintext title (the text you selected) in the `tasks` table. Worth knowing before relying on this for something sensitive.
  - **Storage model — one envelope, sensitive fields blanked, at every layer.** An encrypted note's sensitive fields are serialised into one `NoteSecrets` object, AES-GCM-encrypted with the vault key into `Note.encryptedPayload`, and **emptied on the stored object** (`title: ''`, `content: ''`, `abstract: null`, `tabs: []`, `tagData: {}`). That blanked form is what lives in zustand state, in `notes-storage` localStorage, and in Supabase (`notes.encrypted_payload`, migration `024`) — so a leaked localStorage dump is as unreadable as the cloud row, and **`syncService.ts`/`mappers.ts` stayed dumb pass-throughs with zero crypto awareness** (which is why none of the sync merge/tombstone logic changed). `StructuredTagEntry` gets the same `isEncrypted` + `encryptedPayload` pair (`term: ''`, `fields: {}` while stored). `noteStore` bumped to **v12** (backfills both fields).
  - **Plaintext lives only in a memory cache** (`src/services/noteSecrets.ts`): a `Map` per entity, never persisted, filled while the vault is unlocked and wiped on lock. A cache entry is trusted only while it matches the note's current `encryptedPayload` (a sync pull that replaces the payload makes it stale until re-decrypted) or is `dirty` (an edit applied to the plaintext whose re-encryption hasn't landed yet).
  - **THE RULE: read an encrypted note only through `noteView(note)`** (or `useNoteView(id)` / `useNoteViews()` / `useEntryViews()` in React — `src/store/noteViews.ts`), never `note.title`/`.content`/`.abstract`/`.tabs`/`.tagData` straight off the store: those are blank for an encrypted note. `noteView` returns the plaintext when the cache has it, or a locked placeholder (`title: 'Encrypted note'`, everything else blank) when it doesn't; `isNoteLocked(note)` says which. Converted to read views: `NoteEditor`, `NoteList`, `TagView`, `quickAccess`, `CrossAppRefPicker`, `EditNoteMetaModal`, `NoteEditorPane`, `crossAppLinkCleanup`. **Any new code that displays or searches a note's text must do the same** — forgetting it shows a blank/"Encrypted note" instead of the text, and (worse) writing such a blank back would destroy data, which is why writes are centralised (next bullet).
  - **Writes go through the store actions, which route encrypted notes themselves.** `updateNote`, `addNoteTab`/`removeNoteTab`/`renameNoteTab`/`updateNoteTabContent`/`renameMainTab`, `updateStructuredTagEntry` all funnel through `editNote`/`editEntry` (module-level in `noteStore.ts`): for a plain note that's the old behaviour; for an encrypted one the edit is applied to the cached plaintext *immediately* (the UI never waits on crypto), the stored object is re-blanked, and a background re-encryption is queued (`queueEncrypt`, one in-flight per note, **always encrypting the latest secrets** — otherwise an older payload could land after a newer one and silently revert the note; verified with a 25-edit burst). An edit to a **locked** encrypted note is refused with a console warning rather than risk corrupting it. `addStructuredTagEntry` on an encrypted, unlocked note creates the entry born-encrypted. `NoteEditor` no longer contains any crypto — it saves plaintext like it always did.
  - **New actions**: `encryptNote(id)` (rejects if the vault is locked — never falls back to plaintext), `decryptNote(id)` (rejects if the note's plaintext isn't available), both also converting the note's entries; `upgradeLegacyEncryptedNote(id)`.
  - **Legacy upgrade**: notes encrypted by the first version (only `content` encrypted, title etc. plaintext, no `encryptedPayload`) are folded into the new all-fields payload automatically the next time the vault is unlocked (`noteSecretsSync.ts` → `upgradeLegacyEncryptedNote`). Failures are remembered so an unreadable legacy note isn't retried on every store change.
  - **`noteSecretsSync.ts`** (started from `App.tsx`): re-runs on every vault-status change and every notes/entries change — decrypts whatever encrypted note/entry has a missing or stale cache entry (so unlock, trusted-device auto-unlock and notes pulled in by sync all just work), and clears the whole cache whenever the vault isn't unlocked.
  - **Locking is now sticky and safe.** `lockVault()` is async: it runs **`flush` hooks** first (`NoteEditor` pushes its pending autosave/title/abstract debounces into the store, which queues their encryption), then **`settle` hooks** (`flushEncryptions()` waits for every queued re-encryption to land), *then* drops the key — so locking can never discard an in-flight edit. Two phases because they depend on each other and registration order can't guarantee it (the settle hook registers at app start, long before an editor mounts). It then also **calls `untrustThisDevice()`** and sets a `manuallyLocked` flag. **Root cause of "locking doesn't stay locked"**: Lock now used to clear only the in-memory key and leave the trusted-device copy in IndexedDB, and Supabase re-fires `SIGNED_IN` on every tab refocus, so `checkVaultStatus` found the cached key and quietly unlocked again. The cost of sticky locking: after a deliberate lock you type the passphrase once to unlock (tick "Trust this device" to re-cache it). *Not root-caused*: the separate report that the Account pane didn't visibly update after clicking Lock — not reproducible from the code; possibly the same re-unlock racing the click. Re-test.
  - **The 🔒 is a button** (2026-09-20) in the editor title bar and the Notes list rows: clicking it (or "Remove encryption" in the Note ▾ menu) opens `DecryptPrompt`, which asks for the vault passphrase (or recovery code) *even if the vault is already unlocked*, verifies it (`vault.verifyVaultSecret()` — checks without changing lock state; if the vault is locked, entering it unlocks and decrypts in one step), then calls `decryptNote`, which permanently removes the encryption and every lock indicator. `decryptNote` now decrypts from the stored payload when the cache is cold, since the prompt can unlock and decrypt before `noteSecretsSync` has warmed the cache.
  - **Lock icon 🔒** next to an encrypted note's name in: the editor's title bar (with the title input disabled while locked), the Notes list rows, Quick Access results (also replaces the 📝 icon, subtitle "Note · Encrypted"), the tag view (note headings and encrypted entries), the cross-app note picker + its chips, and the note-details modal. A locked note's name shows as "Encrypted note".
  - **Migration `024_encrypted_note_payloads.sql`** (applied to live; adds `notes.encrypted_payload`, `structured_tag_entries.is_encrypted`/`encrypted_payload`) — see "Live migration status".
  - **Verified** with a Node harness bundling the *real* store/vault/sync-mapper code against a stubbed Supabase (21 checks, all passing): stored note, sync row and entry row contain none of nine planted secret strings; view returns full plaintext when unlocked and only the placeholder when locked; instant view update on edit with payload re-encryption after; rapid-edit ordering; tab ops; entries born encrypted and following their note through encrypt/decrypt; lock wipes plaintext and refuses edits; wrong passphrase rejected; passphrase and recovery-code unlock both restore everything; a payload pulled from "another device" decrypts on unlock; legacy upgrade; `encryptNote` while locked rejects. **Not verified**: the React UI, the trusted-device IndexedDB path, and the real Supabase round trip — no browser/live DB in the harness.
  - **Known limitations**: an *unlocked* encrypted note's plaintext sits in the ProseMirror doc and memory cache like any open document (encryption is at rest/in the cloud, not against someone at an unlocked, signed-in machine); notebook/tag names stay plaintext; a locked note's stale `ArtifactLinkMark` can't be stripped when its linked task is deleted (harmless — click already checks the target exists); title/abstract inputs while locked are disabled rather than hidden.
- [x] **Client-side encryption for Lists** (2026-09-20) — the Notes mechanism (above) applied to Lists: same vault, same envelope model, same rules, with `services/listSecrets.ts` / `listSecretsSync.ts` / `store/listViews.ts` as the direct twins of the three note modules (the crypto, serialised re-encrypt queue and cache-version counter are *shared* from `noteSecrets.ts`, not duplicated).
  - **Granularity: per list.** A list is either plain or encrypted; encrypting it converts all its items, and items added later are born encrypted (never plaintext in the store). Per-item choice was rejected — a "credentials" list where some rows are encrypted and some aren't is a footgun.
  - **Encrypted**: list `name`, `description`, `typeId` (the type — "Credentials", "Memberships" — is itself revealing), `fieldSchema` (field names), `tabs` (tab names); each item's `title`, `data` (all field values), `notes`, `links`. **Plaintext by design**: `kind`, `color`, `icon`, item `status`/`tabId`/`order`, ids, timestamps — and the *number* of items and tabs. `ListItem`/`List` gained `isEncrypted` + `encryptedPayload` (blanked stored form: `name ''`, `description null`, `typeId null`, `fieldSchema []`, `tabs []`; items `title ''`, `data {}`, `notes null`, `links []`); `listStore` **v5** migration backfills both; `syncService.ts`/mappers stayed pass-throughs apart from carrying the two columns.
  - **THE RULE, same as notes: read through `listView()`/`itemView()`** (React: `useListViews()`/`useListItemViews()`/`useListView(id)`), never `list.name`/`item.data` straight off `useListStore` — they're blank for an encrypted list. Converted: `ListsSection`, `AddListModal`, `AddListItemModal`, `quickAccess`'s list provider (🔒 icon, "· Encrypted" subtitle). Writes go through `updateList`/`updateListItem`/`addListItem`/`removeListTab`, which route encrypted lists via `editList`/`editItem` (cache first, background re-encrypt, refused while locked — `addListItem` returns `''` for a locked encrypted list).
  - **UI**: an **"🔒 Encrypt"** button in the list header (enabled only while the vault is unlocked); a **"🔒 Encrypt this list"** checkbox in `AddListModal`'s create form; a clickable 🔒 that opens `DecryptPrompt` (passphrase required, as for notes). **Placement (user-directed, after two iterations):** in the list header the lock sits in the top-right button group *in the same slot as the "🔒 Encrypt" button* (Encrypt when plain, lock when encrypted — nothing else moves); in the sidebar the list's own icon is left alone and the lock is a fixed slot at the **far right** of the row, after the hover edit/delete markers, so those appear to its left and never cover it. A locked list shows a placeholder ("This list is encrypted" + **Unlock encryption**, which opens the Account pane) instead of its contents, and hides Edit/+ Tab. `ListsSection`'s "reset a stale tab to All" effect is skipped while a list is locked so the remembered tab isn't discarded at startup before the vault unlocks.
  - **Migration `027_encrypted_lists.sql`** (`is_encrypted` + `encrypted_payload` on `lists` and `list_items`) — applied to live, see "Live migration status".
  - **Verified** with a second Node harness (`19 checks`, real store/vault/mappers, stubbed Supabase): stored list/items and both sync rows contain none of ten planted secrets (including the type id); views return full plaintext unlocked and only placeholders locked; instant view update on edit with payload re-encryption after; 25-edit burst ends on the last value *in the payload*, not just the cache; born-encrypted item adds; list rename/add-tab/`removeListTab` route through encryption; lock refuses edits and adds; `encryptList` while locked rejects; `verifyVaultSecret` right/wrong/recovery without changing lock state; passphrase and recovery unlock; `decryptList` and `decryptNote` from a *cold* cache right after unlock; re-encrypt after decrypt. **Not verified**: the React UI, IndexedDB trusted-device path, real Supabase round trip.
  - **Is it strong enough for passwords / banking details? Honest assessment (recorded because it was asked directly):** the *cipher* is — AES-256-GCM with a random per-vault key, a fresh random IV per envelope, authenticated (tampering is detected), and the server never sees a key. The *practical* strength is bounded by weaker links: (1) **the passphrase** — the vault key is wrapped with PBKDF2-SHA256 at 250,000 iterations; if a cloud dump leaked, an attacker can guess passphrases offline, and the setup form only requires 8 characters, which is weak against that. A long passphrase (5+ random words) makes it strong; a short one doesn't. (2) **plaintext exists while unlocked** — in memory and the DOM, and (with "Trust this device") the unwrapped key sits in plain IndexedDB on that device, so anyone with access to an unlocked/signed-in machine or its browser profile can read it; encryption protects the *cloud copy*, not a compromised device. (3) **metadata is not hidden** — the server sees that a list exists, its kind/colour/icon, item counts, tab structure, timestamps. (4) **no recovery beyond the passphrase or the recovery code** — lose both and the data is unrecoverable, by design. (5) **the unencrypted local copies of anything created *from* encrypted content** (e.g. a Task created from an encrypted note) are separate plaintext. Net: fine for sensitive lists to keep out of the Supabase operator's view, *provided the passphrase is strong*; for high-stakes secrets (banking, master passwords) a dedicated password manager is still the better tool. Possible hardening, not built: a passphrase-strength meter / longer minimum, raising PBKDF2 iterations or moving to Argon2 (needs a WASM dependency), OS-keychain storage for the trusted-device key, auto-relock after inactivity.
- [x] **Account pane: "Remember my email"** (`AccountPane.tsx`): sign-in form pre-fills the email from localStorage key `todo-remembered-email` (deliberately not a zustand store and not in `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS` — it's a per-device convenience that shouldn't ride along in backups) and focuses the password field when pre-filled. Checkbox is shown on the sign-in tab only, checked by default only if an email was already remembered; saved/cleared on successful sign-in. Password is never stored.
- [x] **Portfolio Supabase sync** (2026-09-19) — `portfolioStore.ts` was localStorage-only since it shipped; new `watchlist_items`, `portfolio_tags`, `investment_purposes` tables (`021_portfolio_sync.sql`, applied to live — see "Live migration status") wired into the standard `syncService.ts` pipeline (soft-delete tombstones, `mergeRecords()`, the `enqueue()` mutex). No `portfolioStore` version bump needed — no domain-model fields changed, only new Supabase tables. `PortfolioTag`/`InvestmentPurpose` have no `createdAt`/`updatedAt` (same as the app-wide `Tag`) — `mergeRecords()`'s existing remote-wins-but-tombstone-aware fallback applies unchanged. `investment_purposes`' built-in seed rows (`SEED_PURPOSES`, fixed ids like `ip-dividend`) are synced as ordinary data, **not** given the `list_types`/Fitness-activity-types "never sync, always re-seeded" treatment — portfolioStore has no active re-seed-on-load mechanism and never blocks editing/deleting a seed purpose, so their fixed ids just naturally deduplicate across devices via the same upsert-by-id every other synced row already uses. `runInitSync`'s `isEmpty` gate now also checks live `watchlist_items` rows. **Deliberately not synced**: `WatchlistColumn[]` (`columnConfig`, per-device table display preferences) — a cosmetic setting, not portfolio data, matching this project's existing precedent of leaving UI-only preferences out of a sync pass unless specifically asked for.

- [x] **Voice dictation (`Ctrl+D`) — v1, desktop-first, Google Speech-to-Text behind a swappable engine** (2026-09-20). Click into a text field, press `Ctrl+D`, speak, pause: the text is typed into that field. Press `Ctrl+D` again **or plain `Enter`** to finish (a phrase still being spoken is transcribed), `Esc` cancels. Stops on its own after 20 s of silence, 2 minutes total, or when the window is hidden — the mic is never left open. With nothing focused it shows "Click into a text field first…"; **the voice pane / AI agent entry point is deliberately not built yet** (the transcript's only destination today is the focused field — see BACKLOG.md "Voice → agent").
  - **Scope decisions (confirmed with the user):** signed-in only (the whole app is account-oriented), privacy of dictated text is not a concern for this use, recording/meeting minutes deferred (a later, paid feature), local Whisper is a later engine. Not over-engineered: the one swap point is `SpeechEngine`. Alternatives considered and rejected for v1: the browser Web Speech API (unreliable in WebView2 — permission errors and empty transcripts are reported; absent in Android's WebView), Windows native `SpeechRecognition` (needs an OS privacy setting, reported lag, Windows-only), Google streaming (gRPC-only, can't run on Vercel Edge).
  - **Pipeline** (`src/services/speech/`): `audioCapture.ts` (getUserMedia + an AudioWorklet loaded from a Blob, AudioContext at 16 kHz so the browser resamples; delivers 50 ms frames) → `utteranceDetector.ts` (energy VAD with an adaptive noise floor; 300 ms pre-roll, ends an utterance after 1.4 s of silence, ignores <250 ms blips, force-cuts at 50 s) → `engines/googleEngine.ts` (implements `SpeechEngine.transcribe(pcm, {language})`) → `insertText.ts` → the field. `dictation.ts` is the controller: a module-level state machine (`idle/starting/listening/finishing/error`), utterances transcribed serially so text lands in spoken order, a `sessionId` so a cancelled session's late results are dropped. **To change the STT backend, change the one `engine` line in `dictation.ts`.**
  - **Text insertion** (`insertText.ts`): `findTextTarget()` accepts the focused text `input`/`textarea`/contenteditable (not number/date/time/checkbox inputs, not disabled/read-only — TimeInput's segments are number inputs, so they're skipped). `insertTextAt()` uses `document.execCommand('insertText')` — deprecated but the one call that behaves like typing in all three kinds (fires React's input events, undo works, ProseMirror/the Notes editor treats it as input) — with a `setRangeText` fallback for plain inputs. It refocuses the remembered target if focus moved, and adds a leading space when the caret follows a non-space.
  - **Server** (`api/speech-recognize.ts`, Edge): Bearer-authenticated like the Strava endpoints; takes `{audio: base64 PCM16 16 kHz mono, language}`, calls Google's **v1 `speech:recognize` REST** with `GOOGLE_SPEECH_API_KEY` (server-only, never `VITE_`; add to Vercel's Development scope too for `vercel dev`), returns `{text}`. Chosen over streaming because Google's streaming is gRPC-only; the cost is that text appears ~1 s after each pause instead of live. Duration is computed from the payload size, never trusted from the client. Optional env: `SPEECH_MONTHLY_SECONDS` (default 3300), `GOOGLE_SPEECH_MODEL`. Reaches the desktop app through `apiFetch()` like every other `/api/*` call.
  - **Usage cap**: `030_speech_usage.sql` (applied). The edge function calls `record_speech_usage()` *before* calling Google; it enforces a per-user monthly cap (default 3300 s, just under Google's 60 free minutes) atomically. Counted in **15-second increments per request**, because Google's v1 API bills each request rounded up to 15 s (my understanding — verify on Google's pricing page): a 2 s utterance uses 15 s, so the free tier is ~240 utterances a month, not 60 minutes of talking. That is why the detector keeps natural pauses inside one utterance (1.4 s) rather than splitting aggressively. Errors map to `SpeechErrorCode` → `LABELS.voice.errors`.
  - **UI**: `VoiceIndicator` (portaled, `z-index: 500`, bottom-centre pill) — a mic in a red pulsing ring, a live 7-bar level meter (history kept in `voiceStore.levels`), "Listening…"/"Transcribing…", ✓ Finish and ✕ Cancel buttons (their `mousedown` is swallowed so they never steal focus from the field), `prefers-reduced-motion` respected; errors/notices show in the same pill for 4 s. Language: `settingsStore.speechLanguage` (`'system'` = the browser's language) with a picker under Settings → "Voice dictation" (`SPEECH_LANGUAGES` in `speech/languages.ts`).
  - **Escape**: `VoiceIndicator` registers with `useEscapeClose(cancelDictation, dictating)` like every other overlay. The indicator opens after any modal being dictated into, so it is the top of the Escape stack — Esc cancels dictation only and leaves that modal open. (`dictation.ts` has no Escape listener of its own.) **Enter** is different: plain `Enter` while listening finishes dictation via a capture-phase listener in `dictation.ts` (`onEnter`, `stopImmediatePropagation`) so it doesn't also submit the form or add a newline; `Ctrl+Enter` and `Shift+Enter` are left alone (Ctrl+Enter is the modal-submit rule). The time limits are the `IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` (20 s) and `SESSION_MAX_MS` (2 min) constants at the top of `src/services/speech/dictation.ts`.
  - **Verified**: `tsc -b`, eslint, `npm run build`, and a Node run of the utterance detector against synthetic audio (pauses kept inside one utterance, blips ignored, flush, level callbacks — 6 checks). **Not verified (needs a real run):** the mic permission prompt and `getUserMedia`/AudioWorklet inside Tauri's WebView2; the edge function against real Google (needs the Google setup + a deployed or `vercel dev` API); `execCommand` insertion into the Tiptap editor; the indicator's appearance. Also untested: Android (no mic button and no `RECORD_AUDIO` permission yet) and the PWA.
  - **Google setup the user must do** (see `.env.example`): enable the Cloud Speech-to-Text API, create an API key restricted to that API, lower the API quota, add a $1 budget alert (alerts do not cap spend), set `GOOGLE_SPEECH_API_KEY` in Vercel and `.env.local`. **Correction (2026-09-24):** the original "free-trial billing account, can't be charged unless manually upgraded" note doesn't hold for every account — this user's Cloud Console asked for a real, one-time €25 minimum payment to activate a Postpay billing account before the API would work at all, unrelated to the general new-customer trial credit. Budget for a real charge, don't assume the trial exempts you.
  - **Paused 2026-09-24, by the user's choice** — not a bug or a technical dead end. Rather than pay the €25 above, the user is using Windows' own built-in dictation (`Win+H`, works in any focused field with zero app involvement) instead, for now. `Ctrl+D` was deliberately left pointed at this (now unconfigured) pipeline rather than reworked — the `not-configured` error it shows now adds a one-line "try Win+H" hint on Windows (`errorMessage()` in `dictation.ts`). Full context, the three `Ctrl+D`-vs-Windows-dictation shapes considered, and what to do if this is picked back up: **`docs/agent-tasks/04-voice-dictation-followups.md`**.

- [x] **Shared in-app dialogs — no more native `window.confirm()` / `alert()`** (2026-09-20). Every native popup in the app (28 sites: deleting notes, notebooks, lists, list items, trackers, routines, activities, activity types, schedules, watchlist items, Endeavours/Purposes/Tags from Manage *and* the hover Sidebar; disconnecting Google Calendar; removing a field that holds data; changing the timezone; resetting or reassigning hotkeys; encryption errors) now goes through one themed dialog.
  - **API** (`src/components/ConfirmDialog/dialogs.ts`, all promise-based so call sites stay one line): `confirmDelete(noun, itemName, detail?)` — the one path for "delete permanently" (red button, Cancel focused, "This cannot be undone.", copy from `LABELS.itemActions`); `confirmDialog({ title, message, itemName?, confirmLabel?, destructive?, irreversible? })` for everything else; `alertDialog(message)` for errors. Handlers that used to be synchronous became `async` (`if (!(await confirmDelete(...))) return;`).
  - **How it works**: `src/store/dialogStore.ts` holds a queue of requests (several can be pending; they are answered one at a time, in order); `<ConfirmDialogHost />` (mounted once in `App.tsx`) renders the first. `ConfirmDialog.tsx` is portaled to `document.body`, `z-index: 1100` (above every modal tier), registers with `useEscapeClose` (Escape = Cancel, and it closes only the dialog, not the modal it opened over) and answers **Ctrl+Enter** with a capture-phase `stopImmediatePropagation` listener so a modal underneath that also binds Ctrl+Enter is not submitted at the same moment. Backdrop click = Cancel. Plain keys are swallowed so App's global single-key hotkeys don't fire behind it.
  - **Two conversions that needed care**: `SettingsPane`'s hotkey-capture listener owns every keypress while it is listening, so it now stops listening *before* opening the reassign dialog (otherwise the dialog's own keys would be captured as the new binding); the timezone-change confirm is `async` before it re-stamps calendar data.
  - **Not converted on purpose**: nothing left — `grep window.confirm` finds none. The rule going forward is in "Component patterns → Confirmations and alerts".
  - **Verified** in a headless browser (16 dialog checks + 8 call-site/stacking checks): focus on Cancel for destructive and on Confirm otherwise, Escape/Cancel/backdrop resolve false, Ctrl+Enter/Confirm resolve true, alert has one button, queue order, Escape closes the dialog but not the modal under it, Ctrl+Enter answers only the dialog, Manage → delete Endeavour end-to-end with no native dialog fired.
- [x] **`useCtrlEnterSubmit` hook, and Ctrl+Enter on every remaining modal** (2026-09-20). `src/hooks/useCtrlEnterSubmit.ts`: `useCtrlEnterSubmit(onSubmit, active?)` for modals/panes whose primary action is a plain button rather than a `<form>` submit (it keeps `onSubmit` in a ref refreshed each render, so an inline closure over current state is safe and can't go stale). Wired into `AddActivityModal` (which had none), `EditNoteMetaModal`, `EditNoteTagModal`, `EditRoutinePane`, `EditTrackerPane`, `CalendarImportReviewModal` (Import — previously excluded, but it has one unambiguous primary action), `StructuredTagPopover` and `MobileCalendarQuickAdd`. The only modal deliberately without it is `NoteTagPresetModal` (a list of independent Install buttons, no primary action). The handlers those hooks call are function *declarations* (hoisted), because the hook has to sit above the component's early `return null` and a `const` handler defined below it trips `react-hooks/immutability`.
- [x] **Codebase audit clean-up** (2026-09-20). *Deleted (nothing referenced them)*: the Vite starter leftovers (`src/App.css`, `src/assets/*`, `public/icons.svg`); `components/CreateMenu` (superseded by `FloatingToolbar`'s inline menu); `services/storage/` (`StorageAdapter`, `localStorageAdapter` — never wired; the doc sentence promising a swappable adapter is gone); unused exports (`getVaultStatus`, `useListView`, `displayBinding`, `hapticMedium/Success/Warning`, types `TrackerViewMode`, `CreateScheduleBlockInput`, `FilterState`/`FilterStatus`/`SortField`/`SortDirection` in `types/index.ts` (the real ones live in `uiStore`), the redundant `LIST_ITEM_STATUS_META` re-export, `ArtifactTargetType`); dead `uiStore` members (`calendarLayersOpen`/`toggleCalendarLayers`/`closeCalendarLayers` left over from the removed layers dropdown, `openEndeavourPicker`, `openPurposePicker`, `toggleNoteTagViewTagId`, `closeEditEntry` — `closeModal` already clears `editingEntryId`); 6 stale `eslint-disable` directives; and the `cross_app_links` table (migration 031, plus the stale Notes-architecture section of this file). *Kept although unused*: `listStore.addListType/updateListType/deleteListType` and `portfolioStore.updatePortfolioTag/deletePortfolioTag/updateInvestmentPurpose/deleteInvestmentPurpose` (the UI for them was never built — see BACKLOG.md), `uiStore.openQuickAccess` and `registerMobileBackConsumer` (documented seams for the Android entry point).
