  - *Pattern fixes*: `todo-hotkey-overrides` was missing from `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS` (custom hotkeys were dropped by backup/restore); "Endeavour" was hard-coded in ~13 places and in `hotkeys.ts` — now `LABELS.collection` / `LABELS.noCollection` / `LABELS.noneInCollection()` / `LABELS.addToCollection` / `LABELS.quickAccessPlaceholder`; `AddScheduleModal` called `useMemo` after its early return (rules-of-hooks); `CollectionPicker`'s dropdown, `MobileQuickAddBar`'s popovers and the hover `Sidebar` now register with `useEscapeClose`; ~15 static inline styles became CSS-module classes (hidden file inputs use the `hidden` attribute; `CrossAppRefPicker`'s `position: fixed` moved into its class, only the measured `top`/`left` stay inline); the Ctrl+H heading chord is registered in `hotkeys.ts`; `scheduleStore`, `notificationStore` and `hotkeyOverridesStore` now have a `version` (v1) — `scheduleStore`'s migrate backfills `requiresCommitment`/`committedDates` on existing blocks (verified: v0 data survives and is backfilled); `eslint.config.js` no longer lints `android/`, `src-tauri/` or `.vercel/` build output; `supabase/.temp/` is gitignored.

- [x] **OAuth `state` nonces — no credential in the Strava / Google Calendar connect URL** (2026-09-20; agent brief 01, task 1). Both connect flows used to put the user's live Supabase access token (a JWT) in the OAuth `state` parameter, so it landed in Google/Strava/Vercel request logs, browser history and `Referer` headers, and while valid (~1 h) it was a full credential for the account. `state` is now a random single-use nonce.
  - **Files:** `supabase/migrations/032_oauth_state.sql`; `src/services/oauthState.ts` (`mintOAuthState(provider)` → `supabase.rpc('mint_oauth_state')`); `src/services/strava.ts` and `src/services/googleCalendar.ts` (connect-URL builders now `await mintOAuthState(...)` and throw if it fails); `FitnessSection.tsx` / `CalendarSidePane.tsx` (`handleConnect` catch the throw and show it — as a message / `alertDialog`); `api/strava-oauth-callback.ts`, `api/google-calendar-oauth-callback.ts` (rewritten); `api/_lib/supabaseEdge.ts` (new `getAnonClient()`; `getUserClient()` is still used by every Bearer-authenticated endpoint).
  - **Flow:** signed-in client calls `mint_oauth_state` (security definer, uses `auth.uid()`; ≥244-bit nonce from two `gen_random_uuid()`s; 10-minute expiry; one live nonce per user+provider, so starting again invalidates the previous one and the table stays small) → nonce goes in `state` → provider redirects to the callback → the callback exchanges the `code`, then calls `save_strava_connection` / `save_calendar_connection` **with the anon key**. Those functions `delete … where nonce = … and provider = … and expires_at > now() returning user_id` — validation and consumption are one statement, so a second presentation can never succeed — and upsert the connection row for that user. Unknown / reused / expired / wrong-provider all come back as `invalid_state` (deliberately not distinguished) and the callback redirects to `/?strava=error&reason=invalid_state` (or `googleCalendar=`). The Google function also keeps the stored refresh token when the response carries none (logic moved from the callback into SQL, since the callback can no longer read the row).
  - **Decisions:** chose brief option A (nonce table + security-definer functions) because it preserves the invariant *no Supabase service-role key anywhere*. Rejected B (signed state + service-role key in two callbacks: bypasses RLS, new Vercel secret) and C (finish the OAuth in the SPA: the Tauri/Android webviews have no session at the Vercel-origin redirect destination). `oauth_states` has RLS on, no policies and no grants; each function is `revoke … from public, anon, authenticated` then granted to exactly the role that needs it (Postgres and Supabase both grant `execute` broadly by default), and uses `set search_path = ''` with schema-qualified names.
  - **Login-CSRF (accepted residual risk, recorded on purpose):** an attacker with their own account can mint a nonce and trick a *victim* into approving the provider's consent screen, which would attach the victim's calendar/Strava to the attacker's account. The old design resisted this only because the state was the victim's own secret. Binding the nonce to the starting browser needs a value that lives in that browser and reaches the callback — a cookie on the Vercel origin — but the Tauri and Android webviews start the flow from a different origin (`tauri.localhost` / `capacitor://`), and a binding the attacker can opt out of (by starting from "another platform") protects nothing, so it was not added half-way. Mitigations that do exist: the nonce is single-use and 10 minutes long, the attacker must first get the victim to open a provider consent URL and approve it, and the scopes are read-only (`calendar.readonly`, `activity:read`). A proper fix is to run the flow in the system browser with a deep-link return and a PKCE-style verifier held by the app (tracked in BACKLOG.md "OAuth flow hardening").
  - **Verified:** `tsc -b`, eslint on the touched files (only the two pre-existing `set-state-in-effect` errors that task 4 owns), and a strict `tsc` pass over both callbacks. **Not verified — needs the user:** the migration has not been run (status **Pending** in "Live migration status"), and I could not run `vercel dev` here (needs the Vercel link + Development-scope env vars, and the migration). Once `032` is run, to test: connect Strava and Google → success redirect and row present; re-open the same callback URL a second time → `invalid_state`; wait >10 min → `invalid_state`; present a Strava-minted `state` to the Google callback → `invalid_state`; confirm `eyJ` appears in no URL in the network panel. Until `032` is run, *connecting* fails with "Couldn't start the connection" (existing connections and syncing are unaffected).
  - **Not built:** cookie/PKCE browser binding (see above); purging expired `oauth_states` rows other than at the next mint (bounded to one live row per user+provider, so not needed).

- [x] **Sign-out clears this device's synced data safely** (2026-09-20; agent brief 01, task 2). `authStore.signOut()` used to wipe only tasks/calendar/tracker, leaving Notes, note tags, structured tag entries, Lists, Schedules, Portfolio, the decrypted-plaintext caches, Quick Access history and the remembered selection ids on the device — on a shared computer the next person saw the previous account's data, and a *different* account signing in could have that stale data uploaded into its cloud (the "remote looks empty → push local up" branch of `runInitSync`).
  - **Files:** `src/services/signOut.ts` (`requestSignOut()`), `src/services/clearLocalData.ts` (`clearSyncedLocalData()`), `src/store/authStore.ts` (`signOut` now calls `stopSync()` → `supabase.auth.signOut()` → `clearSyncedLocalData()`), `AccountPane.tsx` (`handleSignOut` calls `requestSignOut()`), `config/labels.ts` (`LABELS.signOut`).
  - **Flow (`requestSignOut`):** (1) `confirmDialog` — says the synced data leaves this device and comes back on sign-in, and lists what *stays* (Fitness activities/types, routine check-off history, settings) and why; (2) `lockVault()` then `untrustThisDevice()` — flushes in-flight encryptions, wipes the decrypted caches, forgets the trusted-device key — *before* anything is cleared; (3) `forceUpload(userId)` (queued behind any in-flight `initSync`); if it throws (offline, a table failing) a second destructive `confirmDialog` names the reason and offers "Sign out and discard" — Cancel leaves the user signed in with data intact; (4) `authStore.signOut()`.
  - **What is cleared** (each store reset from its own `getInitialState()`, so seeded defaults come back as on a fresh install — built-in list types, the portfolio's seed investment purposes; `persist` rewrites each localStorage key with the empty state, so `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS` is not duplicated): task, calendar, tracker, schedule, note (incl. `structuredTagEntries`), list (custom types too), portfolio (items/tags/purposes — **not** `columnConfig`), `recentItemsStore`, `notificationStore.pending`, and in `uiStore` `activeTrackerId`, `activeRoutineId`, `notesLastEditingNoteId`, `notesLastActiveTabId`, `selectedNoteTagId`, `listsLastActiveListId/TabId`, `activeCollectionIdByView`, `activePurposeIds`, `activeTagIds`, and the open task/event/reminder pane ids.
  - **Deliberately kept (only copy on this device):** `fitnessStore`, `routineStore`, `portfolioStore.columnConfig`, `settingsStore`, `hotkeyOverridesStore`, `notificationStore.notifiedLog`. Deviation from the brief's list: `notificationStore.pending` *is* cleared — it is a derived view of synced items (titles of the previous account's events), not a sole copy. Tracked in BACKLOG.md "Data that exists only on one device": when Fitness/routine instances gain sync they go into `clearSyncedLocalData()`.
  - **Decisions:** the dialog/upload orchestration lives in a service, not `authStore`, so the store doesn't import UI; `clearLocalData.ts` is a separate file so `authStore` can import it without a cycle. Cancelling the "unsaved" warning leaves the vault locked (the lock happened first, by design) — the user unlocks with their passphrase.
  - **Not covered:** an *involuntary* sign-out (Supabase `SIGNED_OUT` after a failed token refresh — `App.tsx` only stops sync and resets the vault module state) does not wipe, since the data can't be confirmed in the cloud there; the data is still on the device until the next explicit sign-out. A deletion whose sync failed earlier isn't retried by `forceUpload` (upsert only), so that item can reappear after the next sign-in.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -b`, eslint on the touched files, and a headless-Edge Playwright script (23 checks, Vite dev server, real stores, `supabase.from` stubbed to succeed/fail): cancel at the first dialog changes nothing and uploads nothing; a failing upload shows the warning with the reason, Cancel keeps the user signed in with all data intact; "Sign out and discard" and the success path both empty every synced store, re-seed the 12 built-in list types and 8 seed purposes, keep Fitness/routine/`columnConfig`/`notifiedLog`, reset the uiStore ids, leave the persisted localStorage copies empty, and the success path uploaded all 17 tables first. **Not verified:** against a real Supabase account (no credentials here) — i.e. sign out → sign in as the same account restores everything from the cloud, and as a different account shows nothing. Both rest on the existing `runInitSync` merge, which is unchanged.

- [x] **Error boundaries** (2026-09-20; agent brief 01, task 3). There was no React error boundary, so any render exception (a malformed persisted record, an unexpected `null`) unmounted the whole tree into a blank white page — no way to reach Settings or export a backup, and reloading re-crashed on the same data.
  - **Files:** `src/components/ErrorBoundary/ErrorBoundary.tsx` + `.module.css` (class component, `getDerivedStateFromError` / `componentDidCatch`, no new dependency); `src/main.tsx` (`scope="app"` around `<App />`); `src/App.tsx` (`scope="section"` around the section switcher, `key={activeView}`); `src/utils/backupExport.ts` (`downloadBackup()` moved out of `AccountPane.tsx`, which now imports it); `LABELS.errorBoundary` in `config/labels.ts`.
  - **Behaviour:** app-level fallback (full page, themed via the CSS variables): one line on what happened, **Reload**, **Export backup**, and the error message + component stack in a collapsed **Details**. Section-level fallback sits in the section's slot, so the header, nav and other sections keep working: **Reload this section** resets the boundary; navigating to another section remounts a fresh boundary (the `key`), and coming back to a still-broken section shows the fallback again. Both log `console.error` with the component stack.
  - **Decisions:** Export backup reads localStorage directly (the same `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS` list, not the stores), and a value that isn't valid JSON is exported as the raw string rather than aborting — a corrupt record is exactly when the backup is needed. The section-level fallback has no Export backup button (Account → Export is still reachable); the app-level one does. Modals/panes rendered at the App root (TaskPane, Settings, Account, …) are outside the section boundary and fall to the app-level one — a per-pane boundary was not added.
  - **Not covered (by nature):** React error boundaries don't catch errors in event handlers, async code or effects' promises; those still surface only in the console.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -b`, eslint, and a headless-Edge Playwright run (10 checks) with a temporary throw (removed afterwards; grep-confirmed): a Notes render throw shows "Notes hit an error" with the nav intact, Tasks works, the fallback disappears on navigating away and returns on coming back while still broken, "Reload this section" recovers once the fault is removed, `console.error` carries the boundary tag; a throw in `App` shows the full-page fallback, Export backup downloads a JSON containing the raw localStorage (seeded with a sentinel), Details shows the message. Screenshot of the section fallback reviewed in light theme. **Not verified:** dark theme and the Android layout of the fallback.

- [x] **`react-hooks` lint debt cleared: 35 × `set-state-in-effect` and 6 × `immutability`** (2026-09-20; agent brief 01, task 4). `npx eslint .` went from **82 errors + 10 warnings to 41 errors + 0 warnings** (all `set-state-in-effect`, `immutability` and `exhaustive-deps` findings gone). The pattern this established is written down in CLAUDE.md "Form state that starts from an item"; the retrofit entry is in BACKLOG.md "Pattern retrofit backlog".
  - **Lazy initialisers, effect deleted** (modals mounted only while open): `AddActivityModal`, `AddCollectionModal`, `AddEntryModal`, `AddListItemModal`, `AddListModal`, `AddPurposeModal`, `AddScheduleModal`, `AddTagModal`, `EditActivityTypeModal` (now mounted conditionally instead of always), `EditNoteTagModal`, `EditTrackerPane`, `EditRoutinePane`, `TaskPane`, `CalendarEventPane`, `CalendarReminderPane`, `MobileCalendarQuickAdd` (now mounted only while open). `AddNoteModal`: the preselected notebook is a lazy initialiser and the inherited Endeavour is *derived* (`pickedCollectionId` is `undefined` until the user picks — replaces a ref + effect). `EditNoteMetaModal` split into a wrapper + `EditNoteMetaForm`, mounted once the note view exists (an encrypted note's plaintext can arrive after the modal opens, which a lazy initialiser alone would miss). `FitnessSection`: the post-Strava-OAuth message comes from the URL in a lazy initialiser.
  - **Adjust-state-during-render**: `AddTaskModal` (advanced flag follows the store), `AddWatchlistItemModal` (a ticker lookup's autofill; the `nameAutoFilledRef` became state), `useItemActions` (dialog closes when the item changes), `TaskList` (expanded set resets on view-mode change), `NoteEditorPane` (re-reads the note on switch/lock), `FloatingToolbar` (resets pickers when it hides), `TimeInput` (segments follow `value` and the 12/24h format), `QuickAccessPane` (highlight clamp; a redundant mount-time reset removed), `ListsSection` (valid selected list, and tab fallback).
  - **`immutability`**: `AddNoteModal`, `AddNoteTagModal`, `BulkUploadWatchlistModal` moved to `useCtrlEnterSubmit` with function-declaration handlers; in `ListsSection` and `NoteEditor` the functions an effect calls (`handleSelectList`; `flushCurrentTab`/`switchTab`) were moved above the effect — the compiler rule still flags a *function declaration* used before its position.
  - **Call-site keys in `App.tsx`** so switching directly from item A to item B remounts the form: `TaskPane`, `CalendarEventPane`, `CalendarReminderPane`, `EditTrackerPane`, `EditRoutinePane`, `EditActivityTypeModal`, `AddCollectionModal`, `AddPurposeModal`, `AddTagModal`, `AddEntryModal`, `AddListModal`, `AddListItemModal`, `AddActivityModal`, `AddScheduleModal` (`AddWatchlistItemModal` already was); `NotesSection` keys `EditNoteTagModal`. `MobileMoreSheet` is now mounted only while open (its drag-offset reset was an effect).
  - **Kept, with a written reason (`eslint-disable-next-line … -- why`)**: `CalendarSidePane` (network fetch when the pane opens), `CalendarView` and `ListsSection` (consume a one-shot request another section posted to uiStore, then clear it), `TickerChart` (loading status mirrors a fetch), `NoteEditor` (reloads Tiptap, an external system, on lock/unlock). `react-hooks/refs` in `NoteEditor`/`ChronicleView` left for docs/agent-tasks/02, as the brief says.
  - **Bugs found:** with the old effects, switching straight from one event/reminder/task/tracker to another left the previous item's *repeat settings* (event/reminder panes) and half-typed inputs in place when the new item lacked them; the keyed remount fixes that as a side effect. No other behaviour was changed.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -b`, `npm run build`, eslint (counts above), and headless-Edge Playwright runs on the Vite dev server with real stores: **54 of 55 checks pass, and the one "failure" is a wrong regex in my check** (the input values it printed — 09 : 15, then 21 : 45 — are exactly right). Per form: create mode opens blank; edit mode shows the item's values; switching straight from A to B shows B and none of A — for the Endeavour, Purpose, Tag, Entry, List, List item, Schedule, Watchlist, Activity, Task, Event, Reminder, Tracker and Routine forms, the notebook (`EditNoteTagModal`) and note-details modals, and the note pane; Activity-type create/edit; Escape closes and Ctrl+Enter saves (Endeavour, Note, Notebook); a note created from an active notebook carries it; `showAddSubtask` while the task modal is open switches it to advanced; the delete dialog in an item pane closes when another task opens; deleting the selected list falls back to another; Quick Access with a no-result query; `TimeInput` follows an externally changed time; the Notes editor (open note, Ctrl+T adds a tab, select, collapse) and the Tasks Overview/Focused toggle throw no errors; `?strava=connected` shows its message and clears the param. (One trap while testing: after a file changes, Vite serves it under a `?t=` URL, so a `page.evaluate` `import('/src/…')` can return a *second copy* of a store — restart the dev server before scripted checks.)
  - **Not verified in a browser:** `MobileCalendarQuickAdd` and `MobileMoreSheet` (Android-only), `FloatingToolbar`'s hide-reset, `AddWatchlistItemModal`'s ticker autofill (needs the network lookup), `BulkUploadWatchlistModal`'s Ctrl+Enter, Ctrl+Enter/Escape on the tracker/routine/note-details panes individually, and the dark theme. Everything was type-checked.
- [x] **Calendar links, Complete button, pane Ctrl+Enter, TimeInput Enter** (2026-09-21).
  - **TimeInput Enter:** Enter in the hour or minute segment now accepts what's typed instead of doing nothing. Hour → commits (the blur handler pads a lone digit) and moves to the minutes; minute → commits, closes the quick-pick list and blurs. `preventDefault` stops a surrounding form (`AddCalendarItemModal`) submitting on it; Ctrl/Cmd+Enter is left alone so it still reaches the pane/modal submit. *Why it was broken:* in 12h mode a lone hour digit ("1") or a one-digit minute is only committed on blur, and Enter neither committed nor blurred.
  - **Ctrl+Enter in item panes:** `TaskPane`, `CalendarEventPane` and `CalendarReminderPane` never bound it (the rule in CLAUDE.md covered forms and Save-button panes, and these three have neither). `useItemActions` now calls `useCtrlEnterSubmit` to blur the focused field — which commits a half-typed title/note/time/link — and close the pane; it does nothing while the archive/delete dialog is open. Listed in `hotkeys.ts` as `task-save`.
  - **Complete button:** `ItemActionFooter` takes optional `completed` + `onToggleComplete` and renders a Complete / Mark incomplete toggle (`CheckCircleIcon`, green when done) first in the same row as Archive and Delete. `TaskPane` wires it to `toggleTask` and stays open (the button flips state) rather than closing. `CalendarEventPane` and `CalendarReminderPane` show it when the item belongs to a task (`Task.calendarEventId` / `Task.calendarReminderId`) and complete that task. The reminder pane previously had no link to its task at all, so it gained the same "Linked task" chip the event pane already had. Strings in `LABELS.itemActions`. No hotkey for completing. Every task edit pane is `TaskPane` (one component, mounted once in `App.tsx`), so this covers all entry points.
  - **Calendar links:** `CalendarEvent.links` / `CalendarReminder.links` (`string[]`, like `Task.links`). `calendarStore` `addEvent`/`addReminder`/`updateEvent`/`updateReminder` reuse `mergeNewLinks` from `utils/links.ts` — the function `taskStore` already uses — so a link typed into the notes is copied into the list, a link the user deleted isn't re-added by an unrelated later notes edit, and duplicates are dropped. `addEvent`/`addReminder` only extract from notes when the caller passed no `links`, so detaching/splitting a repeating series copies the list as-is. Store `v9 → v10` migration backfills `links: []`; mappers send/read `links`; migration `033_calendar_links.sql` adds the columns (**pending — not yet run**).
  - **Shared `LinksField`** (`src/components/LinksField/`): the links list UI (open / edit / delete / add) was extracted out of `TaskPane` and is now used by all three panes; its CSS moved with it out of `TaskPane.module.css`. Behaviour of the task pane's links is unchanged.
  - **Verified:** `tsc` clean and `npm run build` passes. **Not verified in a running app:** none of the four changes has been exercised in a browser or against Supabase — TimeInput Enter/Ctrl+Enter behaviour, the Complete toggle, links extraction from notes, and sync of `links` (which needs 033 run first).
  - **Not built:** links on the *Add* calendar modal (they're added by pasting into its notes, or in the pane afterwards); a keyboard shortcut for Complete; showing `links` on calendar chips/popovers.
- [x] **Note picker dialog, Links in the Add-calendar-item modal** (2026-09-21).
  - **Why:** the cross-app "+ Link" picker (`CrossAppRefPicker`, used by `TaskPane`, `CalendarEventPane`, `CalendarReminderPane`, `AddTaskModal`) was a 260px popover that listed notes by title only. Notes in different notebooks share titles, so you couldn't tell which one you were picking.
  - **What:** `CrossAppRefPicker` now opens `NotePickerModal` (`src/components/NotePickerModal/`), a centred dialog portaled to `document.body` (z-index 1000, above the panes and Add modals). Each result shows the title, the notebook path (`getNoteBreadcrumb`, e.g. "University > Exchanges"), the last-edited date and a two-line content preview. The search takes several words, all of which must match somewhere in the title, path or content (ranked title > path > content, then recency), and highlights the matches; the preview jumps to the first content match. With no query it lists notes by most recently viewed. ↑/↓ moves, Enter or Ctrl+Enter links, Esc closes; already-linked and archived notes are hidden; encrypted notes are searchable by path only and show a 🔒 (their content is never read). Chips in the picker also show the notebook (last path segment, full path on hover).
  - **Keyword suggestions (2026-09-21):** with the search box empty, the picker ranks notes by the keywords of the title of the item being linked from (`CrossAppRefPicker`'s `suggestFrom`: task / event / reminder title, or the title typed so far in `AddTaskModal`) under a "Suggested from …" heading, with the recent list below. The first keystroke replaces them with the typed search; with no title, or no hits, it is just the recent list. `extractKeywords()` (`utils/keywords.ts`) lowercases, drops stop-words, short words and bare numbers, and keeps up to 8; `stemForMatch()` strips a trailing s/ing/ed for matching only. Each keyword is looked up in every note's title, notebook path and content and weighted by rarity across the user's notes (IDF), so a distinctive word outranks a common one; hits in the title count most, then path, then content (log-scaled and capped so a long note can't win on volume). **Cost:** nothing is indexed; per-note plain text is cached for the session (`utils/noteSearchText.ts`, keyed on `updatedAt` + content length, capped at 12,000 chars per note, never holding encrypted notes' text), and the scoring is a substring scan of it. Not measured on a large notes set.
  - **Ctrl+Enter:** the dialog binds it in the capture phase with `stopImmediatePropagation`, otherwise the pane behind it (which now closes on Ctrl+Enter via `useItemActions`) or an Add modal would answer it too.
  - **Files/helpers:** `noteContentToText()` in `utils/noteContent.ts` (plain text of stored Tiptap JSON, capped at 4000 chars); `getNoteBreadcrumb()` now prefers notebook (`kind='area'`) tags over annotation tags, since only notebooks are a location — this also corrects `StructuredTagPopover`'s Location line; Quick Access note results now read "Note · <notebook path>" instead of just "Note".
  - **Links in `AddCalendarItemModal`:** a Links field (the shared `LinksField`) under "More options" for both events and reminders (reminders always show that section). Submitted through `mergeNewLinks(links, notes)`, so links pasted into the notes are still picked up. `LinksField`'s edit input now calls `preventDefault` on Enter so it can't submit the surrounding form.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -b` and eslint on the touched files are clean. **Not verified in a running app:** the dialog's look, keyboard flow and stacking over each pane/modal, and search ranking on a real notes set.
  - **Not built:** picking other target types (Calendar item, List item, Tracker entry — still BACKLOG); filtering the picker by notebook or Endeavour; a return link from the note back to a task/event (proposed, awaiting a decision).
- [x] **Task ⇄ calendar shadow entries now kept in sync by one service** (2026-09-21). A task's deadline has a shadow `CalendarReminder` (`reminderType 'task'`, never rendered — the deadline pill is drawn from the task) and its scheduled date a shadow `CalendarEvent` (`eventType 'task'`, rendered as a real event because a scheduled task blocks time and needs event-only fields: end time, location, tentative…). That logic used to be copied by hand in `AddTaskModal`, `TaskPane` and `MobileQuickAddBar`, and nothing propagated a *scheduled* task's title, or edits made on its event, to the other side (a deadline never showed this because its calendar pill *is* the task). Now `src/services/taskCalendarLinks.ts` owns it: `syncScheduledShadow`/`syncDeadlineShadow`/`syncTaskShadows(taskId)` (create, update or delete the shadow to match the task; a linked id whose entry no longer exists counts as "no shadow"), `addTaskWithCalendar(input)`, `updateTaskLinked(taskId, changes)` (re-syncs when title/deadline/deadlineTime/scheduledAt/scheduledTime change), and `updateCalendarEventLinked(eventId, changes)` (title/date/startTime edited on a task-linked event flow back to the task; end time, location etc. stay on the event). **The task is the source of truth for the shared fields; both sides are editable.**
  - **Callers** use these instead of touching the shadows themselves: `AddTaskModal`, `MobileQuickAddBar`, `TaskPane`, and `CalendarEventPane` (which imports `updateCalendarEventLinked` as `updateEvent`). Any new code that changes a task's title or dates, or edits a task-linked event, must do the same.
  - On a task-linked event `CalendarEventPane` hides the **Repeat** control and the **Event type** picker (the task doesn't repeat; changing the type would break the calendar layer).
  - `deleteEventWithCleanup` on a task's scheduled event now **un-schedules the task** (clears `scheduledAt`/`scheduledTime`/`calendarEventId`; the task stays) instead of leaving a dangling `calendarEventId`.
  - `taskCalendarBackfill` still only creates what's missing (via `syncDeadlineShadow`) and re-tags `eventType`; it never rewrites an existing shadow, so a legacy task whose event was deleted or edited on its own isn't silently reverted.
  - **Verified:** `tsc -b`, `npm run build`, and a Node harness against the real stores (16 checks: create, rename both ways, date/time both ways, event-only fields stay put, clearing a deadline, delete-un-schedules, dangling-id repair, idempotence). **Not verified:** the UI itself.
  - **Not built:** the deadline's reminder shadow has no edit surface of its own, so it has no reverse path.
- [x] **Linked from bar: return links in a note, drag-in, remove-link prompt** (2026-09-21).
  - **Why:** a link made from a task/event/reminder (`CrossAppRefPicker`) only recorded the reverse ref on that item; the note itself showed nothing and gave no way back. (Notes-side links have a mark in the text, but that was the only place a link was visible.)
  - **Linked from bar** (`NoteEditor/NoteBacklinks.tsx`, between the title bar and the tab bar; hidden when there are none): every task / event / reminder whose `crossAppRefs` names the note, whichever side made the link. **Derived, not stored** — `useNoteBacklinks()` / `getNoteBacklinks()` in `store/noteBacklinks.ts` scan the task and calendar stores, so there is no new data or migration and deleting/unlinking on the other side updates it for free; archived items are left out. One link shows as a single pill; two or more collapse into a "🔗 N" chip that opens a list of pills. A pill opens its item (`services/openCrossAppTarget.ts`, now also used by clicking linked text in the editor, replacing that inline code), shows a completed task struck through, and has a ⤵ **Insert at cursor** button — the touch/keyboard route. *(2026-09-21)* Opening the chip's list doesn't take focus from the note (the chip ignores mousedown), because the editor's shortcuts — Ctrl+Tab tab cycling, Ctrl+T — only run while the editor is focused; and any other key pressed while the list is open closes it and still does its normal job (the listener only observes, in the capture phase). Keys used to move around inside the list itself (plain Tab/Enter/arrows while focus is on it) are left alone for keyboard users, and bare modifier presses and Escape are ignored. Verified in headless Edge (10/10): Ctrl+Tab switched tabs and closed the list, typing closed it and still reached the note, Tab inside a keyboard-opened list left it open, Escape closed it.
  - **Drag a pill into the text** (mouse; `artifactLinkInsert.ts`, `handleDrop` in the editor): it becomes the item's title as text with the existing `artifactLink` mark plus a trailing plain space — exactly what highlighting text and linking it makes (click opens the item, Ctrl+click selects it, "Remove link" clears it). The pill stays in the bar afterwards, unstyled (decided: no "placed" indicator). The inserted text is a snapshot of the title, so it isn't renamed if the item is later (accepted; noted in BACKLOG.md). Touch-drag is not built (HTML5 drag from touch is unreliable across WebViews); Insert at cursor covers it.
  - **Remove-link prompt (a rule change):** `FloatingToolbar`'s "Remove link" used to also strip the reverse ref from the task, so with pills it would have silently severed the whole connection. It now only unmarks the text. When the last text linking a note to an item disappears — by that button, or by deleting the text — `NoteEditor` asks "Remove the link too?" (`confirmDialog`; **Enter/Ctrl+Enter = Remove link entirely** via `removeCrossAppRefFromTarget`, **Keep link** = leave it in the bar). *Revised 2026-09-21:* the dialog appears **immediately**, but with `focusDelayMs: 1200` it takes no keyboard focus and ignores Ctrl+Enter for 1.2 s, so focus stays in the editor and a stray Enter while typing goes to the note, not the dialog; when the delay ends it takes focus, unless `isStale()` says the reason is gone (the text came back — e.g. cut and pasted elsewhere — or the link was removed another way), in which case it closes itself as "keep". (An earlier version debounced the *check* by 1.2 s instead; dropped as laggy.) Details that matter: only the ranges each transaction touched are scanned (cheap per keystroke); the check runs against the stored note — main content and every tab — so text remaining anywhere means no prompt; a key already being asked about isn't asked twice; undo/redo transactions never trigger it; loading/switching content is ignored via `isLoadingRef`; pending checks are dropped when the open note changes.
  - **Verified** in headless Edge against the Vite dev server with the real stores (15/15): single pill; insert at cursor; no dialog inside the idle window, dialog after it; Enter removes the link and the pill goes; Keep leaves the connection and the pill; HTML5 drag-drop of a pill inserts linked text; undoing the insertion doesn't prompt; several links show the "🔗 N" chip and its list. `tsc -b` and `npm run build` pass; eslint on `NoteEditor.tsx` reports the same 8 errors as before (pre-existing). **Not verified:** partial deletion of linked text (relies on the mark surviving, reasoned not run), links whose text sits in a non-active tab, Android/touch, encrypted notes, and the visual polish of the popover list.
  - **Not built:** tab targeting (a link naming a specific note tab) — next, see BACKLOG.md; touch-drag.
- [x] **Tab targeting, per-tab pills, hover ✕, focus-delayed prompt** (2026-09-21).
  - **A link can name a tab of the note.** `CrossAppRef.tabId?` (`'__main__'` or a `NoteTab` id; absent = the whole note — every older link, and any link to a note with no extra tabs). It lives inside the existing `crossAppRefs` JSON column, so **no SQL migration and no store version bump**. Decisions: **one link per item per note** (the (type,id) duplicate checks everywhere stay as they were; to point at another tab, remove and re-add); a link to a tab that has since been deleted falls back to the main tab (`effectiveLinkTabId`); a note without extra tabs gets no `tabId`, so its links keep meaning "the whole note" if tabs are added later. Helpers in `utils/noteTabs.ts` (`MAIN_TAB_ID`, `linkTabIdFor`, `effectiveLinkTabId`, `tabNameOf`).
  - **Where the tab is recorded / used:** a link created from a Notes selection records the tab the selection was in (`FloatingToolbar`'s `getLinkTabId` → `uiStore.pendingArtifactLink.tabId` → `AddTaskModal` / `AddCalendarItemModal`). `uiStore.openNote(id, tabId?)` sets a one-shot `requestedNoteTab`, which `NoteEditor` consumes (declared after the note-load effect, so a note switch has already loaded the main tab) — the Task/Event/Reminder panes pass `ref.tabId` when a linked-note chip is clicked. The picker's chips show "Note › Tab".
  - **Picker (`NotePickerModal`):** a note with extra tabs shows its tabs as chips under the result; the search covers tab names and each tab's own text (`getNoteTabTexts` in `utils/noteSearchText.ts`, cached per note+tab, replacing `getNoteSearchText`), and the **best-matching tab is pre-selected** — for typed searches and for the title-keyword suggestions alike (scored per tab; the note ranks by its best tab). Click a chip to link that tab, Tab / Shift+Tab cycles the highlighted note's tab, Enter links the selected one. A note without extra tabs is linked as a whole.
  - **Per-tab pills:** `NoteBacklinks` shows, on the open tab, the links to that tab plus whole-note links; links to the note's *other* tabs count on the chip ("🔗 1 +2", or "🔗 2 on other tabs") and are listed under "On other tabs" in its popover with a "› Tab" button that switches to that tab. Other-tab pills can't be dragged in or inserted (they belong to another tab) but can still be opened and removed.
  - **Hover ✕ on a pill:** a red ✕ appears on hover (always visible on touch screens, `@media (hover: none)`) and removes the link altogether, no confirmation: the item forgets the note (`unlinkCrossAppRef`, which also strips the marks from the stored note) and the live editor's marks for that item are removed too (`removeArtifactMarksFor`; the words stay). Ref first, so the removed-link prompt finds nothing to ask.
  - **Dialog focus delay:** `confirmDialog` gained `focusDelayMs` and `isStale` (`dialogStore.DialogRequest`); see the revised remove-link prompt above. Any other dialog raised while the user is typing could use it.
  - **Verified** in headless Edge with the real stores (24/24): dialog immediate but unfocused during the delay; a stray Enter didn't answer it; it took focus after the delay and Enter removed the link; cut + paste of the linked text within the delay dismissed it and left the link intact; ✕ hidden until hover, shown on hover, removed the link and the mark and raised no prompt; main-tab bar counted whole-note vs other-tab links; popover listed other-tab pills with tab names; the jump switched tabs; `openNote(id, tabId)` opened the right tab; the picker pre-selected the best tab from title keywords and a chip click recorded `tabId`; the picker chip shows "› Visa". `tsc -b` and `npm run build` pass; `NoteEditor.tsx` lint is at its pre-existing 8 errors. **Not verified:** Tab-key cycling in the picker (the test was too loose to count), Shift+Tab, notes with many tabs or long tab names, encrypted notes with tabs, deleting a tab that a link names, Android/touch.
  - **Not built:** touch-drag of a pill; linking two different tabs of one note from the same item.
- [x] **Storage-full guard, image compression, Settings → Storage** (2026-09-21).
  - **Bug reported:** `QuotaExceededError: Setting the value of 'notes-storage' exceeded the quota` at `touchNote`, which crashed the whole Notes section through the error boundary. **Cause:** every persisted store writes to localStorage (about 5 MB for the whole site, shared), notes hold pasted images inline as base64 (`ResizableImage` is configured `allowBase64: true`), and zustand's `persist` calls `setItem` synchronously inside the store's `set` — so the exception escapes the action that happened to be running. Opening a note is just the most frequent write (`touchNote` rewrites `lastViewedAt`, re-serialising *all* notes).
  - **Guard (`utils/persistStorage.ts`):** `persistStorage()` is now the `storage:` of all 14 persisted stores. It catches only the quota error (`QuotaExceededError` / Firefox's `NS_ERROR_DOM_QUOTA_REACHED` / legacy codes 22 and 1014), logs it, and shows an in-app alert — naming the biggest store ("Notes use 4.2 MB of it"), saying changes aren't being saved but stay in memory and still sync if signed in, and pointing at Settings → Storage. Re-shown every 10 minutes while writes keep failing. Everything else still throws. Decision: guard *all* stores, not just notes — a full origin fails whichever store writes next (the session store writes on every navigation).
  - **Prevention (`utils/imageCompress.ts`):** an image pasted into a note is scaled to fit 1600 px and re-encoded as WebP (quality 0.82) before it is stored; small images (≤150 KB), GIFs and SVGs are left alone, and the original is kept if the result wouldn't be at least 10% smaller. Browsers that can't encode WebP fall back to PNG, which is then only used if smaller.
  - **Recovery (`services/shrinkNoteImages.ts`, Settings → Storage → "Shrink images"):** recompresses every inline image over ~110 KB in every note and tab, through `updateNote` / `updateNoteTabContent` (so encrypted notes re-encrypt); locked encrypted notes are skipped; the open note is closed first, because the editor would otherwise autosave its own large copy back over the result. The same section lists the biggest stores (`getStorageUsage()`, sizes counted in characters, approximate).
  - **Verified** in headless Edge on the Vite dev server: with localStorage filled to the brim, adding and opening a note no longer hits the error boundary, the editor renders, and the alert appears naming the store and the remedy; a pasted 2400 px noise image (worst case for compression) went from 12.0M to 2.0M characters at 1600 px WebP; the shrink button reported "Shrank 2 images in 1 note, freeing about 8.9 MB" for one image in a note's main tab and one in another tab, text untouched, open note closed, result persisted (13/13 checks). `tsc -b` clean. **Not verified:** real photos (noise compresses far worse than photos, so real gains should be larger), Firefox/Safari quota error names, the Tauri and Android webviews' storage limits (they may differ from a browser tab), and how many images the user's own notes actually hold — their data wasn't available to look at.
  - **Not built:** images as separate blobs; a lasting "not saving" indicator beyond the alert. *(Moving notes to IndexedDB was built the next day — see "Notes in IndexedDB".)*
- [x] **Notes in IndexedDB** (2026-09-22).
  - **Why:** the follow-up to the storage-full bug — localStorage is ~5 MB for the whole site in browsers, in Tauri's WebView2 and in Android's WebView alike (so the built desktop app does not escape it; a separate origin only means dev and production data are separate). IndexedDB allows hundreds of MB and works the same on all three, so one code path serves every platform (a Tauri-only file store was rejected: it would double the code).
  - **How (`utils/idbStorage.ts`, `main.tsx`):** zustand's `persist` stays synchronous, so nothing else changed. `main.tsx` runs `start()`: `await preloadIdbStorage()` (open database `organisaitor`/store `kv`, load `notes-storage` into an in-memory map, migrate any localStorage copy — written to IndexedDB first, removed from localStorage only after that succeeds; a stale localStorage copy is discarded if the database already has one; asks the browser for `navigator.storage.persist()`), and only then dynamically imports `App` and `ErrorBoundary`. `noteStore` uses `storage: persistStorageIdb()`. `getItem` reads the map; `setItem` updates it and queues a background write (write-behind, one transaction at a time, latest value wins, resolved on transaction completion so a write is durable). Rejected: zustand's async storage — hydration would then complete after first render, opening a window where a sync pull or an empty initial state could be persisted over the real notes. `main.tsx` uses an async function, not top-level `await`, because the default build target (es2020) rejects TLA — found when the production build failed.
  - **Safety:** using the storage before the preload finishes throws (loud, by design — the alternative is silently persisting an empty state); if IndexedDB can't be opened the key stays on the guarded localStorage as before; a failed background write raises the same alert as a full localStorage (`reportPersistFailure`).
  - **Backup / restore:** `downloadBackup()` is now async and reads through `readPersistedValue()` (still works when the app can't render — falls back to reading the database directly); `AccountPane` and `IntegrationsPane` restore through `writePersistedValue()`, which stores to IndexedDB and freezes further IndexedDB writes until the reload restore always does, so the running app can't overwrite the restored data. Backup files are unchanged in format.
  - **Settings → Storage** lists the notes database separately ("Notes (database)"); the storage-full alert now points at Settings → Storage generally rather than at notes.
  - **Verified** in headless Edge (dev server, 18/18): notes not in localStorage but in IndexedDB and surviving reload; two ~10 MB notes (20M characters) saved and reloaded with localStorage full and no alert; a legacy localStorage copy migrated, its key removed, still there after another reload; backup contains the notes; a restore took effect and a store write racing it was dropped; with `indexedDB.open` throwing, the app rendered, notes fell back to localStorage and survived reload. Production bundle (`vite build` + `vite preview`): renders with no ordering error, database created, a note created through the real UI (`N`) is in IndexedDB and survives a reload. `tsc -b` and eslint clean on the touched files.
  - **Not verified:** the Tauri desktop build and the Android WebView (IndexedDB should behave as in Chromium — not tested); Firefox/Safari; two tabs open at once (each holds its own in-memory copy and writes the whole store, so the last writer wins — localStorage had the same property); a very large real-world notes store's write time (each save writes the whole JSON string). **Not built:** per-note records, images as blobs, moving other stores.
- [x] **Sync that survives offline: pending-changes queue, reconcile after load, retries** (2026-09-22).
  - **Why:** reading the sync code (asked by the user: "do I need to force upload to close the app safely?") showed pushes were fire-and-forget. A change made offline, or whose request failed, was never retried; an item edited or created offline was kept locally by the load's merge but never uploaded (only "remote is completely empty" uploaded everything); a deletion made offline was undone by the next load (the cloud row was still alive, so the merge restored it); and a request in flight when the window closed was lost.
  - **What (`syncService.ts`):** (1) a **pending-changes queue** — `watch()`/`trackChanges()` record each changed/deleted row id (`markDirty`) in localStorage `todo-sync-pending` before sending, and `pushIds()` sends the row's current state or a soft-delete and forgets the entry only on success (an entry re-marked while its request was in flight is kept, via a per-entry sequence number); (2) **retries** — `flushPending()` on `online`, every 60 s while anything waits, and after each load; status shows `error … will retry (N changes waiting)` and returns to `idle` when the queue empties; (3) **reconcile after the merge** — `queueLocalOnlyAndNewer()` queues local-only rows and rows whose local `updatedAt` is newer than the cloud's `updated_at`, for tables that loaded; (4) **no resurrection** — `mergeRecords()` skips a live remote row whose id is pending-deleted here; (5) `TABLE_DEFS` registry replaces the seven hand-written subscriptions and `syncDiff` (behaviour unchanged for online use: still an immediate push per change, batched per table); (6) `forceUpload` also flushes pending soft-deletes; (7) `authStore.signOut` clears the queue after the wipe, and a queue belonging to another account is discarded on load (from disk too).
  - **Also:** `NoteEditor` flushes its pending autosave on `pagehide` / tab-hidden — closing the window used to lose the last ~1.5 s of typing, which never reached the store, the local database or the cloud.
  - **Decisions:** the queue holds ids, not payloads, so several offline edits collapse into one push of the latest state; last-write-wins on `updatedAt` stays (no field-level merge); a remote tombstone still beats a local edit (unchanged); the queue is not backed up (per-device bookkeeping — a restored copy would replay stale changes). Rejected: a periodic full re-pull (heavy — every load already downloads every row) and realtime subscriptions (out of scope; another device's changes still arrive only on its next load).
  - **Verified** in headless Edge against a simulated Supabase (requests intercepted with an in-memory PostgREST fake that can be switched offline; 22/22): a task created offline is recorded on disk, absent from the cloud, uploaded on the next launch even though the cloud wasn't empty; a task deleted offline stays deleted after relaunch and the cloud row gets its deletion marker while the other task is untouched; a task edited offline keeps its edit through the merge and reaches the cloud; a change made offline is pushed when the browser fires `online` (no restart) and status returns to `idle`; relaunching a fully in-sync app performs no writes; an online change is pushed immediately and leaves nothing pending; another account's queue is reset (on disk) and `clearPendingSync()` removes it. Regressions: link/tab suite 24/24, list-popover 10/10, IndexedDB 18/18. `tsc -b` clean, `npm run build` passes, `NoteEditor.tsx` lint unchanged (8, pre-existing). **Not verified against the real Supabase project** (no test credentials/session in the harness — the fake models `select`, `upsert` and soft-delete `update` only); per-table RLS/permission failures; two devices editing at once; the first launch after upgrading with a large pre-existing offline backlog.
  - **Not built / known gaps:** local *deletions* made in the moment between opening the app and the load finishing (subscriptions are torn down and rebuilt around the fetch) are not tracked, so such a deletion can be undone by that load — edits and creations in that window are covered by the reconcile; no realtime or periodic pull; no conflict UI; tables without `updatedAt` can't detect an offline edit; a note with large inline images still sends the whole row on every autosave.
- [x] **AI agent command layer — Chunk A (tasks, calendar, schedules, Endeavours/Purposes/Tags)** (2026-09-22). The tool surface an AI agent will use, built and tested but **not connected to any model**: nothing in the app calls it except a dev-only console handle. Design and how to add a command are in `docs/ai/02-command-layer.md`; the reasoning, the invariants register and the remaining phases are in `docs/ai/01-capability-inventory.md`. Adds `zod` (input schemas, also emitted as JSON Schema) and `vitest` (`npm test`).
  - **What it is:** 22 commands (8 read, 6 create, 6 modify, 2 archive) behind `runCommand(name, input, opts)`, which validates, enforces the risk tier and an approval policy (a command can return a *proposal* instead of running — `create_schedule` always does, `update_schedule` when it removes blocks or adds more than 3), runs the handler inside a snapshot, records an audit-log entry, and returns a result. Everything an agent changes is captured as a batch that `revertBatch` can undo.
  - **The boundary:** commands import only `src/agent/access.ts` (`read` / `write`), enforced by an ESLint rule scoped to `src/agent/commands/**` and proven by `boundary.test.ts`. `write` has no delete: agents archive. See "Agent command layer" in CLAUDE.md for the standing rules.
  - **Files:** `src/agent/` (`access.ts`, `types.ts`, `run.ts`, `registry.ts`, `batch.ts`, `errors.ts`, `devHandle.ts`, `commands/{read,tasks,calendar,schedules,organisation,shared,index}.ts`), `src/types/agent.ts`, stores `agentLogStore` (`agent-log`) and `agentBatchStore` (`agent-batches`) — both local-only, added to `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS`, labelled in `LABELS.storage.keyNames`, and cleared on sign-out in `clearSyncedLocalData`.
  - **Extracted so the UI and the agent share one rule (no visible UI change — pure refactors):** `utils/calendarItemInput.ts` (`buildCalendarEventInput`/`buildCalendarReminderInput` — empty→null, end date only if after start, birthday = no times + yearly repeat, links typed in notes copied to links; now used by `AddCalendarItemModal`), `utils/scheduleBlocks.ts` (`createScheduleBlock` — now used by `AddScheduleModal`), `scheduleStore.addSchedule` accepts `blocks` (removing the modal's add-then-update two-step), `taskStore.addCollection`/`addPurpose` return the new id, and sub-task inheritance of priority/Endeavour moved into `addTaskWithCalendar` (used by `TaskPane`'s quick sub-task and the agent).
  - **Decisions:** the executor is always the client (agents call the same store actions as the UI); one boundary module rather than a filter on outputs, so hiding encrypted content and forbidding delete are properties of the code, not checks that can be forgotten; snapshots are kept references (Zustand state is immutable) so no command declares what it touches; revert leaves anything the user edited since alone and reports it; batches merge when callers share a `batchId`; tools are per-intent (~22) rather than per-store-action. Rejected: a separate "agent login" (both would run in the same process with the same in-memory vault key, so it would be the same boundary again); an MCP server (not needed while the agent lives inside the app).
  - **Verified:** `tsc -b`, `npm run build`, and 128 passing Vitest tests (`npm test`) covering every command against the real stores, validation, tier gating, review, rollback, the read-only guard, audit log, undo (incl. edited-since, shared batches, revert-once), the lint boundary, and the extracted shared logic. No new lint errors (the repo's existing 43 are untouched).
  - **Not verified:** the extracted UI paths in a running app (`AddCalendarItemModal` create for event/reminder/birthday, `AddScheduleModal` create, `TaskPane` quick sub-task) — checked by types and unit tests of the shared functions only; the dev handle; persistence of the two new stores in real browser storage; the generated JSON Schemas against a real model provider.
  - **Not built:** a model/chat UI, the review screen for proposals, the agent manual and evals, notes commands (Chunk B: headless note-content module, the editor reloading on external change, encrypted content excluded at `access.ts`), Lists/Records/Fitness/Portfolio commands, and the small synced table for cross-device "created by the agent" attribution (needs a migration). Tracked in `docs/ai/01-capability-inventory.md` and BACKLOG.md "AI Agent Integration".
- [x] **Automated test suite — Phase 1 (pure logic), Phase 5 (pattern tests), and Phase 2 (stores/sync/encryption/edge functions)** (2026-09-24). Brief: `docs/agent-tasks/02-testing-and-engineering-hygiene.md` (status kept current there — Phase 3/4 and Part B are explicitly not done yet). Adds dev dependencies `jsdom`, `@testing-library/react`, `@testing-library/user-event`, `@testing-library/jest-dom`, `fake-indexeddb`, `@vitest/coverage-v8`.
  - **What and why:** the app had no automated tests outside Chunk A's agent-layer suite (128 tests); this session added 253 more (381 total), all against real modules/stores, none mocked beyond what Chunk A's harness already provides.
  - **Phase 1 — pure logic (11 new files, ~240 tests):** `src/utils/{timezone,date,recurrence,scheduleOccurrences,textToTask,icsParser,googleReminders,links,acronymInference,hotkeyBinding,timeGrid,collections,noteContent}.test.ts`. Covers the "known past bug shapes" the brief calls out (DST round-trips, midnight-wrap `dayOffset`, RRULE + EXDATE, TZID case preservation, ambiguous-date skipping, time-range parsing, `mergeNewLinks` never resurrecting a removed link, hotkey binding round-trip). Several tests needed the *actual* regex/Date behavior verified with a throwaway Node script before being written correctly — three genuinely surprising cases now documented in the tests themselves: `acronymInference`'s "Expansion (TERM)" capture runs back to the last punctuation, not just the nearest phrase; a yearly repeat from a leap day permanently drifts to March 1 and never returns to Feb 29 (JS `Date.setFullYear` behavior); ICS line-folding removes the fold's own leading space as syntax, so the source content needs its own space to end up with one. Not covered from the brief's Phase 1 list: `utils/notes.ts`, `utils/quickAccess.ts`, `config/*` template-validity checks (config id-uniqueness is covered instead by the Phase 5 pattern test).
  - **Phase 5 — pattern governance as code (`src/test/patterns.test.ts`, 16 checks):** native popups; persisted-store name/version registration; Escape handling (only `useEscapeClose.ts`/`hotkeyBinding.ts`/documented inline `stopPropagation` handlers, plus SettingsPane's capture-phase and NoteBacklinks' deliberate pass-through exceptions); every `styles.overlay` component calls `useEscapeClose` (directly or via `useItemActions`); every `Add*Modal`/`Edit*Modal`/`Edit*Pane` binds Ctrl+Enter; no hard-coded "Endeavour" string literal outside `labels.ts` (see finding below); no raw `type="time"` outside `TimeInput`; no raw `fetch('/api`; `hotkeys.ts` id uniqueness and `customizable` dispatch coverage in `App.tsx`; `SYNC_TABLES` vs. the `runInitSync` fetch list (same order); every table-creating migration grants (or is covered by 022's documented bulk exception) and is listed in CLAUDE.md; `taskStore`/`noteStore` never import each other; no constant inline styles (`NoteEditor.tsx`'s six documented exceptions aside). Not implemented: CSS-colour-variable audit (never had a defined check to encode) and the unused-exports check (needs `ts-prune` or similar, not installed).
  - **Finding, not fixed here:** the Endeavour-terminology check found real drift — `src/agent/commands/{organisation,read,shared,tasks}.ts` hard-code "Endeavour" in tool descriptions and error messages, added by Chunk A after the 2026-09-20 audit. Deliberately left as a documented exemption in the test (pointing at BACKLOG.md) rather than fixed, since it's AI tool-schema/error text and brief `03`'s own rules require discussing agent-facing wording changes with the user first.
  - **Bug found and fixed:** `taskStore`'s v11 `migrate` had each `if (fromVersion < N)` step `return` immediately after applying its own patch. `migrate` runs once per load with whatever version is on disk, so a store that skipped several app versions in one load (not opened in a while) only ever got the *first* applicable step's fields — everything from later steps (scheduledAt/scheduledTime/calendarEventId, collections' `collectionId`, `archivedAt`, `calendarReminderId`, `crossAppRefs`) silently stayed missing. Fixed by making every step reassign `state` and fall through instead of returning, so a multi-version jump genuinely accumulates every step (`src/store/taskStore.ts`; regression test in `src/store/migrations.test.ts`'s "v2 -> v11" fixture). CLAUDE.md's "Zustand migration rule" updated to describe the fixed (and now-correct) behavior. No SQL/schema change — this only affects how already-broken client-side JSON gets backfilled on load, so nothing to run in Supabase.
  - **Store migrations tested (`src/store/migrations.test.ts`):** `settingsStore` v1→v2 (`calendarLayerVisibility.tentative` backfill — the brief's called-out "or tentative events silently vanish" case) and v0→v2 (unversioned legacy fixture); `scheduleStore` v0→v1 (`requiresCommitment`/`committedDates` backfill, and that an already-migrated block's values are left alone); `taskStore` v9→v11 and the v2→v11 regression above. **Descoped by the user 2026-09-24** for the remaining six stores — the app hasn't shipped, so there's no installed base of old-version localStorage worth protecting; these three are kept only because they're free and one caught a real bug.
  - **Mapper round-trips (`src/services/sync/mappers.test.ts`):** all 17 synced entity types (`Task`, `Collection`, `TrackerEntry`, `Tag`, `Purpose`, `CalendarEvent`, `CalendarReminder`, `ScheduleTemplate`, `List`, `ListItem`, `ListType`, `Note`, `NoteTag`, `StructuredTagEntry`, `WatchlistItem`, `PortfolioTag`, `InvestmentPurpose`) — `rowToX(xToRow(entity))` equals the original (plus `userId` where `rowToNote`/`rowToNoteTag` reconstruct it). All passed on the first run — a clean baseline, not a finding.

  - **Sync pipeline, added 2026-09-24 (`src/services/sync/syncService.test.ts`):** since `mergeRecords`/`hydrateStores`/`upsertAllToSupabase` aren't exported, tested through the public API (`initSync`/`forceUpload`) against a hand-rolled fake Supabase client instead — proves the pipeline actually behaves, not just a helper in isolation, and needed no source changes. Covers: `mergeRecords` (a remote tombstone deletes local; the newer `updatedAt` wins either direction; a local-only record is left alone; `Tag`'s no-`updatedAt` remote-wins-but-tombstone-aware fallback); per-table failure isolation (one table erroring still lets a live remote row on another table hydrate in, and suppresses the "remote looks empty → push local up" branch even when every other table genuinely is empty); the `enqueue()` mutex (`forceUpload` fired immediately after `initSync`, without awaiting it first, still only ever sees post-hydration state); `customListTypes` (a force-upload only ever sends the non-built-in list types).
  - **Encryption harnesses, added 2026-09-24** — permanent ports of the two Node harnesses CLAUDE.md references: `src/services/vault.test.ts` (20 checks; setup/unlock/lock/recovery-code round-trip case/whitespace-insensitive, `verifyVaultSecret` never changing lock state, `lockVault`'s before-lock hooks flush-then-settle ordering and throw-tolerance, `encryptField`/`decryptField`'s fresh-IV-per-call and locked-vault rejection, every `checkVaultStatus` branch including a failed read surfacing as `unavailable` with the error recorded) against **real** Web Crypto (Node 22's native `crypto.subtle`/`btoa`/`atob`; `fake-indexeddb/auto` polyfills the trusted-device cache); `src/services/noteSecrets.test.ts` (15 checks; `@/services/vault` faked here so the queue's ordering guarantee is deterministic instead of racing real crypto) — the lock/view/cache mechanism, and `queueEncrypt`'s core guarantee that a newer edit arriving mid-encryption supersedes the one in flight, proven with **25 rapid edits to the same key ending on the last one delivered, not the first** (the exact regression shape CLAUDE.md names); `src/services/listSecrets.test.ts` (6 checks, the Lists twin of the same mechanism).

All mock only `./_lib/supabaseEdge` (keeping the real `bearerToken`) and, where relevant, `fetch`/the provider `_lib` module — never the endpoint's own logic.
  - **A real, pre-existing, unrelated gap found while wiring this up:** `api/*.ts` had never been type-checked by any tsconfig at all (`tsconfig.app.json` only includes `src`; Vercel type-checks edge functions separately at deploy time). Adding the new `api/**/*.test.ts` files to `tsconfig.node.json`'s `include` (the same pattern already used for `boundary.test.ts`/`patterns.test.ts`) pulled the corresponding sources in transitively and surfaced 5 genuine errors — `res.json()` typed as `unknown` under the Node-only (no-DOM) lib config, unlike `tsconfig.app.json`'s DOM lib — in `api/_lib/googleCalendar.ts` (×4), `api/_lib/strava.ts`, `api/speech-recognize.ts`, `api/strava-sync.ts`. Fixed with a one-line result-type cast at each `res.json()`/`req.json()` call; no behaviour change. Only the new test files were added to `tsconfig.node.json`, not all of `api/**/*.ts` — deliberately, so this pass doesn't have to also chase down every other latent type error across the edge functions it didn't otherwise touch.
  - **A `tsc -b` false alarm worth remembering:** while iterating on the `tsconfig.node.json` change above, `tsc -b --force` (and, separately, a plain `tsc -b` run shortly after other files changed) briefly reported spurious `Cannot find name`/`is declared but never used` errors in `ChronicleView.tsx`/`ListsSection.tsx` that don't exist — confirmed both times by running `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` directly (clean) and by deleting `node_modules/.tmp/*.tsbuildinfo` and re-running `tsc -b` (also clean). Stale incremental build cache after a referenced project's config changes, not a real bug — CI always builds from a clean checkout so it never hits this, but a local `tsc -b` right after editing a `tsconfig*.json` is worth a cache-clear if it reports something that looks wrong.

Re-verified everything above only after it finished; see `docs/agent-tasks/02-testing-and-engineering-hygiene.md`'s status block for the detail.
