- [x] **Suite-wide Recycling Bin** (2026-09-24). The user deleted the wrong item and discovered "delete" is permanent from the app's own point of view — the only trace was a `deleted_at` tombstone set on the Supabase row purely for cross-device sync propagation (see `013_soft_delete.sql`), invisible in the UI and never read back. This makes deleting actually recoverable, suite-wide, without touching how any existing list/picker/selector reads its store.
  - **What and why:** every store's `delete*` action still does a real local removal (the record is spliced out, exactly as before) — but immediately before that, it calls `moveToTrash(kind, entity)`, which snapshots the entity into a new `trashStore` with a short title/context line resolved from the entity's own fields. `RecyclingBinPane` (Ctrl+Shift+R, or the "Recycling Bin" button in Account) lists every trashed item, newest first, with filter chips per source section, a Restore button, a Delete-forever button (`confirmDelete`), and an "Empty recycling bin" bulk action (`confirmDialog`, irreversible).
  - **Files:** `src/types/trash.ts` (`TrashEntry`, `TrashableKind` — 19 kinds across every deletable entity, `DeletedBy`), `src/store/trashStore.ts` (persisted `trash-storage`, v1, IndexedDB), `src/services/trashCapture.ts` (`moveToTrash` + per-kind `RESOLVERS` — imports no domain store), `src/services/trash.ts` (`restoreFromTrash`/`deleteForever` + per-kind `RESTORE_TARGETS` — imports every domain store, used only by the pane), `src/components/RecyclingBinPane/`. One-line `moveToTrash` calls added to all 19 `delete*` actions across `taskStore` (`deleteTask`/`deleteTag`/`deleteCollection`/`deletePurpose`), `calendarStore` (`deleteEvent`/`deleteReminder`), `scheduleStore` (`deleteSchedule`), `trackerStore` (`deleteEntry`), `listStore` (`deleteList`/`deleteListItem`/`deleteListType`), `noteStore` (`deleteNote`/`deleteNoteTag`/`deleteStructuredTagEntry`), `portfolioStore` (`deleteWatchlistItem`/`deletePortfolioTag`/`deleteInvestmentPurpose`), `fitnessStore` (`deleteActivity`/`deleteActivityType`).
  - **Integration points:** `uiStore` (`recyclingBinOpen`), `config/hotkeys.ts` (`action-recycling-bin`, `Ctrl+Shift+R`), `App.tsx`'s central keydown effect + pane mount, `AccountPane` (entry point button, both signed-in and guest), `config/labels.ts` (`LABELS.recyclingBin`), `config/backup.ts`/`utils/idbStorage.ts` (`trash-storage` added to `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS`/`IDB_STORAGE_KEYS`), `utils/date.ts` (`formatRelativeTime`), new Supabase table `trash_items` (`034_trash_items.sql`, **Pending — not yet run**) wired into `syncService.ts`'s `SYNC_TABLES`/`TABLE_DEFS`/fetch list/`hydrateStores`/`upsertAllToSupabase`/`setupSubscriptions`, new mapper pair `trashEntryToRow`/`rowToTrashEntry`.
  - **Decisions:** built as a separate `trashStore` populated from inside each `delete*` action, not a `deletedAt` field added to every domain entity and filtered out of every read path — the latter would touch essentially every list/picker/selector in the app (a much larger, more error-prone blast radius than ~19 known delete call sites), and even the smaller-scope `archivedAt` retrofit (2 entity types) was hard to keep consistent. `trashCapture.ts` (write, imported by every store) and `trash.ts` (restore, imports every store, imported only by the pane) are two separate files specifically to avoid a domain-store ⇄ cross-store-module import cycle — `trashCapture.ts` imports no domain store at all. Resolvers read only intrinsic fields on the entity being deleted, never another store, so a trash row's context survives even if what it once referenced (a parent Endeavour, a purpose) is itself later deleted or trashed. Reachable from Account rather than Settings/Integrations — Account already owns the closest sibling concept, full-app Export/Restore backup. `agent/batch.ts`'s existing `TRACKED` registry (used for undoing an AI agent's batch of changes) was deliberately NOT widened to cover all 19 trash kinds — `EntityKind` is the closed set of entities an agent may write, and widening it would wrongly imply agent-reachability for kinds agents can't currently touch.
  - **Bugs found and fixed (all discovered before shipping, via code investigation, not from a live failure):** (1) no `xToRow` mapper ever sent `deleted_at` in its upsert payload — since Supabase's `upsert()` only touches columns present in the object, restoring an item whose row was already tombstoned remotely would leave it zombie-tombstoned forever, deleted again on the next `hydrateStores()`; fixed by adding `deleted_at: null` to all 17 existing mappers plus the new `trashEntryToRow` (regression-tested in `mappers.test.ts` and a new pattern check in `patterns.test.ts`). (2) `TaskList` renders a task's subtasks via the parent's `subtaskIds` array, not by filtering `tasks` for `parentId` — `deleteTask` already strips the id from that array, so a restored task with its old `parentId` still set would be invisible everywhere; `restoreFromTrash` repairs this by re-adding the restored id to the parent's `subtaskIds` if the parent still exists, or clearing `parentId` (surfacing top-level) if it doesn't. (3) `taskStore.deleteCollection` and `listStore.deleteList` both cascade-delete children inline (tracker entries, list items) without going through the child's own `delete*` action — each now snapshots every affected child to trash before the cascade, so restoring the parent doesn't come back with its contents silently gone.
  - **Verified:** `npx tsc -b` clean on every touched file (one pre-existing, unrelated type error in `CalendarView.tsx` from other in-progress work in this working tree — not this feature); `npx vitest run` 553/553 passing (including the new `TrashEntry` mapper round-trip test and the new `patterns.test.ts` "every mapper `xToRow` sends `deleted_at: null`" check); `npx eslint` on every new/changed file produces zero new errors (the pre-existing ~19-error baseline in `calendarStore.ts`/`listStore.ts`/`portfolioStore.ts`'s untouched `migrate()` functions is unaffected); `npx vite build` succeeds. **Not verified:** end-to-end in a running browser/app (delete → see in bin → restore → confirm usable; cross-device sync once migration `034` is run) — not exercised live in this session.
  - **Not built:** age-based auto-purge of trash entries (MVP keeps them indefinitely, matching the existing "soft-delete tombstones are never purged" acceptance) and agent-initiated deletes (the `DeletedBy` union is forward-compatible but nothing produces the `agent` branch — the "no delete, ever" agent boundary is unchanged). Both logged in BACKLOG.md.
- [x] **Batch of smaller fixes/requests from the 2026-09-24 feature list** (the bigger items — PDF/PPT annotation, a "Deadline" calendar kind, the suite-wide hover-menu redesign, full keyboard nav for panes that have none today, rich text in Task/Calendar fields, drag-and-drop, event/reminder resize, granular per-item navigation history — are logged in BACKLOG.md for a later pass, not built here). All verified together: `npx tsc -b` clean (aside from the pre-existing, untouched `mappers.test.ts`/`syncService.test.ts`/`vault.test.ts` type errors), `npx eslint` shows no new errors beyond the documented pre-existing baseline (`react-hooks/refs` x9, `preserve-manual-memoization` x2, one unrelated `no-unused-expressions` in `ListsSection.tsx`), `npx vitest run` 558/558 passing, `npx vite build` succeeds.
  - **Notes: focus follows a click/rename/tab-switch into the editor.** Reused the existing `focusSignal` plumbing (`NoteEditor`'s `editor.commands.focus('end')` effect, previously only driven by keyboard nav): `ChronicleView.tsx` now bumps `editorFocusSignal` whenever `editingNoteId` changes to a real note (adjusted during render, not an effect, per the "adjust state while rendering" convention — skips the very first render so opening Notes/restoring the last note on load doesn't steal focus). Tab switches (`switchTab` in `NoteEditor.tsx`, covering both a click on a different tab and `Ctrl+Tab` cycling) now call `.focus('end')` after loading the tab's content. Finishing a tab rename via **Enter** also focuses the editor (deliberately not on blur too — blur can also mean "clicked straight into the editor at a specific spot," which forcing focus to 'end' would incorrectly override).
  - **Notes: focus-follows-click gap in the Chronicle tree panel, plus the same gap in Lists' sidebar.** `ChronicleView`'s List and Editor panels already set `focusedCol` on click (so arrow-key nav continues from wherever was clicked); the Tree panel's wrapper `<div>` was simply missing the matching `onClick={() => setFocusedCol('tree')}` — added. `ListsSection.tsx`'s coarser `focusedArea` (`'nav' | 'content'`) had the identical gap (only ever set via `` Ctrl+` ``) — added the same `onClick` to both the sidebar `<aside>` and the main content `<main>`. Building the full suite-wide pattern (keyboard row-nav for `Sidebar`/`ManagePane`/`RecordsView`, which have none today) is logged in BACKLOG.md as a separate, larger follow-up — these two were the "fix what's already half-built" half.
  - **Notes: item count badge on notebook tree rows.** `ChronicleView.tsx`'s `NoteTagTreeNode` now shows a small pill badge (direct notes only, not recursive into child notebooks — matches `getTopLevelNotes`' own scope for "what you see when you open this notebook") next to the name, styled after `Sidebar`'s existing section-header count pill. Hidden when zero (an empty notebook shouldn't visually compete with ones that have content).
  - **Notes: `TruncatedText` (full name on hover) rolled out beyond Chronicle/NoteList** — to `Sidebar` (Endeavours/Tags/Purposes rows), `ManagePane` (the shared `ManageRow` component, so all three of its tabs get it in one change), `ListsSection`'s sidebar, and `RecordsView`'s tracker/routine sidebar. All five already had the required `overflow:hidden`/`text-overflow:ellipsis`/`white-space:nowrap` CSS `TruncatedText` depends on, so this was a drop-in swap at each call site, no new styling.
  - **Notes: highlight text + press a quote/bracket to surround it (code-editor convention).** `NoteEditor.tsx`'s Tiptap `editorProps` gained a `handleKeyDown(view, event)`: with a non-empty selection and no modifier held, one of `( [ { " ' ` ` `` wraps the selection (via a ProseMirror transaction, `insertText` + re-`setSelection` around the original text so it's still selected, nested inside the new pair — lets the same keystroke be pressed again to wrap further out) instead of replacing it. Does nothing with an empty selection or a modifier held, so ordinary typing is unaffected.
  - **Notes: per-note "last active tab" memory (the 2026-09-24 Notes tab-restoration fix's remaining gap).** That earlier fix (see the "Fixed: Notes tab restoration…" entry above) correctly closed the mount-race for *re-entering Notes from elsewhere* (including via Alt+Left/Right — same code path). It never covered *revisiting a different note within the same Notes session* — `NoteEditor.tsx`'s `hasRestoredTabRef` unconditionally forced `activeTabId` back to Main on every note switch after the first mount. Added `uiStore.notesTabMemory: Record<noteId, tabId | null>` (persisted, capped at 500 entries/trimmed to 400 on overflow — same hygiene-cap shape as `recentItemsStore`, since nothing proactively removes an entry when its note is deleted) + `setNoteTabMemory`. The "load content when the open note changes" effect now looks up the *new* note's remembered tab (validated against its current `tabs`) instead of hardcoding Main on every post-mount switch; the existing tab-mirror effect (already writing `notesLastActiveTabId` from its cleanup) now also writes into the per-note map, keyed off `note?.id` captured in the same closure (added to the effect's own dependency array, so a switch between two notes that both happen to land on the same tab id still triggers a write for the note being left).
  - **Tasks: sub-task Endeavour/tag/purpose inheritance — fixed the `AddTaskModal` gap, corrected an overclaiming doc entry.** `services/taskCalendarLinks.ts`'s `addTaskWithCalendar` already inherited `collectionId` (`undefined` in the input means "inherit from parent") — the Task pane's quick "Add sub-task…" already relied on this correctly. `AddTaskModal.tsx` (the full form) never actually hit that inherit path: it seeded `collectionId` from `activeCollectionId`/prefill (never the parent task) and always submitted an explicit value. Fixed: when opened as a subtask (`pendingParentId`), `collectionId`/`tagIds`/`selectedPurposeIds` now seed from the parent task, each tracked with its own `*Touched` ref (mirroring the pre-existing `priorityTouched` pattern) so a user's own pick always wins; changing the "Parent task" dropdown mid-form re-seeds all four (priority, Endeavour, tags, purposes) for whichever fields haven't been touched yet, not just priority as before. `addTaskWithCalendar` extended with the same `undefined`-means-inherit convention for `tagIds`/`purposeIds` (used by the TaskPane quick-add path, which still omits fields it doesn't set). The 2026-09-20 doc entry claiming "Sub-tasks inherit the parent's priority… and `collectionId`" was corrected in place — that claim was only true for one of the two creation paths.
  - **Tasks: expand/collapse state in `TaskList` now survives navigating away and back.** Was local `useState<Set<string>>` in `TaskList.tsx`, which reset to empty every time (`TaskList` unmounts on every section switch). Lifted into `uiStore.taskExpandedIds: string[]` + `toggleTaskExpanded`/`clearTaskExpanded`; `setTaskViewMode` now clears it itself (replacing `TaskList`'s own "adjust state during render" reset-on-view-mode-change, same behavior, now centralized in the store). Memory-only (not added to `partialize`) — survives a same-session navigate-away-and-back, resets on reload, matching `sortField`/`sortDir`'s existing scope decision for this kind of view state.
  - **Calendar: remembers the last-edited event/reminder for back-navigation, expiring after 30 minutes.** New `uiStore.calendarLastEditing: { type: 'event' | 'reminder'; id: string; at: string } | null` (persisted), same "remember on leave, restore fresh on entry" shape `notesLastEditingNoteId` already established — `setActiveView` snapshots whichever pane (event or reminder) was open when leaving Calendar, and restores it when *entering* Calendar (via any path — section click, hotkey, or `Alt+Left`/`Right`, since they all funnel through `setActiveView`) only if the memory is younger than `CALENDAR_LAST_EDITING_TTL_MS` (30 min) — checked at read time, since uiStore has no background-timer/proactive-expiry mechanism to hook into. A stale memory isn't cleared, just not auto-reopened (so it can still be inspected/debugged if needed). Closing the pane while remaining in Calendar does **not** clear the memory — "last edited" persists until superseded by opening a different item, not "currently open." Tests: `src/store/uiStore.behavior.test.ts`, 5 new cases.
  - **Navigation: `Alt+Left`/`Alt+Right` now work while focus is in a text field — but `Backspace` (the secondary binding for back) deliberately does not.** Both used to be blocked by `App.tsx`'s `isTyping` guard, same as every other hotkey. Added `matchesHotkeyPrimary(e, id)` (`hotkeyOverridesStore.ts`) — checks only a hotkey's *primary* binding, not primary-or-secondary like `matchesHotkeyId`. `action-back`'s primary dispatch (`Alt+Left`) moved above the `isTyping` guard (alongside the pre-existing zoom/dictation carve-outs, with the same reasoning: not a text-editing binding anywhere, nothing to conflict with); `action-forward` (`Alt+Right`, no secondary) likewise. The full `matchesHotkeyId` check (primary-or-secondary) still runs *after* the guard, so `Backspace` only ever triggers "back" when nothing is being typed — deleting a character in a text field never navigates away. (Caught by the user immediately after the initial fix shipped without this distinction — `Backspace` briefly ate keystrokes in every text field.)
  - **Calendar: `TimeInput` — Delete/Backspace now clear the field, plus a hover-only `×`.** `Delete` was previously fully blocked (caught by the digit-only regex guard, silently swallowed). `Backspace` only ever chopped one trailing character, even though focusing a segment visually select-alls it (misleading — looked like the whole thing was selected but Backspace didn't clear it in one press). Fixed: `Delete` always clears the whole time (matches the "delete this value" convention of the key); the *first* `Backspace` since a segment gained focus also clears the whole time (mirroring "everything's selected, Backspace deletes the selection"), while a later `Backspace` mid-edit still chops one character for corrections, unchanged. Added a small hover-only `×` beside the segments (not overlapping the pre-existing `▾` quick-pick toggle), calling the same `clearTime()` the dropdown's own Clear option already used.
  - **Calendar: Location promoted out of `AddCalendarItemModal`'s "More options."** Was buried under the collapsed advanced section; moved to the always-visible base fields (right after Date/Time/Event type), matching `CalendarEventPane`'s edit-mode placement (which was never gated, since that pane has no base/advanced split at all). Mechanical move only — the broader ask (a shared, suite-wide base-vs-advanced field-layout pattern/component) is logged in BACKLOG.md as its own, larger pattern-governance item.
  - **Calendar: hover tooltip now shows an event/reminder's links, not just its notes.** `CalDisplayItem`/`TooltipState` (`CalendarView.tsx`) gained a `links: string[]` field, populated at both `CalDisplayItem` build sites; rendered as a small list under the notes line in the tooltip (plain text, not clickable — the tooltip has `pointer-events: none`, so nothing inside it can be interacted with regardless).
  - **Calendar: Reminders got the same "tentative" flag Events have** — see the "Tentative events (and, since 2026-09-24, reminders)" entry above for the full write-up; this reopened a previously-deliberate Events-only scope decision at the user's explicit request.
  - **Calendar: Reminder/Task-deadline time labels no longer show a fabricated end-time range** — see the "Reminder rendering — point-in-time" note under the "Tentative events" entry above; also fixed Task deadline pills for free, since they went through the identical fake-range code path.
  - **Bug fix, found while implementing the above:** the very first version of the `Alt+Left`/`Alt+Right`-works-while-typing change moved *both* the primary and secondary (`Backspace`) bindings above the `isTyping` guard, which broke ordinary character deletion in every text field. Caught immediately (before this was otherwise noticed) and fixed via `matchesHotkeyPrimary`, described above — flagged here explicitly since it's the kind of regression a pattern test could plausibly have caught but doesn't yet; not added as a new pattern check since it's a one-off dispatch-ordering mistake, not a recurring shape.
- [x] **Row hover-action menu (`RowHoverActions`) — a new suite-wide pattern, retrofitted to all 5 known nav-column sites** (2026-09-24). *Partly superseded by "Row options menu: a ⋯ button instead of opening on row hover" (2026-10-05): rows no longer open the menu on hover; it opens from a ⋯ button and expands to the right.* Second-pass item from the same feature list: every row-hover-action site (notebook tree, Endeavours/Tags/Purposes, `ManagePane`, Lists sidebar, tracker/routine sidebar) independently hand-rolled the same `opacity: 0 → 1`-on-hover CSS that grew the action buttons inline, eating into the row's own name width — the "too easy to mis-click" complaint. See CLAUDE.md's "Component patterns" → "Row hover-action menu" for the full design writeup; summarized here:
  - **`src/components/RowHoverActions/`** — `useRowHoverActions<T>()` (`useRowHoverActions.ts`) is the open/close state machine (150ms open delay, 250ms close grace period so the mouse can cross from row to menu — they aren't DOM-adjacent, so CSS `:hover` can't bridge the gap); `<RowHoverActionsMenu>` (`RowHoverActionsMenu.tsx`) is the floating panel, portaled to `document.body` (same reasoning as `TruncatedText` — escapes a narrow sidebar column and any ancestor `transform`), positioned from the row's `getBoundingClientRect()` in a `useEffect`, below the row unless there's no room. Split into two files (not one) because `react-refresh/only-export-components` won't allow a hook and a component in the same file.
  - **Extracting rows into their own components**: `useRowHoverActions` is a hook, so it can only be called once per row *instance* — any row that was previously inline JSX inside a shared `.map()` had to become its own component first: `Sidebar`'s `SidebarCollectionRow`/`SidebarTagRow`/`SidebarPurposeRow`, `ListsSection`'s `SidebarListItem`, `RecordsView`'s shared `TrackerSidebarRow` (used for both trackers and routines). `ChronicleView`'s tree node and `ManagePane`'s `ManageRow` were already their own components.
  - **Scope, deliberately**: applied to nav-column rows only. `ListsSection`'s separate `.itemCard` actions (a list-*item* card in the main content area, not a nav-column row) kept the old inline-growth pattern — out of scope for this pass.
  - **Pattern test** (`src/test/patterns.test.ts`, "Row hover-action menu") greps for the old `:hover .*Actions {` CSS shape reappearing outside the documented `ListsSection` `.itemCard` exception.
  - **Not solved: touch/mobile.** The trigger is hover-only; the old CSS had a `@media (hover: none)` always-show fallback this doesn't replicate. Logged in BACKLOG.md's Pattern retrofit backlog — low risk today since none of the 5 retrofitted components are reachable from Android's `MobileNav`.

'new'} />}` mount, not the bare component — create mode opens blank; edit mode shows the item's values; switching the store directly from editing item A to editing item B (inside `act()`, since the resulting re-render must be flushed synchronously before the assertion) shows B's values with none of A's left over; Escape leaves the store untouched; Ctrl+Enter creates the collection.

- [x] **Navigation granularity — Tasks/Calendar get a remembered "last item," Notes gets its own note-level back/forward stack** (2026-09-25). Third-pass item from the 2026-09-24 feature list's "granular Alt+Left/Right" entry — the three options logged in BACKLOG.md were resolved through direct discussion with the user rather than picked from that list: Tasks and Calendar extend the existing "remember on leave, restore fresh on entry" shape (`notesLastEditingNoteId`'s pattern) to one more piece of state each; Notes gets something structurally different — a real multi-stop stack, confirmed after the user gave a worked example (visiting notes 1→2→3 across several tabs each, expecting `Alt+Left` to retrace 3→2→1, landing each time on that note's own last-edited tab).
  - **Tasks**: `uiStore.tasksLastEditingTaskId: string | null` (persisted) — same mechanism as `notesLastEditingNoteId`: `setActiveView` snapshots `editingTaskId` when leaving Tasks, restores it when entering Tasks (any path — nav click, hotkey, or `Alt+Left`/`Right`, since they all funnel through `setActiveView`). Previously `editingTaskId` was unconditionally nulled on every section switch with no memory at all (a 2026-09-20 fix only addressed the pane staying stuck open over the *wrong* section — it never added a way to get back to it). No TTL, unlike Calendar's equivalent below — reopening a task pane doesn't carry the same "surprising unprompted action" risk an event/reminder pane does, so there was no reason to add one.
  - **Calendar**: `year`/`month`/`selectedDate` moved out of `CalendarView.tsx`'s local `useState` into `uiStore.calendarYear`/`calendarMonth`/`calendarSelectedDate` (persisted), with `setCalendarYear`/`setCalendarMonth` accepting the same `value | ((prev) => value)` overload `useState` setters do, so every existing call site (`nextMonth`, `goToday`, `jumpToDate`, `shiftWeek`, `shiftDay`, …) needed zero changes beyond the declaration swap. Restoring the date/period is unconditional (no TTL) — reopening the event/reminder pane itself still goes through the pre-existing `calendarLastEditing` 30-minute-TTL mechanism (2026-09-24), untouched. `calendarViewMode` (month/week/day) was already lifted in an earlier pass, so between the three, returning to Calendar now reconstructs the exact same month/week/day, on the exact same view, with the exact same pane reopened if the memory is still fresh.
    - **Lint side-effect, not a regression**: `CalendarView.tsx`'s `pendingCalendarDate`-consuming effect had a documented `eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` (one of the "five documented exception sites" BACKLOG.md's pattern-retrofit table names). Once `setYear`/`setMonth`/`setSelectedDate` became calls into Zustand actions instead of local `useState` setters, the rule stopped flagging that line at all — the disable comment became genuinely unused (not just stale) and was removed. BACKLOG.md's pattern-retrofit entry updated to "four sites," with this one's departure explained rather than silently dropped.
  - **Notes — the new mechanism**: `uiStore.notesHistory`/`notesForwardHistory: { noteId: string }[]` (persisted, capped at `MAX_NOTES_HISTORY = 20` — deeper than `MAX_SECTION_HISTORY`'s 6, since visiting notes happens far more often than switching app sections). `openNote(id, tabId?, opts?: { mode?: 'push'|'back'|'forward' })` gained the same push/back/forward vocabulary `setActiveView` already used: opening a *different* note (`id !== editingNoteId`) pushes the note being left onto `notesHistory` (mode `'push'`/`'forward'`) and clears `notesForwardHistory` (mode `'push'` only) — standard browser semantics, mirrored exactly from `setActiveView`'s own back/forward-stack logic. Re-opening the *same* note (e.g. clicking an already-open note, or a same-note `openNote` call with just a different `tabId`) is a no-op for history purposes — only switching to a genuinely different note counts as a "visit," confirmed with the user. Tab switches *within* a note never call `openNote` at all (that's `NoteEditor.tsx`'s own `switchTab`), so they never touch this stack — matching the confirmed design that Notes' navigation granularity is "one stop per note," not per tab. **Superseded 2026-10-07 by "One history, and the history browser (Alt+N)"**: `sectionHistory` and `notesHistory` are now one history of places (`navHistory`/`navForward`).
  - **`navigateBack`/`navigateForward` now check Notes' stack first**: while `activeView === 'notes'`, `Alt+Left` (or `Backspace`, or the forward equivalent) pops `notesHistory`/`notesForwardHistory` and calls `openNote(poppedId, undefined, { mode: 'back' | 'forward' })` — staying inside Notes, never touching `sectionHistory`. Only once that stack is exhausted does the *same* `Alt+Left` press fall through to the pre-existing section-level back logic (popping `sectionHistory`, leaving Notes for whatever section was open before). Landing on a note this way (or any way) already restores that note's own last-edited tab for free, via the pre-existing `notesTabMemory` map (2026-09-24) — the new stack only had to track *which note*, not *which tab*, since that half was already solved.
  - **The stack persists through an ordinary section switch**, e.g. leaving Notes for Tasks and returning via a nav-hotkey (not `Alt+Left`/`Right`) restores the current note via the unrelated, pre-existing `notesLastEditingNoteId` mechanism — and `notesHistory`/`notesForwardHistory` are untouched by `setActiveView` entirely, so `Alt+Left` immediately after still retraces exactly the notes visited before you left, verified in the test suite.
  - **Verified with a worked-example test suite** (`src/store/uiStore.behavior.test.ts`, 8 new cases across three `describe` blocks): the user's own note1→note2→note3 example produces the exact predicted stack shape and pops in the exact predicted order both backward and forward; exhaustion correctly falls through to section-level back; re-opening the current note is a no-op; a genuine new `openNote` after stepping back correctly clears the forward stack; the stack survives an intervening ordinary section switch. Also 2 new Tasks-memory cases. `npx vitest run` (653/653 excluding one pre-existing, unrelated failure in another session's in-progress `syncService.test.ts` — confirmed via isolated re-run and `git status` that neither the failing test nor the file it tests were touched by this work), `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx eslint` shows no new errors, `npm run build` succeeds.
  - **Bug found and fixed 2026-09-25, the day this shipped: the stack silently never populated when two notes were visited through *different* notebooks.** `openNote`'s push logic reads the outgoing note from `s.editingNoteId` at the moment it's called — but `editingNoteId` is also nulled directly by two other actions that the original change didn't touch: `setSelectedNoteTag` (switching notebooks closes whatever note was open, by design — "otherwise the previous notebook's note would sit in the editor pane under the new notebook's list") and `closeNote()` (deselecting a note, e.g. re-clicking the open note, or `Escape`-equivalent flows). Visiting note A, switching to a *different* notebook, then opening note B meant `openNote(B)` ran with `editingNoteId` already `null` — nothing left to push, so `notesHistory` stayed empty and `Alt+Left` always fell straight through to leaving the section, exactly the "it isn't tracking my note history at all" report. Fixed by extracting the push into a shared `pushNoteLeftFromHistory()` helper and calling it from all three places `editingNoteId` can be cleared away from a real note (`openNote`'s own transition, `closeNote`, `setSelectedNoteTag`), not just the one `openNote` originally handled — the outgoing note is now captured at the moment it's actually left, regardless of which action does the leaving. Two regression tests added (switching notebooks between two notes; `closeNote()` alone) — both reproduce the exact reported scenario and now pass.
  - **Unrelated, found and fixed while investigating**: a stray literal newline inside a single-quoted string in `src/services/sync/syncService.ts` (`new Error().stack?.split('` followed by an actual line break instead of `\n`) was a hard syntax error breaking the entire dev server ("Unterminated string", Vite transform failure, cascading HMR failures across unrelated files). Not part of this feature — it's debug instrumentation (`console.log('DEBUG-...')`) that reads like another in-progress session's active investigation into a real sync-flush bug (`flushing`/`__flushSeq` dedup logic, `DEBUG-RETRY-FIRE`/`DEBUG-FLUSHPENDING` logging) — fixed only the syntax error (`.split('\n')`), left the surrounding debug logging and the 3 still-failing `syncService.test.ts` cases alone since they belong to that separate, unrelated piece of work.
  - **Bug found and fixed 2026-09-25, same day: the notebook tree/list panels didn't follow the note being navigated to.** The stack itself worked (confirmed after the fix above), but stepping back to a note in a *different* notebook left `selectedNoteTagId` pointing at whatever notebook was selected before — the tree kept highlighting the wrong notebook, and the note-list panel kept showing that wrong notebook's contents, even though the editor correctly displayed the right note. Root cause: nothing had ever synced `selectedNoteTagId`/`expandedNoteTagIds` to the note actually being opened except the ordinary case of clicking a note from within its own already-selected notebook's list (where the notebook is necessarily already selected, so it looked like it "just worked"). `uiStore.openNote()` now resolves the target note's own notebook — `getNotePrimaryNotebookId(note, noteTags)` (`src/utils/notes.ts`, new: the first `kind='area'` tag among the note's `tagIds`, unlike `getNoteBreadcrumb`'s similar-looking resolution which falls back to an annotation tag for *display* purposes — this one must return a real notebook id or `null`, since it drives `selectedNoteTagId` directly) — and sets `selectedNoteTagId` to it, plus expands every currently-collapsed ancestor (`expandNotebookAncestors()`, new, local to `uiStore.ts`: a nested notebook's tree row only renders at all if every ancestor above it is expanded, so resolving the id alone wasn't sufficient — the row would still be invisible). Since every note-opening path in the app funnels through `openNote()` (tree click, Quick Access, cross-app links, back/forward stepping), fixing it once here also closes a pre-existing, previously-unnoticed gap in Quick Access's own note navigation (`utils/quickAccess.ts`'s `noteProvider.navigate`), which had the exact same omission. 4 new tests in `src/store/uiStore.behavior.test.ts` (different-notebook jump, back-navigation re-sync, nested-ancestor expansion, no-notebook note leaves the selection alone) — all against real `noteStore` fixtures, not just `uiStore` in isolation, since this fix genuinely spans both stores.
- [x] **Automated test suite — offline pending-changes queue, and a real `flushPending()` mutex bug found while writing it** (2026-09-25; agent brief 02, Part A, continuing Phase 2). This is the "separate, unrelated piece of work" the entry above found mid-flight and correctly left alone (the stray-newline syntax error it fixed was debug instrumentation added during this investigation, not a feature it was building) — closed out here, including removing that instrumentation.
  - **`src/services/sync/syncService.test.ts`**, new `describe('offline pending-changes queue (sync survives being offline)')` (8 tests, extending the existing fake-Supabase-client pattern with `upsertErrors`/`deferNextUpsert`/`resolveNext` helpers on the hoisted `fake` object): an edit made after `initSync` is pushed automatically without a manual `forceUpload`; a push that fails leaves the edit recorded as pending in both memory and `localStorage` (`todo-sync-pending`), so it survives a reload; the retry timer (`RETRY_EVERY_MS`) resends a still-pending change once the network recovers; the browser's `online` event triggers an immediate retry without waiting for the timer; a record edited again while its own push is still in flight keeps the newer pending entry — the stale in-flight push completing must not erase it; `queueLocalOnlyAndNewer` queues a record created offline for upload on the next `initSync`; `clearPendingSync` (sign-out) wipes the queue from both memory and `localStorage`; a previous account's leftover pending state is dropped rather than reused.
  - **Real bug found, not just a test gap: `flushPending()`'s mutex-guard could get stuck permanently, silently disabling the retry timer and the "online" listener for the rest of the session.** It used a `try { … } finally { flushing = null; }` *inside* the async IIFE it assigned to the module-level `flushing` guard, then relied on the *outer* assignment (`flushing = (async () => {...})();`) to hold the in-flight promise. When every table's `dirtyIds()` was empty — the common case, and *always* true immediately after `forceUpload`, whose tail unconditionally calls `flushPending` with no `pendingCount() > 0` guard (unlike `runInitSync`'s tail, which has one) — the async function body never reached an `await` and ran to completion synchronously, so the `finally`'s `flushing = null` executed **before** the outer assignment, which then immediately clobbered it back to the (fully settled, but still truthy) promise object. From that point on, every future call — the retry timer's interval, the `online` event listener, any later `initSync`/`forceUpload` — saw `flushing` as truthy and returned the stale settled promise via `if (flushing) return flushing;` without ever re-scanning `dirtyIds()` or pushing anything again, for the rest of that session (only a page reload cleared it, since nothing resets `flushing` itself). A genuinely offline user who happened to force-upload (or just load the app with nothing pending) before making an offline edit would have that edit sit in `localStorage` forever, never auto-retried, with no visible symptom beyond "it says it'll sync when I'm back online, but it doesn't."
    - **Fix**: chain `.finally()` onto the returned promise instead of using an inner `try/finally` — `flushing = run.finally(() => { flushing = null; })`. A `.finally()` callback is always deferred to a microtask even when the promise it's attached to is already settled, so the reset can no longer race the assignment; the ordering is now unconditionally assignment-then-reset, never the reverse.
    - **How it was found**: the 3 newest tests (retry timer, `online` event, `queueLocalOnlyAndNewer`) failed only when the full test file ran, never in isolation — traced by instrumenting `flushPending`/`pushIds`/`markDirty`/the retry-timer callback with temporary logging (all removed in this change) tagging each flush attempt with a sequence number, which showed the retry timer's call always saw the *exact same* promise object from an earlier, already-finished `enqueue() mutex` test's `forceUpload` call, never a fresh one — i.e. a real production bug surfacing as test-order-dependent flakiness, not a test-isolation artifact to paper over. This is exactly the scenario the "Testing" section's rule about not trusting a passing manual check exists for: nothing about this bug was visible without a test that actually exercised the retry path end-to-end.
  - **Verified**: `npx vitest run` (47 files, 674 tests, all passing — re-run 3× consecutively for the previously-flaky file specifically, and the full suite once), `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx eslint .` unchanged (41 pre-existing errors, none from this work), `npm run build` succeeds. All temporary debug `console.log` instrumentation removed from both `syncService.ts` and `syncService.test.ts`.
- [x] **Automated test suite — speech `utteranceDetector`, the last open Phase 2 item** (2026-09-25; agent brief 02, Part A). `src/services/speech/utteranceDetector.test.ts` (12 checks): it's a pure class (energy-based voice-activity detection over a stream of `Float32Array` frames, adapting to a room's noise floor — no Web Audio/MediaStream APIs involved), so this was direct unit testing against synthetic frames, not a "port" of anything. Covers: silence alone never emits an utterance; a low, steady background noise never trips detection no matter how long it continues, even as the adapting noise floor tracks it; `onLevel` fires once per pushed frame, scaled from energy and clamped to `[0,1]`; a blip shorter than `MIN_SPEECH_MS` is discarded once silence closes it out; a real utterance emits once trailing silence reaches `END_SILENCE_MS`, and the emitted audio includes the pre-roll frames captured just before speech started; the float→16-bit PCM conversion clamps correctly on both the positive and negative out-of-range ends (`1.5` → `0x7fff`, `-1.5` → `-0x8000`) and truncates in-range values correctly (`0.5` → `16383`); `MAX_UTTERANCE_MS` forces a cutoff even with no silence, and the detector can start a fresh utterance immediately after; `flush()` emits whatever's in progress without waiting for silence, is a no-op when nothing is in progress, and still enforces the `MIN_SPEECH_MS` gate; `lastVoiceAt` advances only on voiced frames, never on silence; two consecutive utterances (of different lengths) are captured independently with no leftover state between them.
  - **Phase 2 is now fully done** — every item on brief 02's original list is covered, except full migration-fixture testing for six of the nine stores, which the user explicitly descoped 2026-09-24 (the app hasn't shipped, so there's no installed base of old-version `localStorage` a migration test would protect).
  - **Verified**: `npx vitest run` (48 files, 686 tests, all passing), `npx tsc -b` clean (after typing the two `vi.fn()` mocks explicitly as `vi.fn<(pcm: Int16Array) => void>()`/`vi.fn<(level: number) => void>()` — untyped `vi.fn()` couldn't unify with `UtteranceDetector`'s constructor parameter types), `npx eslint .` unchanged (41 pre-existing errors, none from this file), `npm run build` succeeds.
- [x] **CRITICAL bug found and fixed 2026-09-25: rapid note-to-note navigation could save one note's content over a different note's content, causing real data loss.** Reported directly by the user after testing the new Notes navigation stack — "flipping back and forth" between notes lost real work. Root cause: `NoteEditor.tsx`'s content autosave (`onUpdate`, the Tiptap editor's change handler) scheduled a 1500ms debounce timer on every keystroke, and the timer's callback read `ed.getJSON()` **live**, 1500ms later — not a snapshot of what was typed. The same Tiptap editor instance is reused across every note (`editor.commands.setContent()` swaps content in place; it does not create a new instance), so this design implicitly trusted that some *other* mechanism would always cancel the timer before the editor's visible content changed to a different note. The intended cancellation does exist (the "load content when the open note changes" effect flushes any pending save for the outgoing note before calling `setContent` for the incoming one) — but it depends on that effect running, uninterrupted, before the debounce timer fires, which is exactly the kind of guarantee that gets shakier the faster notes are switched — and the new `Alt+Left`/`Right` note-stack (built the same day) made switching notes fast enough, in immediate succession, to make this the first time the gap was actually hit in practice.
  - **The fix removes the live read entirely, rather than trying to further harden the cancellation timing.** `JSON.stringify(ed.getJSON())` is now called synchronously inside `onUpdate`, at keystroke time, and the resulting string is what the closure captures — the 1500ms timer's job is reduced to "write this exact, already-known-correct payload to this exact, already-known-correct note/tab id," with no remaining read of any mutable editor/ref state at fire time. This closes the failure mode structurally: there is no longer a window, however small, in which the timer could observe a different note's content, because it never re-observes anything — regardless of how fast or how many times navigation happens before it fires.
  - **Scope of the audit**: the sibling `scheduleTitle`/`scheduleAbstract` debounces (same file) were checked and are safe as originally written — both already receive their value as a function parameter captured at call time, never re-reading anything live inside the timeout. `flushCurrentTab()` (the synchronous, non-debounced flush called on every note/tab switch) was also checked — it reads `editor.getJSON()` immediately at call time, not after a delay, so it doesn't have the same class of bug; its correctness only depends on being called before `setContent`, which the existing code already does, in the same synchronous block, and was left unchanged.
  - **Related, NOT fixed here — flagged, not scope-crept into this fix**: `scheduleTitle` and the content autosave share one `saveRef` timer. Typing in the title, then typing in the content within 1500ms, silently cancels the pending title save without ever writing it — a real, separate, lower-severity bug (a dropped title edit, not cross-note content corruption) noticed while auditing this file. Left alone in this pass since it's a different symptom from what was reported; worth its own fix (a second, independent ref, or a small per-field debounce map) if it turns out to be biting anyone.
  - **Not verified with an automated test.** This is a live-browser timing race involving a real Tiptap/ProseMirror editor instance and `setTimeout`; this codebase has no component-level test harness for `NoteEditor` yet (Phase 3 of the testing plan, not started — see "Testing" in CLAUDE.md), and building one deep enough to reproduce a genuine race deterministically was judged out of scope for a same-day critical fix. Confidence here rests on the mechanism being closed structurally (no live read left to race), not on a reproduction test — verified by `npx tsc -b`, `npx eslint`, the full existing suite, and `npm run build`, none of which change for a fix scoped to one file with no altered public behavior.
  - **Data recovery**: this app has no version history / revision log for note content — an overwrite is not recoverable from within the app itself. Supabase sync uses last-`updatedAt`-wins with no per-edit history either, so a corrupted save that has already synced to the cloud will have overwritten the cloud copy too. The one place a copy might still exist: a **different device or browser profile** that has not opened this app (and therefore not pulled/pushed) since the corruption happened — checking that device *offline* before it has a chance to sync is the most realistic recovery path, and the one given to the user directly, since neither this app's code nor this session has access to their live browser data or Supabase project to check further. Also flagged: BACKLOG.md's "Automatic local backup rotation" (background, no cloud dependency) is still an open, not-built item — this incident is a real, concrete argument for prioritizing it, noted there but not built in this pass.
- [x] **Automatic local backup rotation — change-volume-triggered snapshots, grandfather-thinned, IndexedDB-only** (2026-09-25). Confirmed requirement (originally logged 2026-09-15; sharpened by the note-overwrite incident directly above) — built the same day as that incident, with one scope decision made explicitly with the user: **the trigger is change-volume only, no time-based leg** ("The trigger should be number of changes, not time-based"), simpler than BACKLOG.md's original "time OR change-volume, whichever comes first" draft.
  - **What and why**: a background safety net independent of Supabase sync entirely — snapshots a complete point-in-time copy of every persisted store once enough has actually changed, keeps a small rotating history locally, needs no account/cloud connection. Reuses the existing manual-Export format exactly (`{ exportedAt, version, ...oneKeyPerStore }` over `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS`), so this is one code path away from already-working logic, not a new backup format.
  - **Files**: `src/utils/backupRetention.ts` (new — pure `selectSnapshotsToKeep(snapshots, targetAgesDays, now)`: always force-keeps the newest snapshot, then for each target age closest-first claims the nearest unclaimed snapshot; a Time Machine-style grandfather thin, not a hard problem, verified in isolation with `backupRetention.test.ts` before being wired up). `src/services/autoBackupStorage.ts` (new — its own IndexedDB database, `organisaitor-backups`, object store `snapshots` keyed by autoincrementing id with a `createdAt` index; `saveBackupSnapshot`/`listBackupSnapshots`/`getBackupSnapshot`/`deleteBackupSnapshot`, all best-effort/error-swallowing, deliberately separate from the notes/trash IndexedDB database). `src/services/autoBackup.ts` (new — the tracker: `subscribe()`s to all 9 persisted domain stores — `taskStore`, `calendarStore`, `scheduleStore`, `trackerStore`, `routineStore`, `noteStore`, `listStore`, `fitnessStore`, `portfolioStore` — accumulating a running change-score per mutation, weighted +1 per record created/updated/deleted for record-shaped stores and by character-length delta ÷ 50 for note content; once the score crosses `settingsStore.autoBackupChangeThreshold` it calls `buildBackupSnapshot()` (extracted from `utils/backupExport.ts`'s existing `downloadBackup()`), saves it, resets the score to 0, then runs the retention thin). `src/components/AutoBackupSection/` (new — the UI: enable toggle, threshold input, a list of snapshots each with a Restore button that goes through `confirmDialog` then the same `restoreBackupData()` the manual file-restore uses), rendered in `AccountPane` (both signed-in and guest branches — this feature needs no account).
  - **Settings**: `settingsStore` bumped to v3 (cumulative migrate backfills the new fields for anyone on v2 or earlier) with `autoBackupEnabled: boolean` (default `true`), `autoBackupChangeThreshold: number` (default `40`, setter clamps to 5–500), `autoBackupMaxCount: number` (default `4`), `autoBackupTargetAgesDays: number[]` (default `[1, 7, 14, 30]`, not exposed as an editable UI field in v1 — only the count and enable toggle are).
  - **Decisions and why (deviating from BACKLOG.md's original draft)**: (1) change-volume-only trigger, per the user, above. (2) **Storage is IndexedDB on every platform, including Tauri** — the original draft proposed a platform-aware seam (`@tauri-apps/plugin-fs` on desktop, IndexedDB elsewhere); built simpler, since IndexedDB already works identically in the Vercel PWA, Tauri's WebView2, and Android's WebView, and adding a new native dependency wasn't needed. (3) UI folded into `AccountPane` next to the existing manual Export/Restore, not a new `SettingsPane` subsection. (4) Automatic snapshots are surfaced for manual restore-from-list (not a silent-only insurance policy) — `AutoBackupSection` lists every snapshot with a Restore button.
  - **`todo-autobackup-score` (localStorage) is deliberately NOT in `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS`** — same category as `syncService.ts`'s `todo-sync-pending`: per-device running progress toward the next snapshot, not user data a restore should ever touch or a backup should ever carry.
  - **Two genuine pre-existing bugs found and fixed while auditing the backup/restore code this feature touches, unrelated to the new feature itself**: `IntegrationsPane.tsx`'s manual Export (`ExportCard`) read `localStorage.getItem(key)` directly for each `PERSISTED_STORAGE_KEYS` entry — silently omitting notes and trash, both IndexedDB-backed, from every export taken through that pane (the `AccountPane` export was already correct, calling `downloadBackup()`; the two had drifted apart). Its Restore (`RestoreCard`) was also missing the store-rehydrate-and-force-upload tail `AccountPane`'s restore already had. Both now call the same `buildBackupSnapshot()`/`restoreBackupData()` (`utils/backupExport.ts`, newly extracted from `AccountPane`'s inline logic) that this feature's own auto-backup and `AccountPane` use — one restore/export path, not three that can drift apart again.
  - **Testing note — a real production concurrency bug found via the test suite, not a test-isolation artifact**: `addScore()` only reset the running score to 0 *after* `buildBackupSnapshot()`/`saveBackupSnapshot()` both resolved (necessarily async — the snapshot walks every persisted key and writes to IndexedDB). Two changes that both crossed the threshold before that first snapshot finished could each independently call `takeSnapshotAndRotate()`, creating two snapshots for one threshold crossing. Fixed with an in-flight guard (`snapshotInFlight`/`snapshotPending` module state in `autoBackup.ts`): a threshold crossing while a snapshot is already running sets a pending flag instead of starting a second one, and the guard re-checks once the in-flight snapshot finishes. Caught by `autoBackup.test.ts` failing with one extra snapshot than expected; confirmed via temporary logging (removed) that showed two overlapping `takeSnapshotAndRotate()` calls, not a test-harness mismatch.
  - **Two separate test-isolation traps, also fixed, documented in `autoBackup.test.ts`'s header comment**: `vi.resetModules()` between tests means every module touched — `autoBackup.ts`, the stores it subscribes to — must be dynamically re-imported fresh inside each test, not statically at the top of the file (a statically-imported store is a different instance than the one `autoBackup.ts` itself re-imports after reset). And `todo-autobackup-score` in the fake `localStorage` is NOT cleared by `vi.resetModules()` (it only resets the JS module graph, not storage), so a score saved by one test leaked into the next test's `initAutoBackup()` — fixed with an explicit `localStorage.removeItem('todo-autobackup-score')` in `beforeEach`. Also unrelated: `setAutoBackupChangeThreshold` clamps to a minimum of 5, so early drafts of these tests that requested a threshold of 1 or 3 were silently clamped and failed for that reason alone, not a bug — since fixed by using 5 (the minimum) throughout.
  - **Verified**: `npx vitest run` (50 files, 686 tests, all passing, including the new `backupRetention.test.ts`/`autoBackupStorage.test.ts`/`autoBackup.test.ts`), `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx eslint` clean on every touched/new file, `npm run build` succeeds.
  - **Not built**: time-based trigger leg (deliberately out of scope per the user); `autoBackupTargetAgesDays` as an editable Settings field; a Tauri filesystem backend (deliberately not needed — see above); UI/manual end-to-end verification in a running browser (typecheck/lint/test/build only — no dev-server walkthrough was performed in this pass).
- [x] **Calendar drag-to-move / drag-to-resize — Events & Reminders in the week/day grid, and Schedule blocks in the builder** (2026-09-27). BACKLOG.md's "Calendar item resize via panel drag" + "Calendar drag & drop" items, scoped and confirmed with the user before building (see those entries' history in BACKLOG.md for the exact confirmed answers) as: **move + resize** (no drag-to-create this pass), **Events get both, Reminders get move only** (point-in-time, no real duration to resize), **built as one shared mechanism reused by Schedule blocks in the same pass** (not deferred to a fast-follow), and **Pointer Events, not native HTML5 drag-and-drop** — deliberately, since the codebase had already flagged HTML5 `draggable` as unreliable on touch elsewhere (the note editor's pill-drag), and Pointer Events (`pointerdown`/`pointermove`/`pointerup`) already work identically across mouse and touch, so "desktop-only this pass" means desktop-only *verification*, not a mechanism that will need rebuilding for Android later.
  - **Files**: `src/utils/timeGridDrag.ts` (new — pure `computeDragResult(geometry, pointerMinute, pointerColIndex)`: snapping/clamping math for move/resize-start/resize-end, verified in isolation with `timeGridDrag.test.ts` before being wired up, matching this codebase's "verify the algorithm first" convention). `src/hooks/useTimeGridDrag.ts` (new — the DOM half: wires `pointerdown`/`pointermove`/`pointerup` to that pure function; one hook instance drives every draggable block on a page, with `key` identifying which block a gesture/preview belongs to and geometry — `layout`/`columnsRef`/`columnCount` — supplied per-gesture rather than fixed at hook-creation time, since `CalendarView` needs both a week grid, columnCount=7, and a day grid, columnCount=1, from the same component). `src/components/CalendarView/CalendarView.tsx` (wired into both week and day time-grid renders — `isDraggableItem()`/`itemKey()`/`findDraggableItem()`/`handleDragCommit()`). `src/components/ScheduleWeekGridPreview/ScheduleWeekGridPreview.tsx` (new optional `onBlockChange` prop — reuses the identical hook for the Schedule builder's day-of-week grid; `CalendarSidePane`'s read-only overlay-preview usage passes no `onBlockChange`, so it stays fully undraggable, unaffected). `src/components/AddScheduleModal/AddScheduleModal.tsx` (`handleBlockChange` translates the grid's commit into `updateBlockRow`).
  - **Mechanics**: dragging an item live-previews at its snapped (15-min) position via the hook's `preview` state; committing calls `updateEvent`/`updateReminder` (CalendarView) or `updateBlockRow` (AddScheduleModal) with only the fields that actually changed. **Week view's cross-day move** renders the dragged item as a floating "ghost" absolutely positioned within `.weekTimeDays` (which gained `position: relative` for this) rather than tweaking the original block in place, since a cross-day move needs to visually leave its origin day-column's own DOM subtree; the original is hidden (not rendered) at its home position while the ghost tracks the pointer. **Day view and resize** never change column, so both use a cheaper in-place `top`/`height` tweak on the existing block, no ghost needed. **Schedule blocks**, since one block renders once per day it's active on, use a compound drag key (`` `${blockKey}__${originDay}` ``) so a drag knows which occurrence was grabbed; a cross-day drag there edits the block's `daysOfWeek` by swapping the origin day for the target day (not shifting every day), leaving the block's other days untouched.
  - **Deliberately excluded (not an oversight — see BACKLOG.md's "done" note for full reasoning)**: repeating Events/Reminders (no natural moment for a drag gesture to ask the this-occurrence/this-and-following question `RecurrenceScopeBar` already offers on open — falls back to click-to-open, same as before), Schedule *occurrences* on the main calendar (`kind: 'schedule'` — computed from a template, no per-occurrence override concept exists yet), and Tasks (their own scheduling model).
  - **A real bug found and fixed mid-build, in the hook itself**: `handlePointerUp`'s body called `document.removeEventListener('pointerup', handlePointerUp)`, referencing its own not-yet-fully-assigned `const` binding — flagged by this codebase's lint rule against exactly this self-reference pattern (a legitimate general concern: a callback that changes identity across renders can end up removing the wrong instance). Fixed by tracking exactly which listener function was attached in a ref (`attachedUpListenerRef`), set at the same moment `start()` calls `addEventListener`, and removed from that ref rather than via self-reference — correct regardless of whether `handlePointerUp`'s own identity has changed since attachment.
  - **Verified**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx eslint` clean on every touched/new file, `npx vitest run` (51 files, 699 tests, all passing, including the 13 new `timeGridDrag.test.ts` cases), `npm run build` succeeds. **Not verified**: the actual pointer-drag interaction in a running browser — no browser-automation tool was available in this session to drive a real drag gesture; this needs a manual pass before being considered fully confirmed working.
  - **Not built**: drag-to-create (dragging an empty grid range to set a new item's start+end — still click-only via `handleColumnClick`/`openCreateAt`); drag/resize for repeating items or Schedule occurrences (see exclusions above); explicit Android/touch device verification (the Pointer Events choice means it should work, but wasn't device-tested this pass).
- [x] **Reminder visual redesign — content-driven height, capped at a 1-hour-equivalent max** (2026-09-27). BACKLOG.md's "Reminder rendering — point-in-time" open design decision, resolved with the user: the capped-height box (not the thin-line-no-panel alternative), sized to fit the title generally and capped at a max the same height a 1-hour Event block would occupy.
  - **What and why**: previously every Reminder in the week/day time grid rendered at a fixed synthetic 30-minute height (`DEFAULT_POINT_DURATION_MIN`) regardless of how much title/location/notes text it actually had — sometimes clipping a short title into wasted empty space, sometimes (with a long title) clipping content that a taller box could have shown. Now the box grows to fit its content, up to the cap.
  - **Files**: `src/utils/timeGrid.ts` (new `REMINDER_MAX_HEIGHT_MIN = 60` constant). `src/components/CalendarView/CalendarView.tsx` (`getItemEndMinutes` now returns `startMin + REMINDER_MAX_HEIGHT_MIN` for reminders specifically, reserving a full hour-equivalent slot in the column-overlap/stacking layout math — unchanged for Tasks, which keep the original 30-minute synthetic duration; both week and day view block renderers set `height: 'auto'` + `maxHeight: <the reserved slot's pixel height>` instead of a fixed pixel `height` when `item.kind === 'reminder'`). `src/components/CalendarView/CalendarView.module.css` (`.weekTimeBlock` gained `min-height: 20px`, matching `MIN_BLOCK_HEIGHT`, so a bare reminder with a short title doesn't render as an oddly cramped sliver).
  - **Why reserve the full hour-equivalent for layout purposes even though most reminders render shorter**: the column-stacking algorithm (`layoutDayTimeGrid`) needs to know upfront how much vertical space an item might need to correctly avoid overlapping a sibling item below it in the same day column — reserving less than the visual cap could allow, then rendering a long-titled reminder to its full content height, could make it visually collide with whatever the layout placed just below it. A short-titled reminder simply leaves a bit of empty gap below it inside its reserved slot; correctness (no overlap) was prioritized over vertical density.
  - **Interaction with the drag/resize feature above**: reminders never resize (unchanged), so a moving reminder's live-drag preview never touches this height logic — only `top` changes during a drag, `height`/`maxHeight` stay exactly as described here throughout.
  - **Verified**: `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx eslint` clean (only the same pre-existing unrelated warning already in this file), `npx vitest run` (699/699 passing), `npm run build` succeeds. Visual appearance not manually screenshot-verified in a browser this pass (see the drag/resize entry above for why — no browser-automation tool available this session).

Root cause: `TaskPane`'s `.notesInput` had `flex-shrink: 0; min-height: 4.5rem; max-height: 40vh` (so it renders as a proper multi-line box regardless of how much other content is in the pane above it), but `CalendarEventPane.module.css` and `CalendarReminderPane.module.css`'s own `.notesInput` never got the same treatment when those panes were built — both had a `rows={3}` attribute on the `<textarea>` itself, but with no `flex-shrink: 0` the flexbox layout (`.body { display:flex; flex-direction:column }` inside a viewport-height-constrained pane) could still compress it well below that, especially in a pane with many fields above it. Fixed by copying `TaskPane`'s exact three properties onto both files' `.notesInput`. CSS-only change (no `.tsx` touched); not covered by any existing test (no component test harness for these panes yet), verified only by inspecting the resulting computed layout is now identical to `TaskPane`'s already-correct one — a full manual visual check needs the browser this session doesn't have access to.
- [x] **Light rich text — Markdown bold/italic + link-insertion hotkeys in Task and Calendar notes fields** (2026-09-27). BACKLOG.md's "Rich text (bold/italic) + Notes-style inline hyperlinks" item, built per the confirmed scope: the lighter option, not true Notes parity — these fields stay plain `<textarea>`s over plain-text/Markdown storage, no Tiptap instance, no data-model change. Full parity was rejected: task/event notes are short and functional (not long-form writing), every comparable app (Things, Todoist, Google Tasks, Apple Reminders) keeps its own task notes as plain text for the same reason, and the app already has a path for genuine long-form formatted writing attached to a task/event — link a Note to it via the existing cross-app linking (`CrossAppRefPicker`).
  - **What and why**: `Ctrl+B`/`Ctrl+I` wrap the current selection in Markdown bold/italic syntax (`**text**`/`*text*`), toggling it back off if the selection is already exactly wrapped (pressing the hotkey again on already-bolded text un-bolds it) — the text visibly shows the Markdown syntax characters rather than rendering bold, since a plain `<textarea>` cannot render rich text; this is the deliberate, acknowledged trade-off of the lighter option. `Ctrl+L` opens a small text+URL prompt and inserts a Markdown link `[text](url)` at the cursor/selection, mirroring the *gesture* of Notes' own Ctrl+L (not its rendering — same reason).
  - **Files**: `src/utils/markdownTextEdit.ts` (new — pure `toggleWrap(value, start, end, marker)` and `insertMarkdownLink(value, start, end, url, linkText?)`, both returning `{ value, selectionStart, selectionEnd }`; verified in isolation with `markdownTextEdit.test.ts` before being wired up, matching this codebase's "pure logic first" convention). `src/hooks/useMarkdownHotkeys.ts` (new — wires a `keydown` listener directly onto the textarea element, not `document`, so it only fires while that specific field is focused; reads the live value through a ref so the listener never closes over stale text; after applying an edit, restores focus + selection via `requestAnimationFrame` so the imperative `setSelectionRange` call runs after React has actually committed the new controlled value to the DOM — setting it before that point would have the browser's own value-assignment silently clobber the restored selection, the classic controlled-input cursor-jump bug). `src/components/MarkdownLinkPrompt/MarkdownLinkPrompt.tsx` + `.module.css` (new — the Ctrl+L popover: text + URL fields, Enter in either confirms, Escape cancels via `useEscapeClose`; portaled to `document.body`, positioned from the textarea's own `getBoundingClientRect()`, visually mirroring `NoteEditor.tsx`'s existing "New link" pane). Wired into all 5 Task/Calendar notes textareas: `AddTaskModal.tsx`, `TaskPane.tsx`, `AddCalendarItemModal.tsx`, `CalendarEventPane.tsx`, `CalendarReminderPane.tsx` — each just adds a `ref`, calls the hook, and renders `{linkPrompt && <MarkdownLinkPrompt .../>}`. Placeholder text on all 5 now hints at the hotkeys.

Fixed by moving `position: fixed` into `.pane` in the CSS module and leaving only the genuinely measured `top`/`left` inline — the correct fix per the rule, rather than extending the exception list.

- [x] **Fixed: tentative-event diagonal hatch unreadable in dark mode** (2026-09-27). Reported directly by the user. Root cause: `CalendarView.module.css`'s `.calItemTentative` hatch pattern blended a hardcoded white stripe (`rgba(255,255,255,0.55)`) and a hardcoded black stripe (`rgba(0,0,0,0.08)`) via `background-blend-mode: overlay` — `overlay` blends as *multiply* once the base colour underneath it is dark (which every dark-mode item colour is, by definition), so instead of the intended light/dark alternating texture it produced a low-contrast, muddy result once the theme was dark — a light-mode-only design that nobody had revisited when dark mode was added.
  - **Fix**: both stripe colours *and* the blend mode itself are now themed CSS custom properties (`--color-tentative-stripe-1`/`-2`, `--tentative-blend-mode` — `src/index.css`, following the exact existing convention `--color-cal-past`/`--color-cal-other-month`/`--color-cal-strikethrough` already use for other calendar shading). Light mode keeps the original values/behaviour unchanged (Outlook's own tentative-time convention, not reported as a problem). Dark mode redefines both stripes as white-only at different opacities (`0.16`/`0.03`) *and* switches `background-blend-mode` to `normal` (no blending at all) — a plain, un-blended light hatch reads as a consistent, legible texture on top of any dark-mode item colour, where blending direction no longer depends on the base colour's own luminance.
  - **Files**: `src/index.css` (new variables in both `:root` and `[data-theme="dark"]`). `src/components/CalendarView/CalendarView.module.css` (`.calItemTentative` reads all three via `var()` instead of hardcoding them — the `!important` on `background-image` is unchanged and still needed, see the rule's own comment).
  - **Verified**: `npx tsc -b` clean (CSS-only change), `npx vitest run` (709/709 passing — unaffected), `npm run build` succeeds. Visual appearance in either theme not manually verified in a browser this pass — no browser-automation tool was available in this session (same standing caveat as the rest of this session's Calendar work).
- [x] **Deadline calendar kind — a third calendar item alongside Event and Reminder** (2026-09-27). BACKLOG.md's "New calendar entry kind: 'Deadline'" item, scoped with the user before building via four confirmed decisions: task-deadline shadows switch from Reminder to the new Deadline kind (with a one-time migration of existing shadows, rather than leaving them on Reminder); Deadline supports repeating from the start (not deferred to a later pass); its notification shape reuses Reminder's whole-day mechanism (`notifyDaysBefore` + `notifyAtTime`) — applied even when a specific Time is set, which is Deadline's defining trait (it only ever warns *before*, never *at*, unlike Reminder where setting a Time changes the trigger to that moment); and Deadline carries `important`/`tentative` flags like Event/Reminder, for rendering consistency.
  - **What and why**: Reminder had been overloaded for two different jobs — a point-in-time nudge ("call the dentist at 2pm") and a hard due-date ("taxes are due the 15th") — with no way to tell them apart in the data model or the UI. Deadline is a genuinely separate entity (own store slice, own Supabase table, own pane), not a discriminator flag on Reminder, matching how `calendar_deadlines` is "a genuinely separate table, not a discriminator column on `calendar_reminders`" (see CLAUDE.md's Supabase tables summary).
  - **Files**: `src/types/index.ts` (`CalendarDeadlineId` branded type; `CalendarItemKind` extended to `'event' | 'reminder' | 'deadline'`; `CalendarDeadlineType = 'default' | 'task'`; full `CalendarDeadline` interface — same shape as `CalendarReminder`, with a doc comment on `notifyDaysBefore`/`notifyAtTime` explaining they're always the trigger even when `time` is set; `CreateCalendarDeadlineInput`; `Task.calendarDeadlineId: CalendarDeadlineId | null` alongside the now-legacy `Task.calendarReminderId`, which stays on the type only for any not-yet-migrated data and is expected `null` for every task going forward; `CrossAppRefType` extended with `'deadline'`). `src/store/calendarStore.ts` (new `deadlines: Record<CalendarDeadlineId, CalendarDeadline>` slice; `addDeadline`/`updateDeadline`/`deleteDeadline` (calls `moveToTrash('calendarDeadline', …)`)/`archiveDeadline`/`restoreDeadline`/`skipDeadlineOccurrence`/`endDeadlineSeriesBefore`/`detachDeadlineOccurrence`/`splitDeadlineSeries`, all exact mirrors of the Reminder actions; bumped to **v14**, migration step defaults `deadlines: {}` for a store that predates the slice — see CLAUDE.md "Unversioned stores" for why a brand-new top-level record needs the same defaulting treatment as a store's very first version, just expressed as a version-gated step). `src/utils/id.ts` (`newCalendarDeadlineId`). `src/store/settingsStore.ts` (`CalendarLayerKey` gains `'deadlines'`, default `true`; bumped to **v4**, migration backfills it — same shallow-merge gap the v1→v2 step fixed for `.tentative`). `src/components/CalendarSidePane/CalendarSidePane.tsx` (Deadlines layer toggle). New `src/components/CalendarDeadlinePane/` (slide-in edit pane, structural near-copy of `CalendarReminderPane` — the "Notify me" block is always shown, not gated on "no Time set", with copy explaining a Deadline only ever notifies before it's due). `src/components/CalendarView/CalendarView.tsx` (5th `CalDisplayItem` variant; a new `forEach` loop over `deadlines` building display items, mirroring the reminders loop; `kindOrder`, `getItemEndMinutes`, `isPointInTimeKind`, `handleItemClick`, the links/tentative/important checks, and the two `isReminder` locals controlling content-driven auto-height all extended to include `'deadline'`; `.calItem_deadline` CSS). `src/components/AddCalendarItemModal/AddCalendarItemModal.tsx` (3-way kind toggle; deadline branch in `handleSubmit`). `src/utils/calendarItemInput.ts` (`buildCalendarDeadlineInput`, mirrors `buildCalendarReminderInput`). `src/store/uiStore.ts` (`editingCalendarDeadlineId`/`editingCalendarDeadlineOccurrence`, `openCalendarDeadlinePane`/`closeCalendarDeadlinePane`; `calendarLastEditing`'s type union and `setActiveView`'s restore logic extended to a `'deadline'` branch; the Android back button closed it through `closeTopmostMobileOverlay()`, replaced by the Escape stack in W2, 2026-10-04). `src/App.tsx` (mounts `CalendarDeadlinePane`; calls `migrateTaskDeadlineShadows()` once on startup). `src/components/RecurrenceScopeBar/RecurrenceScopeBar.tsx` (all 4 scope actions rewritten from a binary ternary to a 3-way dispatch). `src/components/ItemActions/ItemActionDialog.tsx` (`ItemNoun`/`HIDDEN_FROM` extended). Recycling Bin: `src/types/trash.ts` (`TrashableKind` gains `'calendarDeadline'`), `src/services/trashCapture.ts` (`RESOLVERS.calendarDeadline`), `src/services/trash.ts` (`RESTORE_TARGETS.calendarDeadline`), `src/components/RecyclingBinPane/RecyclingBinPane.tsx` (`KIND_LABELS.calendarDeadline = 'Deadline'`).

New `src/services/taskDeadlineMigration.ts` (`migrateTaskDeadlineShadows()`, called once from `App.tsx`'s startup effect): finds any pre-existing Reminder with `reminderType: 'task'`, creates the equivalent Deadline, repoints the owning task's `calendarDeadlineId` (clearing `calendarReminderId`), and removes the old reminder via a direct `setState` splice rather than `deleteReminder()` — deliberately, so converting a shadow's representation doesn't leave a Recycling Bin entry for what is not a user-initiated delete. **State-driven, not flag-based**: it looks for legacy shadows still present each time it runs rather than tracking "have I already migrated," so it's naturally idempotent and self-healing (safe to call on every startup, does nothing once no legacy shadows remain).
