- [x] **Checklist lists (Shopping)** (2026-09-28). A third `ListKind`, `'checklist'`, for shopping-list style lists: add items, tick them off; a ticked item is struck through and moves to the bottom of the list. New built-in type **Shopping 🛒** (`lt-shopping`, no default fields), shown first in the create-list type picker under "Checklists".
  - **Files**: `src/types/lists.ts` (`ListKind`), `src/store/listStore.ts` (built-in type, `toggleListItemChecked`, persist `merge`), `src/components/ListsSection/ChecklistView.tsx` (new; styles are the `.check*` classes in `ListsSection.module.css`), `ListsSection.tsx` (content fork + sidebar grouping), `AddListModal.tsx` (type picker grouped per kind), `src/utils/quickAccess.ts` (kind label), `src/config/labels.ts` (`LABELS.listKind`, `LABELS.checklist`).
  - **How it works**: the checked state is `ListItem.status === 'done'` (unchecked = `'want'`). No new field, so there is no persist-version bump and no Supabase migration (`lists.kind` is unconstrained `text`; `list_items.status` already syncs). `toggleListItemChecked(id)` is the one place the rule lives: checking sets `status: 'done'` and `order = max order in the list + 1`, so the item most recently ticked is always last; unchecking sets `'want'` and leaves `order` alone, so it rejoins the unchecked group at its end. `ChecklistView` renders unchecked items by `order`, then a "N checked · Clear checked" divider, then checked items by `order`, struck through. Goes through `editItem`, so encrypted checklists work like other lists.
  - **UI**: an "Add an item…" box at the top (Enter adds and keeps focus, into the current tab if one is selected); click a title to rename in place; ✎ opens the usual `AddListItemModal` (title, tab, custom fields, notes, links; no status picker, which stays watchlist-only), and any custom field values and notes appear as a grey line under the title; ✕ deletes with confirmation; **Clear checked** deletes every checked item after a `confirmDialog` (they go to the Recycling Bin). Sidebar is now grouped Checklists / Watchlists / Reference, with a group heading shown only when more than one group has lists (before this change the "Reference" heading showed even when it was the only group).
  - **Related store changes**: `addListItem`'s default `order` is now `max + 1` instead of the item count (the count collided with orders bumped by checking, and after deletes). A persist `merge` now re-adds any built-in list type missing from the saved `listTypes`. Before this, built-ins were only re-seeded by the v2 migration, so Shopping would never have appeared on an existing install. User edits to a built-in are kept.
  - **Decisions**: I reused `status` rather than adding a `checked` boolean because it needs no migration, and switching a list's kind keeps a meaningful state. Checked items move to the bottom rather than staying in place, as requested. A checked item's original position is not remembered.
  - **Verified**: `src/store/listStore.behavior.test.ts` (new: checklist kind, check moves to bottom, uncheck, add-after-bump ordering, built-in re-added on rehydrate); full suite 738/738, `tsc -b` clean, no new eslint errors, `npm run build` passes. Also checked once in headless Chromium against `vite preview`: type picker, creating a Shopping list, quick-add, check/uncheck ordering, Clear checked, dark mode, no page errors.
  - **Not built**: drag-to-reorder items; an animation for the move; agent commands for lists (none exist for any list kind); Android-specific verification.

- [x] **Fixed: misaligned sync load put rows in the wrong stores; sync now keyed by table name** (2026-09-28).
  - **Symptom**: opening the Calendar side pane crashed the section (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'localeCompare')` in `CalendarSidePane`'s schedule sort). Every sync also logged `null value in column … violates not-null constraint` pushes for `schedules`, `list_items`, `list_types`, `note_tags`, `structured_tag_entries`, `watchlist_items` and `trash_items`.
  - **Cause**: `runInitSync` fetched through a hand-written `Promise.all` list and destructured the results into positional variables, which `hydrateStores(...args)` read by position again. While "Deadline calendar kind" was being built (2026-09-27), a dev-server reload between the edits adding `calendar_deadlines` to those lists ran one load with them one position apart. Every store from `tracker_entries` on merged the *next* table's rows and persisted them: schedules got tracker entries (no `name` → the crash), lists got schedules, list items got lists, list types got list items, notes got list types, note tags got notes, structured tag entries got note tags, watchlist items got structured tag entries, portfolio tags got watchlist items, investment purposes got portfolio tags, and trash got investment purposes. The committed code was aligned; the damage stayed in local storage. Misrouted rows whose shape met the wrong table's constraints (probably lists, notes, portfolio tags and investment purposes) may also have been uploaded to Supabase.
  - **Fix (prevention)**: `syncService.ts` fetches `SYNC_TABLES.map(...)` and passes a `RemoteRows` map keyed by table name to `hydrateStores(remote)` and `queueLocalOnlyAndNewer(remote)`. There is no positional list left to drift. `src/test/patterns.test.ts` check #9 now asserts that the fetch comes from `SYNC_TABLES`, that no hand-written `supabase.from('x').select` list exists, and that `hydrateStores` reads `remote.<table>` for every table.
  - **Cleanup (one-off, now removed)**: a temporary `services/sync/findMisroutedRecords.ts` flagged a record when its id belonged to the store its row came from, or when it lacked a field that store requires (`name`/`listId`/`content`/`typeKey`/`kind`). Lists, portfolio tags and investment purposes could be recognised by id only, because their source rows had the same shape. It ran first as a dry run (logging only), because an automatic removal was blocked as an unapproved data deletion. After the user reviewed the output, they applied `removeMisroutedRecords()` themselves. It removed the flagged records straight from the record maps (not through `delete*`, so the copies didn't land in the Recycling Bin), and ran after `setupSubscriptions` so each removal was also soft-deleted in Supabase. The user confirmed it worked (side pane fixed, sync errors gone) on 2026-09-28, and the file, its test and its call were then deleted. Recorded here in case the same shape of damage is ever seen again.
  - **Verified**: full suite and `tsc -b` clean after each step; the removal was verified by the user on their real data (console showed the removal, a second reload found nothing, Calendar side pane opens).
  - **Dry-run output reviewed 2026-09-28** (the user's real data): 31 schedules, 1 list ("Herbstsemester 2026", a copy of the schedule), 5 list items, ~40 list types, ~40 note tags, ~24 structured tag entries, 5 watchlist items, ~45 portfolio tags (named after watchlist companies), ~57 investment purposes (copies of the portfolio tags, including the junk ones) and trash entries (copies of the investment purposes, including the `ip-*` seeds). No notes were flagged. Every flagged record was confirmed as a copy: its id belongs to an unflagged genuine record in the store it came from. The output also shows **the misaligned load ran more than once**. Junk portfolio tags were uploaded, then a later load pulled them from Supabase into investment purposes (likewise the junk list → list items). So Supabase held junk too, which is why the removal had to soft-delete remotely, not only locally.

- [x] **Calendar: Travel event type, week-grid alignment + whole-day zone, collapsed night** (2026-09-28).
  - **Travel event type**: `CalendarEventType` gains `'travel'`, for a flight, a train or bus, or a long drive. That is time that's booked out but isn't something you're "at". It renders as a timed block that still occupies its slot, but quieter than a normal event: a pale fill, a dashed outline in the event's own colour, and 🧳 before the title (`.calItemTravel` in `CalendarView.module.css`; `isTravelItem()`; `CalDisplayItem.travel`; `SpanSlot.travel` for a multi-day journey). This follows the convention in Apple Calendar and Outlook, which show travel time as a lighter block that still blocks the time. It uses `background-color`, not the shorthand, so a tentative journey still gets the hatch on top. `event_type` has been free text since `001_initial.sql`, so no migration was needed.
    - **Decision: separate from `background` events.** Those are an ambient multi-day stay ("Mum is visiting", "in Vietnam this week") drawn as a line over the days. Travel is the journey itself, in the hour grid.
    - The pickable types (Event/Birthday/Travel, with icons) moved from two hard-coded copies (`AddCalendarItemModal`, `CalendarEventPane`) plus the import review's `<option>`s into `src/config/calendarEventTypes.ts` (`PICKABLE_EVENT_TYPES`, `EVENT_TYPE_ICON`). The names are in `LABELS.calendarEventType`.
    - Timed blocks never showed a type icon (only all-day pills did); travel blocks now show 🧳 before the title.
    - The agent's `create_calendar_item` accepts `eventType: 'travel'`, and its help text explains when to use it.
  - **Week-grid alignment**: the hour grid scrolls and the rows above it (day header, multi-day spans, untimed items) don't. The grid's scrollbar made its day columns narrower than the header's, so every column boundary was offset. Fixed with `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on all of them (plus `overflow-y: hidden` on the non-scrolling rows, since the property only applies to an element with non-visible overflow).
  - **Whole-day zone**: the header, span and untimed rows (week) and the untimed row (day) are tinted `--color-surface-alt`. The hour grid starts below a 2px `--color-border-strong` line (new token, light and dark), and the row directly above drops its own hairline so the two don't stack.
  - **Collapsed night**: `buildHourLayout(activeHours, { collapseNight: true })` (`utils/timeGrid.ts`) folds midnight–6am into one 34px band (`NIGHT_END_HOUR`, `NIGHT_COLLAPSED_HEIGHT`) when nothing visible is scheduled in those hours. Hour 0 carries the band's height and hours 1–5 are zero-height. `minutesToY`/`yToMinutes` spread the night proportionally across the band, so the current-time line, a click and a drag all land at the matching night time. Week and day views opt in. The Schedule builder's preview grid doesn't (it's for laying out blocks at any hour). The band has a faint hatch and one two-line gutter label; `renderGutterLabels()`/`renderNightBand()` in `CalendarView` now draw the gutter for both views instead of two copies.
  - **Verified**: `timeGrid.test.ts` (collapse on/off, opt-in, proportional mapping and its inverse) and `agent/__tests__/calendar.test.ts` (travel event keeps its times). Full suite 743/743, `tsc -b` clean, no new eslint findings. Checked in headless Chromium against `vite preview`, week and day, light and dark, no page errors: columns aligned, zone tint and divider, night band, current-time line inside the band, travel block with 🧳 and dashed outline, tentative travel hatched.
  - **Not built**: a separate "Travel" layer toggle in the side pane (travel follows the Events layer); Apple-style travel time attached to another event (a buffer before a meeting); a mode-specific icon (✈️/🚆/🚌); Android-specific verification.

- [x] **Export an automatic backup snapshot as a JSON file** (2026-09-28). Automatic snapshots (see "Automatic local backup rotation") live only in IndexedDB — on desktop that is inside the WebView2 profile (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\com.organisaitor.app\EBWebView\…`) as LevelDB files nobody can use directly. This gives a way to get one out as an ordinary file.
  - **What**: a new "Export automatic snapshot" card in Data & Integrations → Backup (`SnapshotExportCard` in `IntegrationsPane.tsx`), between Export and Restore: a dropdown of this device's snapshots (newest first, preselected) and an Export button that downloads the chosen one to the Downloads folder as `organisaitor-autobackup-YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM-SS.json` (the snapshot's own time, UTC).
  - **Files**: `utils/backupExport.ts` — new `downloadAutoBackupSnapshot(id, createdAt)`; the Blob/anchor download step was pulled out of `downloadBackup()` into a private `downloadBackupFile()` so both exports share it. The file is the same pretty-printed `{ exportedAt, version, ...oneKeyPerStore }` shape a manual Export writes, so the existing Restore card (and Account's Restore) accept it unchanged.
  - **Decisions**: kept in Integrations only, as asked — `AutoBackupSection` in Account still offers Restore per snapshot but not Export.
  - **Verified**: `tsc -b` clean, eslint clean on touched files, full suite 743/743, `npm run build` succeeds. **Not verified**: the download in a running app (desktop or browser) — no UI walkthrough this pass. No automated test added (`backupExport.ts` has no test file; the new function is a read plus the existing download step).

- [x] **Lists: links from tasks, calendar items and notes; linked checklists as a task's steps; reusable checklists** (2026-09-28).
  - **What**: a task, calendar event, reminder or deadline can link to a whole list (`CrossAppRefPicker`'s new "+ List" → `ListPickerModal`), and a list can link to notes (the Notes row of `ListsSection/ListLinksBar.tsx`). When a task's linked list is a checklist, its items appear inside `TaskPane` as tickable steps (`LinkedChecklists`), and the task row shows a "📋 3/7" pill (`TaskItem`, `checklistProgress()`). Ticking works from either side, because each row *is* the `ListItem`; nothing is copied or mirrored. Ticking the last open item asks "Complete “<task>”? Everything on <list> is ticked off." (Complete task / Not yet). A checklist marked **Reusable** (`List.resetOnTaskComplete`, a checkbox in `AddListModal` for checklists only) is unticked when a linked task is completed, ready for next time. Checklists also get an **Untick all** button next to Clear checked.
  - **Decisions and why**:
    - **One copy of each item, not mirrored sub-tasks.** Mirroring every list item as a Task sub-task was rejected: two synced copies of every item, conflict handling on edit/delete/reorder, double Recycling Bin entries, no readable copy possible for an encrypted list, and a reused shopping list has a different lifecycle from a one-off task.
    - **Reusable is a per-list option, not a new list kind.** Kind decides how a list renders; reusability is about what happens after a linked task is done, and only checklists have ticks. This matches how Todoist unticks a recurring task's sub-tasks when it recurs, and shopping apps' "uncheck all", rather than the template-and-copy model (Things/Notion), which suits one-off packing lists better than a weekly shop.
    - **The prompt asks per open linked task, and only when *all* of that task's linked checklists are done.** It re-checks the task after the wait (it may have been completed or deleted meanwhile).
    - **Where the refs live**: links *to* a list are `{ type: 'list' }` entries on the source item's existing `crossAppRefs`, so tasks and calendar tables need no migration. A list's links *to notes* are the new `List.crossAppRefs`, following the rule that the item holds the ref and the note derives its "Linked from" pill (`store/noteBacklinks.ts` now includes lists). A list's own "Linked from" row is derived the same way from tasks and calendar items, never stored.
    - **`updateListLinks`** (listStore) writes `crossAppRefs`/`resetOnTaskComplete` directly, because they're plaintext even on an encrypted list. `updateList` refuses any edit to a *locked* encrypted list, and deleting a note must still be able to drop a list's link to it.
    - **Agents are left out on purpose.** Agents have no list access, so `access.write.toggleTask` stays the plain store toggle and an agent completing a task doesn't untick a reusable list. That would be an untracked, non-undoable change to data agents are kept away from (encrypted lists included). Agents also can't see `crossAppRefs`, so a link to an encrypted list reveals nothing.
    - **Every UI path that completes a task** (TaskItem, TaskPane incl. sub-tasks, the calendar panes' Complete, NotificationCenter) now calls `toggleTaskWithLists`, and every UI checklist tick calls `toggleChecklistItemWithTasks`, so the rules can't differ by entry point.
  - **Files**: `services/taskListLinks.ts` (new), `components/ListPickerModal/` (new), `components/LinkedChecklists/` (new), `components/ListsSection/ListLinksBar.tsx` (new), `CrossAppRefPicker` (`types` prop, list chips, "+ Note"/"+ List"), `services/crossAppLinkCleanup.ts` (`deleteListWithCleanup`, list branch in `removeCrossAppRefFromTarget`, `deleteNoteWithCleanup` drops lists' note refs), `services/openCrossAppTarget.ts` (`'list'` opens Lists on that list via `setListsLastActive`), `store/noteBacklinks.ts` + `NoteBacklinks` (📋 pills), `listStore` v6 (`uncheckAllListItems`, `updateListLinks`, migrate backfills both new fields), `mappers.ts`, `038_list_links.sql` (**pending**), `LABELS.listLinks`/`LABELS.checklist.*`, `CrossAppRefType` gains `'list'`.
  - **Verified**: `services/taskListLinks.test.ts` (new: progress, reset on complete, no reset on un-complete, the prompt on the last tick, decline, reusable message and reset, multi-list wait, no prompt for done/archived tasks or on untick), `crossAppLinkCleanup.test.ts` (list delete drops task/event refs and note text, note delete drops list refs, unlink from list, `updateListLinks` on a locked list), mapper round trip with the new fields. Full suite 756/756, `tsc -b` clean, no new eslint findings. Checked in headless Chromium against `vite preview` with a seeded v5 `lists-storage` (so the v6 migration ran for real): the "📋 2/3" pill on the task row, the checklist inside the task pane, ticking the last item from the task pane brings up the prompt, "Complete task" completes the task, and Lists shows "Linked from: Weekly shop" (struck through) plus Untick all. No page errors.
  - **Not built**: see BACKLOG.md "Linking to whole Lists". Starting a link from inside a note; single-item links; linked checklists inside calendar panes; agent parity; links surviving a Recycling Bin restore.

- [x] **Notes "Create ▸ Calendar item": phrase cues for kind, type, tentative, important, repeat** (2026-09-28).
  - **What**: `inferCalendarItemFromSelection` (`utils/textToTask.ts`) now reads what the selected text *says*, not only its date and time. Previously it chose only between Event and Reminder (a time range or an event word meant Event). It now also returns `'deadline'`, an `eventType` (`'travel'` / `'birthday'`), `tentative`, `important` and a `repeat` (`freq` + `interval`). `FloatingToolbar` passes these through `CalendarItemPrefillExtra` into `AddCalendarItemModal`'s initial state, which opens "More options" when repeat, tentative or important was inferred, so the guess is visible, never applied silently.
  - **Kind precedence** (most explicit first):
    1. **Deadline**: "the deadline for X is…", "deadline: …", "due", "overdue", "expires", "submit", "hand in", "lodge", "cut-off", "last day to", "applications close", or a date introduced by "by", "before" or "no later than" ("pay the bill by Friday").
    2. **Reminder**: "remind me", "don't forget", "remember to", "make sure to", "note to self", "ping me", "heads up".
    3. **Event**: a time range, an event word (the old list plus call, zoom, standup, 1:1, dentist, doctor, physio, gym, coffee, drinks, exam, tutorial and others), a journey, or a birthday.
    4. Otherwise a reminder, as before.
    - So "remind me that the deadline for the grant is Friday" is a Deadline (which notifies ahead anyway), and "remind me to book the flight" is a Reminder, not a journey.
  - **Other cues**:
    - **Travel**: flight, "fly to", "train/bus/coach to", ferry, "drive to", departs, boarding, layover, "uber/taxi to", commute. These make the event `eventType: 'travel'`.
    - **Birthday**: birthday, b-day. All-day, with any time dropped, and repeats yearly.
    - **Tentative**: maybe, possibly, perhaps, tentative(ly), provisional, TBC, TBD, "pencil in".
    - **Important**: urgent, important, asap, critical, "don't miss", "!!".
    - **Repeat**: "every day/week/month/year", "every Monday" (weekly), "every 3 days", "every other week", fortnightly (weekly ×2), daily/weekly/monthly/yearly/annually.
  - **Titles**: phrases that only say what kind of thing it is come out of the title with the date and time: the reminder phrases, the deadline lead-ins ("The deadline for the tax return is 31 October" → "The tax return"), the tentative words, a leading "Urgent:", "!!", and the repeat phrase. Then `tidyTitle()` drops a dangling "is", "due", "by" or "on" at the end, or a stray "for", "that" or "to" at the start, and restores a capital letter the original had. Words that are part of what the thing is ("Passport expires", "urgent" mid-sentence) stay.
  - **Decisions**:
    - The word "deadline" alone doesn't make a deadline. It must lead the text, be followed by "is"/":", or be "the deadline for X is…" (`DEADLINE_FOR` requires an "is" still to come). Otherwise "Meeting to discuss the deadline for the report" would stop being a meeting.
    - "Renew" alone isn't a deadline cue; "renew … by Friday" is, through the "by" rule.
    - A weekday in "every Monday" gives both the repeat and the date (the next Monday).
  - **Verified**: `utils/textToTask.test.ts` gained 28 cases: a table of kind + title per phrasing, deadline-over-reminder, reminder-over-event, "discuss the deadline" staying a meeting, a deadline keeping its time, travel, birthday (yearly, no time), tentative/important on and off, and five repeat phrasings. Full suite 784/784, `tsc -b` clean, no new eslint findings. **Not verified in a browser**: the modal opening pre-filled from a real note selection. That path is a state initialiser per field; worth one manual try (select "Remind me that the deadline for X is Friday" in a note, press Ctrl+Q, pick Calendar item).
  - **Not built**: a location from "at <place>" (too easily confused with "at 2pm"); a weekday set for "every weekday"; "all day" wording; picking the Endeavour from words in the text.

- [x] **Notes: a truncated note title extends out in its row on hover** (2026-09-28).
  - **What**: hovering a note title that's cut off with an ellipsis now shows the whole title in place, as if the row widened out to the right, instead of in a tooltip below the row. The title is drawn over the truncated one in the same font, colour and row background (hover or active tint included), running past the note column up to the window edge (then ellipsised). A soft shadow on its right edge marks where it spills over.
  - **How**: `TruncatedText` gained `reveal?: 'tooltip' | 'extend'` (default `'tooltip'`, unchanged for every other user). `'extend'` measures the span (`getBoundingClientRect`, `getComputedStyle` font/colour/letter-spacing) and the nearest non-transparent ancestor background, then portals a fixed, `pointer-events: none` copy to `document.body` (`.extended` in `TruncatedText.module.css`). `NoteList` passes `reveal="extend"`.
  - **Decision**: the old comment put the tooltip below the row so it wouldn't cover the row's inline hover buttons (NoteList's ↳/↰/✎/✕ still sit right of the title). The overlay ignores the pointer and shows only while the pointer is over the title text itself, so moving towards a button hides it before the pointer arrives.
  - **Scope**: note titles (`NoteList`) and, at the user's request the same day, notebook names in the Chronicle tree (`ChronicleView`). The tree's actions open from the row's ⋯ button (see "Row options menu", 2026-10-05), which the extended name doesn't cover. Sidebar, ManagePane, Lists and Records rows keep the tooltip; switching one is `reveal="extend"` at its call site.
  - **Verified**: headless Chromium against `vite preview`, light and dark, with a seeded long-titled note and a long notebook name. Both render in place over the row and past their column. A short title is unaffected, and the notebook's floating action menu stays visible below the extended name. No page errors. No automated test (UI hover behaviour; no Playwright suite yet).


- [x] **Fixed: moving between notes overwrote a tab with the note's Main content** (2026-09-30).
  - **Bug** (reported by the user as serious data loss): Alt+Left/Right to a note whose remembered tab (`uiStore.notesTabMemory`) wasn't Main showed tab 2 as active, but the editor held the note's **Main** content. The next save wrote Main over tab 2. Saves come from typing, clicking another tab (`switchTab` always flushes the open tab first), a cross-app "open this tab" request (it switches tabs the same way) and leaving the note. The same happened on any in-session note switch that restored a remembered tab, not only back/forward.
  - **Cause**: `NoteEditor`'s note-load effect restored the remembered tab id but always called `setContent(note.content)`. That mismatch dated from "revisiting a note lands back on its last tab". It didn't show on first mount (returning to Notes from another section) only because the lock/unlock effect runs on mount too and happened to reload the right tab.
  - **Fix**: `resolveNoteTab(note, tabId)` in `utils/noteTabs.ts` returns the tab id and its content **as a pair**. A missing tab resolves to Main for both the id and the content. All four places that load a tab into the editor now use it: the note-load effect, `switchTab`, the lock/unlock reload, and the requested-tab path (via `switchTab`). Now the editor can't mark one tab active while showing another tab's content.
  - **Verified**: `components/NoteEditor/NoteEditor.test.tsx` (new, jsdom and real Tiptap) covers two cases: back-navigating to a note on its remembered tab 2 and then clicking Main leaves both tabs' stored content intact, and first mount on a remembered tab shows that tab's content. The first case **fails on the pre-fix code** (checked). `utils/noteTabs.test.ts` covers the pairing and the fallback.
  - **Not recoverable by this fix**: tabs already overwritten before it. Their earlier content may still be in an automatic local backup snapshot (Account → automatic backups) or a manual export.
  - **Follow-up, same day: the per-note tab memory itself was unreliable.** After the fix above, the user reported that notes reopened on Main. `notesTabMemory`/`notesLastActiveTabId` used to be written by an effect *cleanup* (when the note or tab changed, it recorded the previous pair). That meant:
    - For the whole time a note was open, its memory held Main. React ran the cleanup of the render that had just opened the note, which still had the previous note's tab.
    - A reload or app restart while on that note forgot the tab, since `uiStore` persists that value.
    - During each switch it also briefly recorded the new note with the previous note's tab.

    Before the fix above, the restored tab *highlight* hid this. Now `NoteEditor`'s `setTabFor(noteId, tabId)` is the only way the active tab changes. It sets the state, the ref and both memory fields together, at the moment the tab becomes active. The leave-time mirror effect is gone.
    - **Verified**: headless Chromium against the dev server. Open A → tab 2 → B → Alt+Left, Alt+Right, Alt+Left, then reload and reopen A. The memory is correct at every step and A reopens on tab 2 with tab 2's content. `NoteEditor.test.tsx` gained the real-flow case: tab clicked, the app writes the memory itself, Alt+Left/Right. It also asserts the memory while the note is still open.

- [x] **Calendar `T` = today; Notes tree scrolls to the open note; new tab focuses its name; "Normal" clears all formatting** (2026-10-01).
  - **Calendar `T`**: jumps to today in whichever view is showing (month, week or day). It calls the Today button's own `goToday`. The handler sits in `CalendarView.tsx`'s component-local keydown effect beside `O`/`Tab`/arrows, with the same guards (not while typing, not while a calendar modal/pane is open). `hotkeys.ts` id `calendar-today`.
  - **Notes tree follows the open note**: `uiStore.openNote` already selected the note's notebook and expanded its ancestors. In a long tree the row was simply off-screen, so it looked as if nothing had happened (checked in headless Chromium with a notebook nested 3 deep under 25 others). The tree row (`ChronicleView`'s node, via its existing `anchorRef`) and the note row (`NoteList`'s `NoteRow`) now `scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' })` when they become selected/active. 'nearest' leaves an already-visible row where it is.
    - **Follow-up, same day: the fix above wasn't the reported problem.** The user meant that a notebook reached by *hovering* its parents open (ChronicleView's hover-expand), then clicked, collapsed out of sight as soon as the mouse left the tree. `setSelectedNoteTag` expanded nothing, and only opening a note expanded the path. `setSelectedNoteTag` now also runs `expandNotebookAncestors`, so selecting a notebook keeps the path to it open. Reproduced and confirmed in headless Chromium (hover University → Biology, click Genetics, move away: the path used to collapse and now stays). Test: `uiStore.behavior.test.ts` "setSelectedNoteTag — keeps the path to the selected notebook open". The scroll-into-view above stays; it's still useful in a long tree.
  - **New tab → name box → editor**: `handleAddTab` and Ctrl+T already opened the tab's rename box, but `switchTab` then focused the editor. Tiptap applies focus a frame late, so the editor took focus back from the box. `switchTab(tabId, { focusEditor: false })` is now used for a brand-new tab. Enter in the name box already moved into the editor.
  - **"Normal" clears formatting**: `extensions/normalText.ts`'s `setNormalText(editor)` is used by both Ctrl+H 0 and the style dropdown's Normal. It makes the block a paragraph *and* removes every formatting mark: bold, italic, underline, strike, code, colour/text style, super/subscript, and any formatting mark added later, since it's a keep-list, not a remove-list. It also clears the stored marks, so the next typed text is plain.
    - Scope: the selection, or with nothing selected the whole paragraph.
    - **Kept on purpose**: links, annotation tags (`noteTag`) and cross-app links (`artifactLink`). They carry meaning rather than style, and stripping a cross-app link would also raise the "remove the link too?" prompt.
  - **Verified**: `extensions/normalText.test.ts` (real Tiptap in jsdom: heading → paragraph with every mark gone, meaning-marks kept, selection-only scope). The scroll and tab-focus behaviour was checked in headless Chromium against the dev server (the focused element after "+", Enter and Ctrl+T; screenshots of the tree), not in the automated suite.

- [x] **Notes: a Title for each tab; the style dropdown follows the cursor** (2026-10-01).
  - **What**: each tab can have one **Title**: a line above Heading 1, larger and never numbered. Add it with **Ctrl+H then H** (first shipped as Ctrl+H then T, changed the same day at the user's request because T reads as "new tab", Ctrl+T) or **Title** in the style dropdown. It is inserted as the tab's first line, starting as the tab's name. If the tab already has one, either action jumps to it instead.
    - The Main tab's Title starts as the **note's** title, unless the Main tab has been renamed (`titlePrefillFor` in `utils/noteTabs.ts`).
    - After that the Title and the tab name are independent.
    - Enter in the Title continues in an ordinary paragraph.
  - **Decisions** (asked and confirmed by the user 2026-10-01; the alternatives are kept for the record):
    - Prefill once and then stay independent — rejected: always synced both ways, and synced until the title is first edited.
    - Always the top line, added by an action rather than by converting any line — rejected: any line, one per tab.
    - Main tab uses the note title — rejected: always the tab name.
  - **How**: `extensions/NoteTitle.ts` is its own node (`noteTitle`, rendered as `div[data-note-title]`), not a heading level. That way it picks up none of H1's numbering (`HeadingNumbering` counts only `heading`), the Academic/Highlight heading styles, or the table of contents.
    - The `insertOrFocusNoteTitle(prefill)` command inserts it at the first block of the first section, or selects the existing one.
    - An `appendTransaction` plugin keeps "one, at the top" whatever else happens: a Title arriving anywhere else (paste from another tab, drag, split) becomes Heading 1.
    - Enter is handled by the node itself: a direct `tr.split` into a paragraph. Chaining `splitBlock().setParagraph()` hit a stale-position RangeError in Tiptap.
  - **Dropdown fix (pre-existing bug, found while building this)**: the style dropdown never followed the cursor. It showed whatever it had at the last re-render, e.g. "Normal" inside a Heading 2, because `NoteEditor` doesn't re-render on selection-only changes. The dropdown's value and the columns picker's active count are now read through Tiptap's `useEditorState`.
  - **Verified**: `extensions/NoteTitle.test.ts` (real Tiptap with the section schema) covers the insert with prefill, one-per-tab, Enter → paragraph, a stray Title → Heading 1, and not being numbered. `utils/noteTabs.test.ts` covers `titlePrefillFor`. Headless Chromium against the dev server: Ctrl+H then the Title key on an extra tab and the dropdown's Title on Main both saved the right prefill, and the dropdown read `2` / `p` / `title` as the cursor moved.
  - **Not built**: the Title isn't shown in the table of contents or used as the tab's label anywhere else.


- [x] **Task links: waiting on / follow-up of / related, with reasons; blocked tasks; completion toast** (2026-10-01).
  - **What**: a task can be linked to another task with a type and an optional reason, and chained.
    - **Waiting on** (`dependsOn`): the task is *blocked*, greyed out with a count in the list (restyled the same day: light grey fill + grey outline, no padlock — see the "hover Complete" entry), until everything it waits on is completed or archived. The other task reads it as "Unlocks".
    - **Follow-up of** (`followUpOf`): where a task came from; the other task reads it as "Led to". A follow-up created before its origin is done waits for it too.
    - **Related**: a plain connection, reads the same from both ends.
  - **Completing a task** goes through `services/taskCompletion.ts` `toggleTaskCompletion`, which every UI path now uses (checkbox, swipe, task and calendar pane footers, sub-task rows, notifications).
    - A blocked task asks first: **Complete anyway** (primary, Ctrl+Enter; only this task) or **Complete all N**, which completes the whole chain upstream, furthest first.
    - Every completion shows a toast: "Completed “X” (+N)", what it **unlocked**, **+ Follow-up** and **Undo** (reopens everything that action completed).
  - **Requirements**: the user's request for task-to-task links with a reason, chaining ("rely on one or more finishing") and "completing one launches a new one". Merged with BACKLOG.md's "Task contingency / sequencing" (`blockedBy`, greyed with a lock icon), now built.
  - **Decisions (confirmed with the user 2026-10-01)**:
    - One generic link with a type and reason, stored on one side and derived on the other, with the kinds in ONE registry (`config/itemLinkKinds.ts`) and `targetType` so other item types can reuse it.
    - Blocked tasks stay greyed in place by default. A Settings → Display toggle ("Move blocked tasks out of the list", `settingsStore.hideBlockedTasks`, v5) instead puts them in a collapsed "🔒 Waiting on other tasks" group. The user asked for both with a setting.
    - Completing a blocked task is allowed with a warning, plus the option to complete the blockers too.
    - Planned follow-ups are real tasks that wait on their origin, not "recipes" created later.
    - The completion prompt is a non-blocking toast, not a question each time.
    - Archived counts as done.
    - The three kinds are fixed for now; user-defined kinds are later.
  - **Decisions (mine)**:
    - `followUpOf` blocks, rather than a follow-up carrying both `followUpOf` and `dependsOn`: one link, the right meaning.
    - A link to a deleted task is ignored rather than cleaned up, so a Recycling Bin restore brings it back.
    - The primary button of the blocked prompt is the one that affects the least (this task only).
    - The follow-up inherits Endeavour, tags and purposes like a sub-task does, but not priority.
  - **Files**:
    - Types: `ItemLink`/`ItemLinkKind`, `Task.itemLinks` (`types/index.ts`), `taskStore` v13 (`addItemLink`/`updateItemLinkReason`/`removeItemLink`).
    - Rules and config: `config/itemLinkKinds.ts`, `utils/taskLinks.ts` (all rules, pure), `services/taskCompletion.ts`.
    - Components: `components/TaskLinks/TaskLinksField.tsx` (TaskPane's "Task links" section: grouped rows, click to open, inline reason, remove; + Waiting on… / + Follow-up / + Related…) and `components/TaskPickerModal/`.
    - `AddTaskModal`: `uiStore.showAddFollowUp`/`pendingFollowUpOf` drive the "↳ Follow-up of" banner and link. `TaskItem`: 🔒 pill and dimmed title. `TaskList`: the optional blocked group.
    - Sync: migration `039_task_item_links.sql`, the `item_links` mapper, and `LABELS.taskLinks`.
  - **New suite-wide pieces** (recorded in CLAUDE.md "Component patterns"):
    - **Toasts**: `showToast()` + one `ToastHost`; the app had no toast before.
    - **`choiceDialog`**: a confirm with a third button (`dialogStore` results are now `'confirm' | 'alternate' | 'cancel'`).
    - **"Task completion — `toggleTaskCompletion()`"**: the one completion path.
    - Pattern tests for the last two.
  - **Verified**:
    - `utils/taskLinks.test.ts`: blocking incl. archived-as-done and follow-ups, missing targets ignored, deep blockers, unlock detection, self/duplicate/symmetric/loop refusal, both-direction relations.
    - `services/taskCompletion.test.ts`: prompt, cancel/anyway/all, Undo, unlocked detail, reopen.
    - `ConfirmDialog.test.tsx` (choiceDialog), `mappers.test.ts` (item_links round trip), `migrations.test.ts` (taskStore v13, settingsStore v5), `patterns.test.ts`.
    - Headless Chromium against the dev server: add a "Waiting on" link from the pane with a reason, the blocked pill in the list, the three-button prompt, "Complete all 2", the toast, and + Follow-up → the new task saved with a `followUpOf` link.
  - **Not built** (BACKLOG.md "Task links — follow-ons"): agent commands for links (agents can't see or make links; an agent completing a blocked task is simply allowed); linking an *existing* task as a follow-up or "unlocks" from this side; ticking the last linked-checklist item completes the task without the blocker prompt or toast; calendar shadows of a blocked task show no lock; user-defined link kinds; links from other item types (events, notes, list items) using the same `ItemLink` shape; recurring tasks (next session).
  - **Migration `039` is Pending**: until the user runs it, task upserts fail for the `tasks` table only; changes stay queued locally and sync once it's applied.

- [x] **Overview — everything, from every app, for a question** (2026-10-01). A new top-level section (top of the nav, above a divider, hotkey `0`). Each app contributes a source to one registry (tasks, events, reminders, deadlines, notes, lists, list items, tracker entries), all mapped onto one row: What / When / Status / Endeavour.
  - **Views**: every Endeavour gets an automatic "Everything for X" Overview. The user can save their own Overviews (sources, Endeavour, open/all, date window, title search, sort, grouping), which sync.
  - **Lists** gained a list-level Endeavour.
  - **Full write-up**: [`overview.md`](overview.md) (model, source mapping table, architecture rules, decisions, files, plan).
  - **Migration `040`** (new `overviews` table + `lists.collection_id`) is **Pending** until the user runs it.
  - **Verified**: `src/overview/engine.test.ts` and `mappers.test.ts`, and headless Chromium against the dev server (see "Manual check" in `overview.md`'s Verified section, filled in below).
  - **Fixed alongside**: sign-out's `clearSyncedLocalData` now also clears calendar deadlines.

- [x] **Tasks: hover Complete for "complete + follow-up" and more; a task's own parent/sub-tasks can't be linked** (2026-10-01).
  - **Hover options on Complete**: at the user's request, the choices the Completed toast offers afterwards can now be made up front. Hovering the task checkbox (TaskItem) or any pane footer's Complete button (task pane, and the event/reminder/deadline panes of a task's own calendar entries) shows:
    - **Complete + add follow-up**: completes, then opens the follow-up form. The toast then offers only Undo.
    - **Complete, with the N it's waiting on**: only when blocked; completes the chain without the prompt.
    - **Add a follow-up (keep this open)**.

    Plain click is unchanged.
  - **How**:
    - `services/taskCompletion.ts` gained `taskCompletionOptions(taskId)` (empty for a completed or archived task) and `toggleTaskCompletion(taskId, { thenFollowUp, withBlockers })`.
    - The new generic `components/HoverOptions/` wraps the button.
    - `ItemActionFooter` takes `completeOptions`.
    - `RowHoverActionsMenu` gained `align` (`'start'` for a trigger at a row's left edge), `estimatedHeight` and `className`, all defaulting to the old behaviour.
    - Desktop only: Android has no hover and keeps swipe-to-complete.
    - Picking "Complete + add follow-up" on a blocked task still asks the usual waiting-on question.
  - **Hierarchy links refused** (user request, same day): a task's ancestors and its whole sub-task tree are already connected to it, so `canAddLink` returns `'hierarchy'` for them (`utils/taskLinks.ts` `hierarchyIds`, the one place the rule lives), and `TaskLinksField` leaves them out of the picker.
  - **Verified**:
    - `taskCompletion.test.ts`: the option labels, "with blockers" skipping the prompt, "then follow-up" opening the form with the toast offering only Undo.
    - `taskLinks.test.ts`: ancestors and descendants refused, siblings fine.
    - Headless Chromium: the hover menu appears under the checkbox, and "Complete + add follow-up" on a blocked task raises the waiting-on prompt.

- [x] **Tasks: the open task is highlighted in the list; keyword-suggested pickers everywhere; blocked tasks restyled** (2026-10-01).
  - **Open task highlighted** (user report: clicking a linked task opened its pane but the list didn't show where it was):
    - `TaskItem` outlines the task open in the pane (`uiStore.editingTaskId`) and scrolls it into view. The scroll is delayed 260 ms, after the parent's 220 ms sub-task reveal, or it stops short.
    - `TaskList` derives, never stores, what must be shown for it: its parent chain is expanded, and its Completed / Archived / "Waiting on other tasks" group is open. Closing the pane puts the list back as it was.
    - Works however the task was opened: task links, Overview, notifications.
  - **Pickers suggest by keywords** (user request, made a suite-wide pattern): `utils/suggestRank.ts` (`rankSuggestions`/`rankSearch`) generalises `NotePickerModal`'s ordering. `TaskPickerModal` (suggestFrom = the linking task's title) and `ListPickerModal` (suggestFrom = the task/event/reminder/deadline title, via `CrossAppRefPicker`) use it, with "Suggested from “…”" / "Recent" labels (`LABELS.pickers`). Recorded in CLAUDE.md "Pickers suggest by keywords", with two pattern tests.
  - **Blocked tasks restyled** (user request): no padlock, since 🔒 means "encrypted" in this app.
    - The row gets a grey wash with diagonal stripes laid *over* it (a `::after` overlay; tokens `--color-blocked-wash`/`--color-blocked-stripe`, redefined for dark mode), plus a grey outline (`--color-border-strong`) on the top, right and bottom only. The first version was a plain grey fill under the tint, which the user found too hard to tell apart; the overlay, the same hatching idea as tentative calendar items, reads clearly on tinted rows too.
    - The Endeavour tint (an inline background-color) and the priority gradient (a background-image) still show over the fill, and the left edge keeps the Endeavour colour.
    - The pill reads "waits on N".
    - The "Waiting on" link kind and the blocked group use ⛓.
  - **Verified**: `utils/suggestRank.test.ts` (suggestions first by weight and rarity, recency tail, stems, typed search needs every word), and the pattern tests. Headless Chromium: a blocker that's a sub-task under a collapsed parent is revealed and outlined; a blocked task with an Endeavour and high priority keeps both colourings under the grey outline; the Related picker for "Book specialist" suggests "Book exam room".

- [x] **Android W2 — mobile primitives, back button, touch paths for hover actions** (2026-10-04). Workstream W2 (`docs/agent-tasks/06-android-w2-mobile-primitives.md`; gaps A3, A4, A5, A8, A10, B1, B2, I1 and D13's hotkey field in `docs/android/10-gap-analysis.md`). Rules: CLAUDE.md "Bottom sheets and touch gestures (Android)", "Escape key" and "Row hover-action menu".
  - **Back button = the Escape stack.** `closeTopOverlay()` exported from `src/hooks/useEscapeClose.ts` runs the top entry's handler and returns whether there was one. `App.tsx`'s `backButton` listener: `closeTopOverlay()` → `mobileBackConsumer` → `uiStore.navigateBack()` → `CapApp.minimizeApp()`. `navigateBack()` now returns `boolean`, so a note-level step in Notes counts (it used to check `sectionHistory.length` and minimise with notes still to go back through). `closeTopmostMobileOverlay()` is deleted. **Audit before deleting it:** every flag on its list (decrypt prompt, Quick Access, every modal, calendar quick-add, the task/event/reminder/deadline panes via `useItemActions`, tracker/routine/activity-type/notebook editors, `noteTagViewActive` via `NotesSection`, the side pane, Manage, Integrations, Recycling Bin, Account, Settings, `NoteEditorPane`, `MobileMoreSheet`) already registered `useEscapeClose`, so no component needed the call added.
  - **`BottomSheet`** (`src/components/BottomSheet/`) and **`ActionSheet`** + `ActionSheetButton` (`src/components/ActionSheet/`). The filter pickers' `'sheet'` variants and `MobileMoreSheet` now use `BottomSheet`; their duplicated `.sheetOverlay`/`.sheetPanel` CSS is gone. New token `--color-backdrop` (both themes).
  - **`useLongPress`** (`src/hooks/useLongPress.ts`, + `.module.css` for the Android-only `-webkit-touch-callout`/`user-select`) and **`useSwipeRow`** (`src/hooks/useSwipeRow.ts`; `startsAtEdge`, `lockAxis`, `clampOffset`, `swipeOutcome` exported pure). `haptics.ts` gained `hapticMedium`/`hapticWarning`.
  - **Long-press for hover actions.** `useRowHoverActions` attaches `useLongPress` on Android and disables its hover handlers there; `RowHoverActionsMenu` renders its children in a `BottomSheet` (new `title` prop; `onClose` now comes in `menuHandlers`). New `RowAction` (`RowHoverActions/RowAction.tsx`, context in `rowActionsContext.ts`) replaces the raw icon buttons at every site (`ChronicleView`, `ListsSection`, `ManagePane`, `OverviewSection`, `RecordsView`, `Sidebar`), with labels from the new `LABELS.rowActions`. `HoverOptions` (split into a wrapper + `WithOptions` so the long-press listener attaches even when options appear after first render) opens an `ActionSheet` on Android; `TaskItem` now passes its completion options on Android too.
  - **Swipe-left Archive / Delete with Undo** (`TaskItem`, decision D2). `services/undoableActions.ts`: `archiveTaskWithUndo` (store `archiveTask`, no reason; Undo = `restoreTask`) and `deleteTaskWithUndo` (`deleteTaskWithCleanup` at once; Undo restores from the Recycling Bin). Toast copy in `LABELS.swipeActions`.
  - **Decision: how Undo finds what to restore.** Not by having `moveToTrash` return an id: a task delete is a cascade (sub-tasks, the shadow event and deadline each get their own entry), and restoring only the task would leave the rest in the bin and the task pointing at a missing deadline. `services/trash.ts` gained `trashedBy(run)` (the entries a delete created, by diffing the trash store's keys; insertion order = creation order) and `restoreAllFromTrash(ids)` (newest first, so a parent is back before its sub-tasks re-attach). That needed the rule change "`services/trash.ts` is never imported by a store or by `trashCapture.ts`" (was "only by `RecyclingBinPane`"), now a pattern test.
  - **Toast above the bottom chrome**: `--mobile-nav-h` (defined in `MobileNav.module.css`, used by `MobileNav` and `MobileQuickAddBar`), `--quick-add-h` (set on `<html>` by a `ResizeObserver` in `MobileQuickAddBar` while mounted). **`TruncatedText`** clamps to two lines on Android and doesn't react to the emulated hover.
  - **Hotkeys:** `HotkeyDef.touch: string` (required), filled for all 51 entries.
  - **Bugs found and fixed on the emulator:** (1) `AddTaskButton`'s speed dial also opened on CSS `:hover`, which sticks after a tap on touch screens, so its options stayed open over the list; now `@media (hover: hover)` only. (2) The speed dial's flex column is as tall as its hidden options and covered the bottom-right of the task list, swallowing taps and swipes even when closed (on desktop too); the column is now `pointer-events: none` with the FAB and shown options `auto`. (3) The Lists sheet had no title (`title={list.name}` added).
  - **Tests:** `useEscapeClose.test.ts` (closeTopOverlay order and return; now `cleanup()`s between tests), `uiStore.behavior.test.ts` (navigateBack's return, including a note-level step with no section history), `useLongPress.test.ts`, `useSwipeRow.test.ts`, `undoableActions.test.ts` (cascade delete + Undo leaves the bin as it was), and pattern tests: no hand-rolled sheet CSS, no raw `<button>` in a `RowHoverActionsMenu`, every hotkey has `touch`, `services/trash.ts` stays out of stores, and back is the Escape stack.
  - **Verified on the emulator / desktop, and not verified:** see "W2 — mobile primitives" in `docs/android/implementation-status.md` (verified list there). Desktop, in headless Chrome against the production build with real mouse events: the checkbox hover options, the Manage row hover menu (Edit, then Escape closes the modal, then Manage), the Endeavour filter as a dropdown (not a sheet), and Escape closing the delete dialog before the task pane all behave as before; no swipe wrapper on desktop. **Not verified:** haptics (no real device), real system edge-back gestures, Records rows on a phone (no Records Android layout yet).

- [x] **Android W1: platform services — `/api/*`, OAuth return, file export, soft keyboard** (2026-10-04). Brief `docs/agent-tasks/05-android-w1-platform-services.md`; gaps A1, A2, A7, A9 in `docs/android/10-gap-analysis.md`; branch `android/w1-platform-services`. Detail and the emulator log: `docs/android/implementation-status.md` "W1 — platform services".
  - **`/api/*` on Android** (`utils/apiFetch.ts`): an Android branch calls `PRODUCTION_API_ORIGIN + path` through `CapacitorHttp` (native, no CORS) and adapts the result into a standard `Response`, so no caller changed. It covers exactly what callers use: string bodies (JSON — passed parsed, because CapacitorHttp serialises JSON itself and would re-quote a string), plain-object headers, and `ok`/`status`/`headers`/`json()`. `CapacitorHttp` is deliberately *not* enabled globally in `capacitor.config.ts`: that would patch every `fetch`, Supabase's included. New pattern test: no `fetch('/api…` outside `apiFetch.ts`.
  - **OAuth return** (decision D5): `oauthRedirectOrigin()` (beside `PRODUCTION_API_ORIGIN`) gives the production origin under Android and Tauri for `redirect_uri`. Connect buttons call `openOAuthFlow(provider, url)` (`services/oauthState.ts`): a page navigation on web/desktop as before; on Android `@capacitor/browser`, resolving when the flow returns or the browser is closed, after which the button refreshes its status. `mintOAuthState` mints with `p_client: 'android'` on Android (migration 041) and falls back to the one-argument call if that fails, so nothing breaks before the migration runs. The callbacks (`api/*-oauth-callback.ts`, via `api/_lib/oauthReturn.ts`) send a web flow to `/?strava=…` / `/?googleCalendar=…` exactly as before and an Android flow to `organisaitor://oauth-done?provider=…&status=…&reason=…`, served as a small page that redirects by script and also shows a "Return to Organisaitor" link (if the browser ever refuses to open the app without a tap). On failure they call `discard_oauth_state` to learn the client (and consume the nonce); unknown → web. `services/android/deepLinks.ts` is the one `appUrlOpen` router (`registerDeepLink(host, handler)`, plus the cold-start launch URL, with a short duplicate guard); `services/android/oauthReturn.ts` registers `oauth-done`: settle the waiting button, `Browser.close()`, go to Fitness / Calendar + side pane (as the web return does), sync Google calendars, toast (`LABELS.oauthReturn`). One new Android-gated effect in `App.tsx` starts both. `AndroidManifest.xml` registers the `organisaitor` scheme on `MainActivity`.
  - **Saving files** (`utils/saveFile.ts`): `saveFile(filename, blob)` — anchor download on web/Tauri (moved from `backupExport.ts`), on Android write to `Directory.Cache` (base64) and open the share sheet; dismissing the sheet isn't an error. `downloadBackup`/`downloadAutoBackupSnapshot` use it, so Account, Integrations, auto-backup and the ErrorBoundary all do. Installed `@capacitor/filesystem` and `@capacitor/share` (`file_paths.xml` already had a `cache-path`). Also fixed `ErrorBoundary.exportBackup`, which wrapped the async `downloadBackup()` in a sync `try/catch` and so never showed "Couldn't export a backup".
  - **Soft keyboard**: no layout change needed — with the existing `Keyboard: { resize: 'body' }` the Tasks quick-add bar (with its chips), the calendar quick-add sheet and AddTaskModal's bottom field all stay above the keyboard. Both quick-add title inputs now have `enterKeyHint="done"` (the keyboard shows ✓).
  - **Decisions**: the client is recorded with the nonce (as the brief recommended) rather than encoded in `state` or the `redirect_uri` — the redirect URI must match what's registered with Google, and the nonce is already the trusted record. Added `discard_oauth_state` (not in the brief) so a provider error (user declined) also returns to the app. The callback returns a page, not a bare 302 to the custom scheme, so there's a tappable fallback; on the emulator the script redirect worked without a tap. Save functions return `'ok:android'` rather than a new return type, keeping signatures and the web `'ok'` unchanged (ADR-9).
  - **Tests**: `utils/apiFetch.test.ts`, `utils/saveFile.test.ts`, `services/android/deepLinks.test.ts` (router + `oauth-done` route + `openOAuthFlow`), `api/oauth-callbacks.test.ts` (both callbacks: web/Android success, provider error, migration not run, rejected state), and the pattern test.
  - **Verified on the emulator** (Pixel 8 API 35): Portfolio ticker search and quote; backup export → share sheet with a valid JSON file; Restore opens the system file picker; `oauth-done` warm and cold start; the full in-app-browser round trip against a local stand-in for the provider and callback (real callback HTML); the three keyboard checks and the Done key. **Not verified**: real Google Calendar or Strava connect (needs migration 041 run, the callbacks deployed, and a signed-in account — the emulator was a guest); ICS import (its pane is unreachable on Android, gap I7); Tauri (not run). Web behaviour is covered by the unit tests (unchanged redirect URLs, anchor download), not re-checked by hand.

Tauri's connect flow still navigates the app window to the provider (BACKLOG "Desktop (Tauri) and Android API access").
- [x] **Fixes from the first phone test: calendar whole-day rows, note tab deletion, backup restore of Notes** (2026-10-05). Reported by the user after installing the W1+W2 APK.
  - **Calendar whole-day zone filled the screen on Android.** On a phone the week view's day columns are ~50px wide, and `.weekViewItem .calItemTitle` wraps with `word-break: break-word` in rows with no height limit. A long untimed title (deadline, reminder) broke a letter or two per line and pushed the hourly grid off the screen. Fix, Android-scoped in `CalendarView.module.css`: titles clamp to two lines (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`); `.weekSpanRowWrap` capped at 4.5rem and `.weekUntimedRowWrap`/`.dayUntimedRow` at 7.5rem, each scrolling inside itself. Tapping an item still opens it. Desktop unchanged.
  - **Note tabs: deleted with one tap, no confirmation, on every platform.** The tab's × was titled "Close tab" but called `removeNoteTab`, which deletes the tab and its content permanently (not via the Recycling Bin). Android: the × is no longer rendered (Notes on a phone is read-first, D11; the user asked for no tab deletion on the phone). Desktop: it's titled "Delete tab" and asks `confirmDelete('tab', name, …)` first (CLAUDE.md: every delete confirms). `NoteEditor.tsx`.
  - **Restoring a backup didn't restore Notes (signed in).** `restoreBackupData` (`utils/backupExport.ts`) wrote every key back, but rehydrated only `taskStore`/`calendarStore`/`trackerStore` before `forceUpload`, a list written before the other stores synced. `forceUpload` reads live store state, so it uploaded the *current* notes, lists, schedules, portfolio, overviews and trash over the cloud copy, and the reload's pull brought them back: the restore was silently undone for those stores. Fix: rehydrate all nine synced stores. Backups themselves always contained Notes (`notes-storage` is read from IndexedDB via `readPersistedValue`). Tests: `src/utils/backupExport.test.ts` (fails without the fix); pattern test "backup restore rehydrates every synced store" (every store `syncService.ts` imports must be in the rehydrate list).
  - **Not fixed (logged in BACKLOG.md, needs a decision):** with two devices, a restore can still be undone by the *other* device. Restored records keep their old `updatedAt`, so a device holding a newer copy pushes it back on its next load (`queueLocalOnlyAndNewer`).
  - **Verified:** `tsc -b`, full Vitest suite (899). **Not verified:** on the phone or emulator (calendar layout, tab ×, a real restore round trip).

Visits are recorded in `uiStore.openEditSchedule`, so schedules opened from the side pane show in Recent/Frequent too. Labels: `LABELS.schedule`, `LABELS.quickAccessScheduleHidden`; the placeholder now names schedules.

New `RowOptionsMenu` (`components/RowHoverActions/RowOptionsMenu.tsx`): a "⋯" at the row's right end, shown while the row is hovered (20px always reserved, so nothing shifts). Hovering the "⋯" (or clicking it) opens the actions laid over the button and running right (`RowHoverActionsMenu`'s new `align='beside'`; it runs left within 160px of the window edge). `useRowHoverActions` gained `longPressRef` (Android long-presses the row, since no ⋯ is shown there) and `openNow`.

Now a keyboard-opened note (`kbOpenedNoteId`, plus a zeroed focus signal so a freshly mounted `NoteEditor` doesn't focus either) leaves focus where it is. `→` or `` Ctrl+` `` enters the editor; clicking a note, Quick Access, links and back/forward still focus it.

- [x] **Row options menu moved to the left, as a dropdown; long-title row background; duplicate calendar links; Ctrl+H→H; Alt+Shift+↓ duplicates a line** (2026-10-05, second round the same day).
  - **Row options menu moved to the left.** The right-hand ⋯ expanded out over the next column, which the user didn't like. `RowOptionsMenu` is now each row's leading icon slot (a fixed 22px, empty for rows with no icon so all titles line up). On row hover the icon becomes "⋯"; hovering the ⋯ drops the actions down as a single narrow column of icon buttons (`RowHoverActionsMenu`'s `align='dropdown'`, replacing `'beside'`; `.menuColumn` forces the site buttons to 22px). The column covers only the icon slots below, never a title. It now also draws `.rowTail`, a full-row-height strip of the row's background from the row's right edge to the end of the text, which carries the edge shadow. Also fixed: the row's hover colour is still fading in (NoteList rows transition `background`) at the moment of `mouseenter`, so it was read as transparent and the column's white was used instead. `rowBackground` now reads each ancestor with its transition suspended.

Tests in `NoteEditor.test.tsx` (Ctrl held, and released), on the real editor; the held case fails without the fix.

    - A selection over several lines in the same container copies them all.

Escape with the list open closes only the list.

Switching keeps the typed name/title (`uiStore.createDraft`). `AddNoteTagModal` is now keyed by `pendingNoteTagKind` in App.tsx, so Notebook ↔ Tag remounts it fresh. Designed to grow: a new kind is one registry entry plus the switcher in its pane (CLAUDE.md "Creation panes: the switcher").
