- [x] **Note blocks: Hierarchy and Pyramid** (2026-10-07).
  - **What and why:** the user asked for a tree hierarchy block, with tier labels (e.g. taxonomic ranks) shown to the side or the top depending on orientation and several designs, and a pyramid block "unless there's a real argument against" (there wasn't: it's a common, distinct structure: Maslow, food chains, funnels).
  - **Hierarchy** (`\hierarchy`, also `\tree`, `\taxonomy`, `\ranks`, `\orgchart`): `extensions/Hierarchy.ts`. Items are stored flat, each with a `level`, and written as an outline. Enter adds an item (its first child if it has children, else a sibling; into a waiting empty item first; the text after the cursor goes with it). Tab / Shift+Tab move the item and its children a level (never more than one below the item before it). Enter on an empty item moves it up a level, then out of the block. The pill's ⇤ / ⇥ do the same on touch. **Level names** (`tiers`) are named in a popover (the pill's "Levels", a click on a level name, or the right-click menu). They show down the left (Tree), across the top (Columns) or in the margin (Outline, once per run of the same level). `extensions/hierarchyLayout.ts` reads the tree from the levels: effective levels, parents, each item's span across the leaves, its place among its siblings. Designs: **Tree** (top-down; one CSS grid with rows = levels, columns = leaves, connectors from each item's pseudo-elements), **Columns** (the same grid turned left-to-right), **Outline** (an indented list with elbows). Colours: one, or one per level.
  - **Pyramid** (`\pyramid`, also `\layers`, `\funnel`, `\maslow`): `extensions/Pyramid.ts`. At least two layers, top first, each a label and a note (keys like the breakdown's). Each label is drawn on its shape (a clip-path from the widths at its edges, `layerWidths`) and kept inside it; notes sit in a column beside it, which goes away when no layer has one. Designs: **Pyramid**, **Funnel** (narrowing to a spout), **Stacked** (rounded bars stepping out). Colours: one (deeper towards the top) or Spectrum.
  - **Both** follow Block designs (pill, Design ▸, frames, Select block) and are in `NOTE_BLOCK_KINDS`, `NOTE_BLOCK_NODES`, the right-click menu (`hierarchyMenu.ts`, `pyramidMenu.ts`) and copy as text (`noteClipboardText`: an indented list with the level names; "Pyramid, top to bottom:" and a numbered list). Labels `LABELS.noteBlocks.hierarchy` / `.pyramid`.
  - **Bugs found and how:** with Playwright: the branch lines ran past the first and last siblings (a `:not()` made the general rule more specific than the first/last ones); the pyramid's top label broke mid-word; an empty note's placeholder sat a line low (after the empty line's `<br>`; now laid over it).
  - **Verified:** `extensions/Hierarchy.test.ts`, 7 tests (layout, effective levels and subtrees, writing an outline with Enter/Tab/Shift+Tab, children moving with their parent, leaving the block, level names and copy-as-text, round trips; pyramid keys, minimum layers, shapes, round trip); `Breakdown.test.ts`'s frames test now covers both blocks. In the real app with Playwright: every design, light and dark. **Not verified:** Android (gap F18).
- [x] **One history, and the history browser (Alt+N)** (2026-10-07).
  - **What and why:** the user asked for a visually appealing, animated way to scroll back through history, detailed enough to tell places apart down to the note, with Notes' own note-level history and the app-wide section history made one.
  - **What a stop is** (the user's answers, 2026-10-07): Notes, every note (its tab and notebook too); Tasks and Lists, one stop per visit, returning to the task or list you were on; Calendar, one stop per visit, returning to the period and view you were looking at (week of 10–16 Dec, January's month view); Records its tracker or routine; Overview its selection; other sections one stop.
  - **How:** `store/navHistory.ts` (`NavPlace`, `pushPlace`, `travel`, `MAX_NAV_HISTORY` 40) and, at the bottom of `store/uiStore.ts`, `capturePlace` (where you are now) and `goToPlace` (sets the section's own "where was I" memory, then switches with `mode: 'silent'`). `uiStore.navHistory` / `navForward` replace `sectionHistory` and `notesHistory`. A normal `setActiveView`, or leaving a note in Notes (`openNote`, `closeNote`, changing notebook), pushes a place; `navigateBack` / `navigateForward` are `travelHistory(-1 / 1)`. Back from Tasks now returns to the note you left, not just to Notes. uiStore v3 migrates the old section stops into places and drops the note stops.
  - **The browser:** `components/HistoryBrowser/` (Alt+N, `action-history`, customizable, works from inside a text field; More → History on Android). A full-screen blurred overlay with a 3-D carousel: older stops to the left, "You are here" in the middle, anything you went back from to the right. Each card shows its section (icon from `NavSidebar/navItems.tsx` `SECTION_ICONS`, a hue per section), how long ago, where it sits (notebooks, an Endeavour, a parent task), the title, a detail (the tab, the calendar view, a due date, an item count) and a preview of the text (`describePlace.ts`, read live, encrypted items as "Encrypted"). Wheel, ← → / Home / End or a swipe moves; Enter, or a click on any card (changed 2026-10-08: it used to be the centred card only, a click on another centred it), goes there through `travelHistory`, so every stop in between stays in history. Esc closes (`useEscapeClose`).
  - **Also:** `CORE_NAV_ITEMS` and the section icons moved to `NavSidebar/navItems.tsx` (component files stay component-only for fast refresh; the lint count fell by one).
  - **Verified:** `store/uiStore.behavior.test.ts` rewritten for the one history (16 history tests: push/clear, back/forward, cap, Calendar's period and view, Tasks' task and Lists' list, `travelHistory` several stops, `travel` itself, a stop per note, notes and sections sharing a stack, notebook changes and closing a note, the quick-view pane not being a stop). In the real app with Playwright, light and dark: the carousel after visiting a note, Calendar, Tasks and Records, moving through it, and Enter on the note's card reopening the note. **Not verified:** Android (gap F20).
- [x] **Charts in notes (`\chart`)** (2026-10-07).
  - **What and why:** the user asked for charting, simplest option first but kept neutral so the library can be changed later (with a possible spreadsheet app in mind), data typed in by hand or pasted as CSV; and asked for waterfall and 100% stacked column/line charts.
  - **Design (CLAUDE.md "Charts"):** `src/charts/chartSpec.ts` is the library-neutral `ChartSpec` and the pure data helpers; `src/charts/chartAdapter.ts` is the only module that knows Chart.js (4.5, new dependency), loading it with `import('chart.js/auto')` on the first chart shown — the build puts it in its own chunk.
  - **The block:** `extensions/Chart.ts`, one atom node (`variant` = the chart type, `spec`, frames) with its own view. Types (the block's designs): Column, Bar, Line, Area, Pie, Doughnut, Scatter (labels read as x), Waterfall (each value a change from the running total, floating bars green/red, an optional Total bar). Several series can be side by side, stacked, or 100% stacked (the pill's stacking button and the right-click menu; column, bar, line, area). `\chart` (also `\graph`, `\plot`) inserts an empty chart with its table open.
  - **Data in:** the table under the chart (title, values-axis title, the labels' heading, series names, cells; + Row, + Series, × on hover). Enter goes down a row (adding one at the end). Typing redraws at once and saves after half a second or on leaving a cell. Pasting a table: **Paste data…** (a box, prefilled with the current data, Ctrl+Enter to use), into the headings (replaces everything), or into a body cell (fills from that cell, adding rows and series). Spreadsheet copies (tabs), CSV with quotes, semicolons; numbers like 1,234 / $12 / 45% / (30) / 12,5. The table opens with the pill's **Data**, a double-click on the chart, Enter on a selected chart, or the right-click menu; Esc closes it. Copying a chart gives its title and a tab-separated table.
  - **Files:** `src/charts/chartSpec.ts`, `src/charts/chartAdapter.ts`, `src/charts/ChartWidget.ts` + `ChartWidget.module.css` (the chart, data table and paste box, for any app), `components/NoteEditor/extensions/Chart.ts` (the block: node, `\chart`, pill, frames, selection, hosting the widget), `chartMenu.ts`, `objects/chartBlock.ts`, the block's spacing and pill in `NoteEditor.module.css` ("Chart"), labels `LABELS.charts` (shared) and `LABELS.noteBlocks.chart` (the block's own).
  - **Moved out of Notes, 2026-10-08:** the user pointed out charts will be reused in other apps (Records next), so the editing half moved from the note block into `ChartWidget`, a framework-free class any host mounts (`onCommit`, `onEditingChange`, `onEscape`). The paste box became an inline panel instead of the note editor's block popover, so it needs nothing from Notes. Behaviour is unchanged; `Chart.test.ts` and the Playwright screenshots were re-run.
  - **Bugs found and how:** with Playwright: the pill covered the chart title (room added above the chart); the pie and doughnut icons drew as solid dots (the pill fills SVG circles, so they're paths now).
  - **Verified:** `charts/chartSpec.test.ts` (22: numbers, pasted tables, CSV quoting and semicolons, headings, round trip, 100% shares, waterfall steps, normalising stored data), `charts/chartAdapter.test.ts` (5: column/bar, 100% stacking on bars and areas, waterfall, pie and scatter, stacking ignored where meaningless), `extensions/Chart.test.ts` (3, with drawing faked: `\chart` and typing, pasting into the headings and copy as text, JSON/HTML round trip and the stacking button). In the real app with Playwright, light and dark: the empty chart, pasting a four-quarter table, side by side, 100% stacked columns and areas, line, pie, doughnut, waterfall, bar. **Not verified:** Android (gap F21).
  - **Not built:** BACKLOG.md "Charts in notes".
- [x] **Calendar items: a new note from the pane, and linked notes' abstracts** (2026-10-08).
  - **What and why:** the user asked for (1) making a new note from a calendar item, choosing its notebook with the same search as linking (whose suggestions they rate highly), or choosing a note to add a tab to, linked like any note link; and (2) a linked note's abstract shown in the item's notes, editable there and saved to the note, without losing anything typed in the item's own notes.
  - **New note:** "+ New note" in the event, reminder and deadline panes' Linked items (`CrossAppRefPicker`'s new `newNoteTitle` prop). It opens `NotePickerModal` in **place mode** (`mode="place"`, `onPlace`): the same search and keyword suggestions from the item's title, with the notebooks among the results (matched by name and the notebooks above them) and each row labelled "New note in" / "New tab in"; "New note outside any notebook" at the end. `services/noteFromItem.ts` `createNoteForItem` makes the note in the notebook (`addNote`, which also takes the notebook's Endeavour) or a tab of the note (`addNoteTab`) and returns the `CrossAppRef` (with `tabId` for a tab), which is added to the item's `crossAppRefs` — so the note's "Linked from" bar shows the item, as with any link. The name is the item's title and date (`newNoteTitleFor`: "History lecture · Oct 8, 2026"), so a weekly lecture's notes can be told apart. A toast confirms it, with Open.
  - **Abstracts:** `components/LinkedNoteAbstracts`, under the notes field of the three panes: each linked note that has an abstract (once, however many of its tabs are linked; never a locked encrypted note), as a box headed "From the note “…”" (a click opens the note), its text editable in place and saved to the note on leaving it (`updateNote`, which re-encrypts an encrypted note). **The item's own notes are never merged with it** (the user's question: what happens to notes typed in the item): they stay the item's, in the field above; the abstract stays the note's. A change made in the note shows in the box unless it's being typed in.
  - **Also:** `NoteEditor` now takes a changed abstract from the store when its abstract box isn't focused (it kept its own copy, and a later save would have written the old text back over an edit made from the calendar). `CrossAppRefPicker`'s × now removes only the link it's on: a note linked whole and by a tab was removed twice over (found in the Playwright run).
  - **Decisions:** the picker gained a mode rather than a second picker, as the user asked, so suggestions work the same. Offered in the calendar panes only, as asked; the task pane and the add-task form could take it with the same prop. Rejected: copying the abstract into the item's notes (two copies drift apart) and putting it in the notes field (it would mix with what the user typed).
  - **Verified:** `services/noteFromItem.test.ts` (4: note in a notebook, outside any, a tab with its `tabId`, the name and date regardless of time zone) and `components/LinkedNoteAbstracts/LinkedNoteAbstracts.test.tsx` (2: which notes show, once; editing saves to the note, and a change in the note shows while open). In the real app with Playwright: linking a note with an abstract shows it; "+ New note" suggested the right note and notebook first; picking the note added a tab and linked it, with the toast; an edit to the abstract in the calendar appeared in the note. **Not verified:** Android, though nothing here is hover-only (gap analysis not needed: plain buttons and textareas).
- [x] **Note tabs: created and modified dates on hover** (2026-10-08).
  - **What and why:** the user asked to see a tab's date created and modified bottom left (where a link's address shows) while hovering its heading, and whether it needed a migration.
  - **How:** each tab heading carries `data-link-preview` with `tabDatesText(note, tabId)` (`utils/noteTabs.ts`), which the app-wide `LinkHoverPreview` shows bottom left: "Created 8 Oct 2026, 09:00 · Modified 8 Oct 2026, 10:30".

The main tab's created date is the note's; its modified date is the new `Note.mainTabUpdatedAt`, stamped by `updateNote` when `content` changes — that one needs a column: migration `047_note_tab_dates.sql` (noteStore v13 backfills null). Tabs from before today, and a main tab untouched since, say "not recorded". Encrypted notes: the tab dates travel inside the encrypted tabs; `mainTabUpdatedAt` is plaintext metadata like `updatedAt`.
  - **Verified:** `store/noteTabDates.test.ts` (3: a new note, the main tab stamped by content only, a tab stamped when added and by content not rename), `utils/noteTabs.test.ts` (the text, "not recorded"), `mappers.test.ts` round-trips both. In the real app with Playwright: hovering the Main tab showed both dates. **Not verified:** Android (gap F22: no hover).
- [x] **Greek letters by name in every text field (`//name`)** (2026-10-08).
  - **What and why:** the user wanted an easier way to write Greek letters, starting a set of special characters, "the same way everywhere — any text field", and asked which key to use and whether a library would do it.
  - **Decisions:** `//` (the user's own suggestion): `\\` already types a backslash and `\` makes blocks; `//` starts only at the start of a word, so URLs never trigger it. No library: nothing does in-app expansion for web fields (Tiptap's Typography covers arrows and ©; OS tools live outside the app), and the work is a lookup table plus two small hooks.
  - **How:** CLAUDE.md "Special characters". `src/specialChars/charSets.ts` (24 letters with capitals, six variant forms, look-alike aliases w → ω, f → φ, h → η, v → ν; `findCharTrigger`, `matchChars`, `exactChar`), `charPicker.ts` (the list under the cursor), `fieldInput.ts` (every input and textarea, from one listener set installed in App.tsx; caret position from a mirror of the field), `NoteEditor/extensions/SpecialCharInput.ts` (the note editor; not in code). Hotkey entry `special-chars`; labels `LABELS.specialChars`.
  - **Verified:** `specialChars/specialChars.test.ts` (5: the trigger at word starts and never in a URL; matching, capitals, aliases, variants; a textarea — Enter puts the letter in, ↓ and Tab, the whole name and a space, Esc keeps the text and doesn't reach the document), `extensions/SpecialCharInput.test.ts` (3: name and space, Enter, not in code or a URL), pattern tests (installed once, the extension registered, no other table of letters). In the real app with Playwright: `//th` → θ, `//Delta ` → Δ and `//lambda ` → λ in a note; `//ps` → ψ in the add-task title, saved in the task. **Not verified:** Android (gap F22).
  - **Not built:** other sets (arrows, maths symbols, sub/superscript digits); a way to see the whole table (Settings, or a help card).
- [x] **Notes and navigation round: history cards, account icon, dev tools switch, notebook tree memory, block delete and picking, note backdrops** (2026-10-08). Requested together by the user.
  - **History browser:** a click on any card goes there (was: only the centred one; others were centred first). `HistoryBrowser.tsx`.
  - **Account icon:** the header button was `◎`, which reads as nothing in particular and is also the Purpose icon. Now `AccountIcon` (a person in a circle, Lucide's circle-user) from `components/Icons`, per "Icons come from one place".
  - **Dev tools no longer open on launch** of a dev build. `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`: `OPEN_DEVTOOLS_ON_START` (false), or `ORGANISAITOR_DEVTOOLS=1` in the environment for one run. Documented under Tech stack in CLAUDE.md. Verified with `cargo check`; not run as `tauri dev`.
  - **The Notes tree reopens as it was left:** `uiStore.expandedNoteTagIds` added to `partialize` (uiStore v4, cumulative migrate backfills `[]`). Deleted notebooks' ids stay in the list harmlessly (the tree only looks ids up). Tests: `uiStore.behavior.test.ts` "session memory".
  - **Deleting a whole block:** Delete block in every block's right-click menu, and a trash button at the end of its pill shown only while the block is selected (`blockDesigns.ts` `deleteBlock`; `selectButton` now returns Select + Delete, so every block got it at once). Ctrl+Z restores.
  - **Ctrl+Alt+click** in a block selects it whole. **Ctrl+click** picks parts of a block: `extensions/blockPicks.ts` (`NoteBlockPicks`, priority 1000 so a block's own Backspace handling doesn't run first; every handler is a no-op without picks). Picked parts get an outline (`[data-block-picked]`); Delete/Backspace deletes them, Ctrl+C/Ctrl+X copy/cut them as text, right-click offers Copy / Delete N selected / Clear. Any edit or a plain click clears the picks. Ctrl+click on a link or linked item inside a block keeps its old meaning (select its text).
    - **Decisions:** what a block "item" is comes from one map, `BLOCK_PART_NODES` (timeline entries, quote fields, cycle stages, breakdown parts, hierarchy items, pyramid layers; the chart has none). Whether a delete is allowed comes from the block's schema, not a per-block minimum: picking every part deletes the block, anything leaving it invalid (a cycle needs two stages) is refused with an `alertDialog`. Deleting a hierarchy parent leaves its children at their levels (no re-parenting). The cursor stays where it was while picking (the mousedown is consumed), so typing clears the picks rather than replacing them.
    - **Verified:** `blockPicks.test.ts` (8: every block names its part type; pick, delete, undo; un-pick and Esc; edits clear picks; the cycle refusal; all parts → whole block; `deleteBlock`); in the real app (Chrome via Playwright against the dev server): two timeline entries picked and outlined, Delete removed them, Ctrl+Alt+click selected the block and showed the pill's trash button. Not tried on every block type by hand.
  - **Note backdrops:** the room below the end of a note (was `.editorWrap::after`) is now `NoteEditor/NoteBackdrop.tsx`. Hovering it shows an `ImageIcon` button in its middle (always shown on touch); the picker offers Plain (the old grey), eight presets and "Use your own picture…" (compressed by `compressImageBlob`, 1600 px WebP, stored as a data URL), plus Remove picture. One choice for every note: `settingsStore.noteBackdrop` (v7), so it's per device and goes into backups with the settings.
    - **Presets** (`config/noteBackdrops.ts`, looks in `NoteBackdrop.module.css` under `[data-backdrop]`, one rule for the area and its swatch, pure CSS gradients, no image files): Aurora (dark green and dark purple glows on black — the user's example), Lattice (green and purple diagonal lines on black), Ripples (green and purple rings on black), Dusk, Blueprint, Ocean, Paper, Sunset. Fixed colours in both themes, on purpose.
    - **Not built:** a picture per note or per notebook; syncing the picture across devices (it's a device setting; a picture in settings would also make every settings sync heavy if settings ever sync); moving the picture out of localStorage (a 1600 px WebP is usually 100–300 KB, inside the guarded quota).
    - **Verified:** settings migration test (v6 → v7); in the real app: the hover button, the picker, Aurora / Lattice / Ripples applied. Upload not exercised (file chooser).
  - **Also fixed:** `patterns.test.ts`'s "note editor has SpecialCharInput" regex required LF line endings, so it failed on this Windows checkout (`core.autocrlf=true`) even at HEAD; it now accepts CRLF.
  - **Android:** the backdrop and block delete work by tap (the pill shows on touch); history card taps already worked. Picking parts has no touch path yet: gap F23 in `docs/android/10-gap-analysis.md`.
- [x] **Ctrl+H on normal text makes Heading 1; a held Ctrl no longer cancels the sequence** (2026-10-08/09). `NoteEditor.tsx`, the editor-hotkeys effect.
  - **Heading 1 at once:** the user wanted Ctrl+H alone to make Heading 1 from normal text (Ctrl+H, 1 was redundant there), while Ctrl+H then 1–5 still sets any level, including on a line that's already a heading. Ctrl+H on a plain paragraph now runs `setHeading({ level: 1 })` immediately and still waits for a second key. Ctrl+H on a heading changes nothing until the digit. If the second key is H (go to the Title), the line goes back to a paragraph first (`undoQuickHeading`), so the Title step never leaves a stray heading behind. 0, S and A act on the line as before.
  - **Held Ctrl:** a held Ctrl auto-repeats its own keydown, which counted as "any other key" and cancelled the sequence before the digit. Modifier keydowns are now ignored while waiting.
  - **Verified:** `NoteEditor.test.tsx` "Ctrl+H levels" (alone → H1; held Ctrl then 2 → H2, a heading left alone by Ctrl+H, then 1 → H1; Ctrl+H, Ctrl+H → Title with the line still a paragraph), plus the existing Title and Author tests.
